Publisher’s Note

This Gloss on the Pre-eminent Text of Grace, “The 
Marital Garland of Letters”, is authored by Sri Muruganar, 
a scholar-devotee whose scholarship and devotion were 
astounding in their depth and quality. This commentary 
exuding the flavour of the chaste Tamil of Sangam era is 
terse, succinct and a little complex. Instances of a sentence 
running into a full page and more are not uncommon.

Profuse are the references to the rules and nuances of 
Tamil grammar to arrive at diverse interpretations which 
bring out the various layers of meaning. The alternate 
renditions highlighted by a skilful analysis are a source of 
delight to discerning readers. All of them have been translated 
though the explanation of the intricate rules of grammar, not 
indispensable for a proper comprehension have been skirted 
except when necessary. The author himself had included 
three addenda written by him and the latest Tamil edition 
carries, besides these, a small ‘research paper’ on the Text 
and some additional details, both of which were found as 
manuscripts in the archives. All these have been translated 
in full and integrated into the body of the main Text itself 
under the respective verses.

It is fervently hoped that this translation by “Kays” has 
done full justice to the labour of love of this venerated and an 
eminent sadhak and that the devout would derive immense 
benefit by a perusal of this work.

Arunachala 
Akshara Mana Malai

Foreword
Ariyādi yidara jīvara daha-vārija guhai-yil
Aṟivāy-rami paramāt-tumaṉ Aruṇāchala Ramaṇaṉ
Arunachala Ramana, is the Paramatman, the 
Absolute Awareness, revelling as the form of Atman 
in the Bliss of Being in the cave of the Heart-Lotus 
of all beings from Hari and Brahma.
That all the sacred utterances bestowed by Bhagavan in 

the shape of various Texts are but words of Grace elicited 
by the requests of earnest devotees is a fact well known to 
those who moved closely with Bhagavan. There is a history 
behind the origin of each Text of Sri Bhagavan. Let us delve 
deep into the backdrop responsible for the emanation of 
Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai, the first and foremost 
Text of Bhagavan.

While Bhagavan was dwelling in the Virupaksha 
Cave on the Hill the devotees who were residing with and 
serving him implored Bhagavan to compose and bestow a 
hymn that they could sing while they went into the town 
for bhiksha (begging for alms). Bhagavan, who was ever 
established firmly in Transcendent Quiescence with total 



x Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

subsidence of the mind, without the least urge of ‘I’ in 
anything, responded, “Where is the need for a new hymn 
while there are a plentitude of excellent hymns of Thēvāram 
and Tiruvāchakam” and lapsed into silence. But the devotees 
were persistent and their importunations continued unabated 
with great zeal for a hymn authored by Bhagavan himself. 
But not a single thought stirred in the heart of Bhagavan.

One fine day when Bhagavan left for the 
circumambulation of the Hill, cherishing the fond hope that 
Bhagavan after all, may feel inclined to fulfil their request, 
a devotee followed him with paper and pencil. That very 
day was marked as the propitious moment for the grant of 
the devotees’ prayer by the Expansive Grace of the Divine. 
Bhagavan felt prompted to pen the hymn on Arunachala 
Siva. 

The sacred name of Arunachala, the effulgent sphurana 
or throb of which his heart was lit up with, right from the 
“non-discriminating age of childhood (aṟivaṟu siṟuvaya 
dadumudal)” provided the auspicious beginning of the 
inspired hymn. The revealed words illumined with Grace 
surged from within the tabernacle of his heart and gushed 
forth, and were all recorded by Bhagavan in the paper 
brought by the devotee. Bhagavan, for the first time, laid 
bare with an ecstasy of devotion laced with dense erotic 
flavour, that is quite out of this world, the Divine Leela of 
the wonder of Supreme Love that was enacted between him 
and Arunachala in the innermost privacy of his heart in the 
sweet solitude marked by the serene peace of the jungles on 
the slope of Arunachala. The ecstasy of Bliss that welled 
forth brought on irrepressible tears of joy that streamed 
down, drenched the paper and clouded his vision from seeing 



Foreword xi

aright. En route the circumambulatory path, he sat down at 
various places and in small measures gradually recorded the 
words that sprang from within the heart with the effulgence 
of Grace. He completed the Text of Akshara Mana Malai – a 
hymn non-pareil – compacted of 108 mantras even before 
the pradakshina was over.

Many a time when devotees desired Bhagavan to explain 
the import of certain verses that have the great and inherent 
worth of being learnt and recited, he would reply, with his 
face wreathed in a smile, “Why don’t you expound the 
content yourself? I also, just like you, have to ponder over 
and explain the meaning. If I had written after reflecting on 
something I could explain. They sprang forth somehow and 
unexpectedly.”

The grandeur of Grace that the Text of Akshara 
Mana Malai is soaked in, can well be realized and felt 
by the devotees in the light of the Truth that Arunachala 
Siva Himself sang of Himself which this historical detail 
discloses.

Sri Muruganar has many an achievement to his credit. 
Becoming an irretrievable servitor to the revered Feet of 
Bhagavan, he had the rare fortune of moving in close intimacy 
with Bhagavan. The immense blessing of worshipping the 
Flowers of his Feet with delectable verses and the unique 
privilege of requesting and getting favoured with the 
bestowal of a new Text (Uḷḷadu Nāṟpadu) which has earned 
for him gratitude from devotees the world over are all his. 
Muruganar has now become the favoured recipient of the 
munificent grace of Bhagavan and the praise and veneration 
of the devotees for having written this skilful commentary 
on Akshara Mana Malai. 



xii Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

He, anchoring his hope totally on Bhagavan’s Grace, has 
stored and strung all that he learnt and just the way he learnt 
from Bhagavan by listening to him on numerous occasions 
and has compacted them all in this commentary for Akshara 
Mana Malai with content, deep and extensive – though 
brief in form – enriched with fragrance, values and virtues. 
Natanananda, the reputed and wise scholar with deep love 
and affection for both Bhagavan and Muruganar, has well 
explained in his Preface, the exalted state of Bhagavan 
Ramana the Great and all the subtle truths and nuances of 
this Text of Grace and the rare commentary on it.

Sri Chengalvaraya Pillai, who has vowed hereditary 
allegiance to the Flowery Feet of Lord Muruga, has revealed 
with great joy, the overwhelming ecstasy he experienced 
in his heart, by an interesting study and research of his on 
the greatness of the Text of Akshara Mana Malai and the 
numerous points of excellence that this commentary bears 
within.

All the blessed devotees who decide upon Bhagavan 
Sri Ramana as their ideal bridegroom and seek enfoldment 
within the sweep of his grace, may approach him with this 
garland that smells sweet with the fragrance of grace and 
become a target of his love, great and divine.

R. Viswanathan



The All-Pervading Arunachala Siva 
As 

The Throb of ‘I’
Dēhaṅ gaḍa-nihar jaḍa-midaṟ
Kaha-meṉun tihazhvila-dāl
Nāhañ chaḍala-mil tuyi-liṉi
Diṉa-muṟu nama-diya-lāl
Kōhaṅ kara-ṉevaṇ uḷaṉuṇarn
Duḷa-ruḷak guhai-yuḷḷē
Sōham spuraṇa-varuṇa-giri 
Siva-vibu suyam-oḷirvāṉ.

– Sri Bhagavan
This body is insentient and akin to a mud pot. It 
does not shine with the light of awareness of ‘I’. In 
the bodiless awareness of our sleep, by our inherent 
nature of being the form of awareness, which is our 
daily experience, we are not this body. Who then 
is the one who rises as ‘I’? In the Heart Cave of 
those who have realised and have become “That 
Which Exists” by enquiry into our nature, Siva – 
the Transcendent Arunachala, the Form of radiant 
Light, shines as ‘I-I’, in all His Self-Effulgence.






Sri Ramana Sadguru Paramatmane Namaha

Preface

Tradition has it that Saint Māṇikkavāchakar, pointing 
to Lord Nataraja enshrined at the ‘sanctum sanctorum’ of 
The Temple (Chidambaram: The Hall of Wisdom), as the 
‘meaning’ of his monumental work Tiruvāchakam (The 
Sacred Utterances) dissolved into the Effulgence therein 
and shone as “That”. The import of this incident is worth 
being reflected upon deeply. It is certain beyond doubt that 
if Bhagavan Sri Ramana were to be asked the content of 
Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai that he had bestowed, he 
also would have looked at Arunachala in Perfect Silence and 
would have shone, indeed, as Arunachala Himself.

That the biography of Bhagavan is but the history of 
the play of Grace divine is abundantly clear. Right from 
the non-discriminating age of childhood, the ‘sphurana’ of 
Arunachala, the Lord’s own name, spontaneously throbbed 
in his heart, swallowed in entirety his body, willing soul and 
wealth, transformed him verily into Itself and made him 
abide as “That”.

Though one may detect a faint sense of duality at the 
commencement of the letter he wrote before parting from his 
kith and kin as, “I, in search of my Father, by His command 
have started,” the moment he espied Arunachala, as he 
himself proclaims, “asking as to ‘Who is the seer?’ with 
a mind turned inward when I sought within, the perceiver 



xvi Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

(ego) disappearing, I beheld the surviving Atman shining 
(kaṇḍavaṉ evaṉ eṉak karuttiṉuḷ nāḍak kaṇḍavaṉ niṉḏṟiḍa 
niṉḏṟadu kaṇḍēṉ)”, he attained the Transcendent state of 
the Self, remaining as the Self. He abided as mere “Being”, 
the nature and form of Arunachala, that allowed nothing 
other than the form of the Self to rear its head but dissolved 
everything as Itself, the grand expanse of Awareness. He 
remained in that state of Kevala Nirvikalpa Samadhi for 
quite a protracted period.

Grace and Being are one – and indivisible – indeed. Yet, 
by the experience of this two-fold nature of the Selfhood 
the Bliss of wisdom saturated with Grace with no distortion 
in the state of abidance as Being, he attained naturally the 
state of Sahaja Nirvikalpa Samadhi – a state of unbroken 
Awareness – perceiving everything with the Eye of Grace.

At this juncture emanated this Text, the first of its kind, 
out of this plenary experience in the very shape and form 
of this Experience. His mind, speech, capabilities and the 
prowess of intellect had all been already gobbled up by 
Arunachala like the darkness consumed by the rays of the 
sunlight, and he abided as mere ‘Chit’ (Awareness) – the 
state of Grace – when from within pulsated the throb, as 
“Arunachala, Arunachala”, thundered by the powerful voice 
of silence. What else could be the meaning of this sphurana 
but Arunachala, “Awareness, full and Pure?” A close 
scrutiny of his own words in some of these couplets, besides 
highlighting Bhagavan’s deftness in performing severe 
austerities and accomplishment of the siddhi of Kaivalya, 
brings to the fore certain fascinating facts. 



Preface xvii

Those couplets are:
“Entering my mind and forcefully pulling me away, 

Thou hast kept me imprisoned and inescapably secure in 
the home of Thy Heart (aham puhundu īrttuuṉ ahaguhai 
siṟaiyāy amarvittadu eṉ).” 

“Removing the dense darkness of my delusion by the 
magic power of Thy Grace, Thou hast made me the Supreme 
Form of Thee (maimayal nīttu aruḷ maiyiṉāl uṉadu uṇmai 
vasam ākkiṉai).” 

“Like a deft magician, Thou, stupefying me with 
the wonder powder of Thy Holy Grace, took away my 
jivabodham and revealed Sivabodham (poḍiyāl mayakki eṉ 
bōdhattaip paṟittu uṉ bōdhattaik kāṭṭiṉai).” 

“Exorcising me of the ghost of ego, Thou, Brahman, 
beyond being exorcised, hast possessed me with a firmer 
grip turning me into another ghost (pēyttaṉam viḍaviḍāp 
pēyāp piḍittu eṉaip pēyaṉ ākkiṉai)” and 

“What ‘tapas’ have I performed that, among many 
devotees, Thou hast singled me out as the target of Thy 
Grace (kuṟiyāy eṉaik koḷak kuṟaiyeṉ seydēṉ?)”

“Right Awareness may dawn to some extent after aeons 
of learning and worship of Siva” is an old adage. Many of 
the revered Seers, true to this dictum, after hard and long 
spells of adherence to painful and rigorous practices, ripen 
in competence by the wealth of Grace so earned. Then they, 
through self effort coupled with Guru’s grace, get instructed 
in the secret of scriptures, the intent and indicated content 
of the maha vakyas like “Aham Brahmasmi”, “soham”, and 
“Sivoham”. They follow this up further with performance of 



xviii Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

austere tapas in various ways like pipīlikam (the way of the 
ant), vihaṅgam (the way of the bird) and markkaḍa (the way 
of the monkey) and achieve union with ‘That’ and abide as 
the Content of Brahman.

But the play of Grace paints a different stroke in the 
poem of Bhagavan’s life. True knowledge, the great and rare 
fortune of Deliverance, upadesa from or the benign grace 
of a sadguru – none of these was ever even dreamt of by 
Bhagavan, much less either desired or endeavoured for. 
That “he was transformed into Arunachala”, rather than “he 
became Arunachala”, will be more in the fitness of things 
can be asserted with certitude. This very experience of 
Brahman, Pure Awareness, that came to him in a flash by the 
way of marjala nyaya (the passive kitten), is extolled by him 
in sheer wonder in the verse “Despite being ignorant of the 
meaning of Mahavakya, Tat Tvam Asi (That Thou Art), you 
have gained the very state of Brahmanhood, the state of non-
difference. So saying, do proclaim that what I have gained is 
the Truth of Reality and thus do pacify me (tattuvam teriyā 
tattaṉai uṯṯṟāy tattuvam iduveṉ)” by addressing Arunachala 
who gifted him that experience.

Thus swallowed by the mighty effulgence of the sweep 
of His Grace, he simply dissolved as “That”. But this was 
a wonderful experience totally unexpected and a grand new 
one. He, anxious to ascertain his nature and form and get 
anchored solidly in this new experience, enquired after 
himself by adopting the quest ‘Who am I’, which technique 
he later passed on as “Atma Vichara” to his disciples who 
sought and worshipped him.



Preface xix

Thus in truth, the content of this Text is 
‘Arunachalam’. That is, indeed the Unmoving Being with 
the nature of Being-Awareness-Bliss (Sat Chit Ananda). 
It is the sky of Awareness from where speech and thought 
return unable to approach. Who can divine the meaning 
of that silence, the True Knowledge? Is it not the avowal 
and testimony of the Seers of that Experience that “If they 
gain that Content, they become ‘That’ and ‘That’ Itself 
will reveal?” Hence it is but certain that only those who 
are capable of abiding as “That” are, indeed, fit for that 
experience.

Muruganar, the great, is possessed of extra-ordinary 
prowess and talent. Accomplishing by the might of the 
revealing grace of his Master, challenging deeds – not easy 
of achievement – is his forte. Praying to the Lotus feet of his 
Master by his grace, propelled, guided and blessed by the 
might of that grace, he has embarked on this great, rare and 
fruitful endeavour of penning a Commentary to this Text of 
Grace, Akshara Mana Malai. That he has crowned himself 
with tremendous success is no wonder for a person of his 
stature.

He, with the divine grace of his Master as his Eye, 
has authored this Text of Commentary and this feature 
sufficing in itself calls for no other commendation. This 
unique feature of special significance is, indeed, a great 
blessing and an honour.

This commentary was written even while Bhagavan, 
who bestowed this Text of Grace, was in his holy physical 
frame. Muruganar stored and strung together all that he had 



xx Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

ascertained from Bhagavan himself as the intent, the idea 
and the content of each single term in the original Text with 
great clarity, so that there be no disparity between the content 
of the original and that conveyed by the commentary.

Among the celebrated commentaries of the Texts of 
Grace like the Prasthana Traya (the basic Three, namely, 
the Brahma Sutras, the Upanishads and the Bhagavad 
Gita) at many places doubts regarding the identity of the 
import intended by the author and the one conveyed by the 
commentary often assail and agitate the minds of the readers.

That this Text of Commentary steers clear of such 
discrepancies totally eschewing them, is yet another credit, 
sui generis, to this Text.

This Text, taken so far as yet another addition in the 
form of Text of litany to the existing rich treasure of mystic 
and Bhakti literature, as a special mode of worship of the 
Lord in the form of Arunachala, now shines as a Lakshana 
Grantham (a standard, path-breaking Text), thanks to this 
outstanding commentary by this scholar. This labour of love 
has further secured a fond place for Muruganar in the hearts 
of all devotees and it is no exaggeration to say has made him 
abide, enriched in boundless grace, with his Master. It is but 
certain that the learned ones will welcome this commentary 
with eager anticipation.

Hail the Rosy Feet of Sadguru Sri Arunachala Ramana 
Bhagavan.

Sri Ramana Nagar
20.2.1952 Natananandan



Commendation

This Text, Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai, is a 
wedding garland strung with fragrant flowers of verses in 
Tamil Tongue that Bhagavan Sri Ramana Maharshi adorned 
Lord Arunachala with. That the people of the world at 
large may gain Release by repeated recitation of this Text 
underlies the largesse of this gift. 

Now let us peruse the rationale behind the facts the 
following pertinent queries raise in seriatim:

1.	 Why did Bhagavan choose Lord Arunachala Siva as 
the deity to be hymned in this litany?

2.	 What lies behind the reason to compose in the order 
of the alphabets of the Tamil language?

3.	 What is the sanctity of the number 108?
4.	 Why, in the Tamil Tongue?
5.	 Even then, why as a garland of verses in Tamil?
6.	 The reason to string it as a wedding garland and
7.	 The reason behind the choice of short, pithy couplets.
(1) The pre-eminence given to Lord Arunachala by the 

Seers, right from the hoary past is quite suggestive and makes 
the choice self-evident and automatic. Saint Arunagirinathar 
extols the sublime nature of Lord Arunachala Siva thus:

Yāṉ-eṉadu eṉac-caruvum īṉa samayattu-evarum
Yārum uṇardaṟku ariya nērmaik-kāraṉ*

* Kandhar Aṉubhūti, 4-Tiruvēḷaikkāraṉ Vaguppu.



xxii Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

The One who remains the content of Subtle Truth 
beyond the ken of those of alien religions who, 
with the overweening pride of “I and mine” forever 
contest and enter into wordy combats.
Arunachalam is the consecrated place where the sovereign 

Lord deflated the vaunting pride of the ego of the duo (Brahma 
and Hari) and rained grace on the twain by bestowing jnana 
(knowledge) on them. Further it is the sacred spot that grants 
Release the very instant it is thought of. 

This truth is highlighted in a verse in Kanda Puranam:
Eṉ-ṉihar evarum il-eṉḏṟu iruvarum ihalum ellai 
Aṉṉavar naḍuvu tōṉḏṟi aḍi-muḍi teriyādu āhi 
Uṉṉiṉar taṅgaṭku ellām ollaiyiṉ mukti nalhi 
Taṉ-nihar iṉḏṟi niṉḏṟa tazhal-perum Shailam*
The mighty Hill of fiery effulgence that straddled 
aloft with base and crown unknown between the 
two who vying with each other contended “there 
is none equal to me,” and bestows Deliverance 
expeditiously on all who merely think of It.
Bhagavan sang of Siva who resides as the presiding 

deity here so that the people of the world remembering this 
great and holy place, may obtain the fruit of Deliverance, rid 
of ‘I’ and ‘mine’.

(2) On the choice of the alphabets of Tamil language: 
The prime need of the devotees desirous of the fruit 

of Deliverance is the ceaseless rememberance of the Lord, 
coupled with repeated singing of His praise and worship. This 
* Srī Kacchiyappa Sivācchāriyār, Kanda Purāṇam, 1-Uṟpatti 
Kāṇḍam, 23-Vazhinaḍaip Paḍalam, v. 11.



Commendation xxiii

Text, based on the order of the alphabets lends itself for its easy 
commitment to memory and facilitates frequent recitations.

3) What is the sanctity of the number one hundred and 
eight? 

Saint Jnana Sambandar bespoke, “If mantras as potent 
medicines are required here they are: These mantras are 
verily those medicines. Further all other grievous miseries 
will be undone by this mantra. O, Good heart! Meditate on 
His name.” This Text contains within, repeated rendition of 
this holy chant of the five-lettered mantra – A-ru-na-cha-la – 
as many as 108 times, which, according to the hoary tradition, 
is an ideal, nay, a number mandatory, for japa.

4) Why in the language of Tamil? 
The language dearest to the heart of Lord Siva is Tamil. 

The following sacred pronouncement by Siva Himself, 
chronicled in Periapuranam is testimony enough – if one is 
required – of this truth.

“‘Love-laden verses are verily worship with Flowers. 
Sing in Tamil’, so said Lord Siva who spoke the Vedas four.”

5) Why a garland of Tamil verses? 
Garlands strung of syllables of praise and sung with a 

heart and tongue of piety, indeed, befit the Lord most.
The devout can deck and decorate the Lord depending 

on the degree of desire, devotion, and economic status, either 
with garlands of flowers or of gold. Were it to be asked what 
delights the Lord most, it is nothing but the garland of words 
and verses in Tamil. Saint Appar exclaims in addressing his 
heart with the import, “Oh heart, Let thy Garland of verses 
in Tamil be thy offering to the Lord, worship him and be 
freed.” The verse is as follows:



xxiv Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

Pūm-paḍi mak-kalam poṟpaḍi mak-kalam eṉḏṟu-
ivaṯṯṟāl

Ām-paḍi mak-kalam āhilum ārūr iṉidu-amarndār
Tām-paḍi mak-kalam vēṇḍuvarēl Tamizh mālai-gaḷāl
Nām-paḍi mak-kalam seydu tozhu-dui maḍa neñjamē.*
O foolish mind! Though gifts of gold and fragrant 
flowers be deemed as worthy offerings, If Lord Siva 
residing in all grace at Thiruvarur desired offerings 
wreathe for Him gifts of garlands of dulcet verses in 
Tamil and be delivered.
Bhagavan, fully conscious of this truth, and knowing 

with certitude that only a garland woven with verses of 
Tamil delights the Lord most, threaded this garland.

6) Why a wedding garland? 
We shall answer that. Is not wedding, an auspicious 

event, a landmark that unifies two souls? The best course for 
a jivatman, aspiring to become one with the Paramatman, 
is to adorn the Lord with the marital garland to commingle 
with Him. All the learned elders who have known this truth 
have longed for the wedding garlands that have been worn 
by the Lord.

Māl-koṇḍa pēdaikku-uṉ maṇa-nāṟum
Mār-taṅgu tārait-tandu aruḷvāyē†
Would you not graciously give this maiden 
passionately in love with Thee, the garland that 
sways on Thy Fragrant chest?

* Appar Thēvāram, 6th Tirumuṟai, 102-Tiruvārūr, v. 3.	
† Aruṇagirināthar, Tiruppugazh, v. 1296.



Commendation xxv

Pēdai koṇḍa koḍi-dāṉa tuṉba mayal-tīra
Kuḷir-mālai yiṉ-kaṇ aṇi-mālai tandu
Kuṟai-tīra vandu kuṟuhāyō*
Won’t you come in the cool hours of the evening 
and bestow Thy garland of kadamba flowers on me 
betokening Thy love for me, the poor one, who, like 
Valli is tormented by pangs of love for Thee and 
thus relieve me of my longing for Thee?
So does Saint Arunagirinathar beseech for the same as 

the preceding two verses proclaim. Well, if it is a wedding 
garland, yet another one for exchange is required. How is 
that possible? Verse 108 says “Granting me Thy Garland of 
Grace, Pray graciously wear my bridal garland of Letters.” 
For a devotee-servitor, it is easy to adorn the Lord with a 
garland. But will the Lord ever bestow on a devotee the one 
that He has worn? Will it ever happen? Is it that easy? In that 
state where the devotees bereft of the pride of ‘I’ and ‘mine’ 
long for Him the Lord simply becomes totally self-effacing 
and of easy access to them and bestows on the seekers what 
they seek. Valli, the consort of Lord Subrahmanya who was 
well aware of this fact wreathed herself with victory and 
obtained the Lord. She, seeking Him alone “forgot herself, 
lost her name, lost herself and placed her head at the Feet of 
the Lord.” So much so Lord Subrahmanya, abandoning His 
abode at Thanikai Himself ran, post-haste, to Vallimalai to 
bless her with His hand and union. This is hailed as Valli’s 
Path of True Love. Arunagirinathar has hailed the Lord as:

* Aruṇagirināthar, Tiruppugazh, v. 101.



xxvi Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

“Oh Lord! Thou repairing where the tribal belle, fawn 
like was and desired her (kuṟamāṉai aṇmi virumbiya 
Perumāḷē)” and 

“The one who hastened to Kurichi and attempted to wed 
her (muṉṉam kuṟicchiyil ceṉḏṟu kalyāṇam muyaṉḏṟavaṉē)” 
and further 

“He who embraced the damsel of the hills to reciprocate 
her Love (kuṟamāduḍaṉ māl kaḍaṉām eṉavē aṇaimārbhā).”

Kumara Gurupara affirms:
Tāṉ-eṉḏṟavar-muṉ oḷittu-ōḍit taṉṉai izhan-davar 
Muṉ, yāṉ eṉḏṟu ceṉḏṟi-ḍum Kāsip-pirāṉ*
The Lord of Kasi who conceals Himself out of sight 
and runs away from those who assert themselves as 
‘I’ but appears as ‘I am’ before those who lose the 
‘self’ to Him.
Maṇivāchakar too expresses it as

Tēḍi, nī āṇḍāy; Siva-purattu Arasē!†
O Sovereign of Sivapuram!
Of Your Own volition,
You chose and ruled me.
The following is offered in a lighter vein. If He volunteers 

to go over and offer a marital garland to those who have 
offered themselves up to Him, how is it possible? He has the 
bride, Devi Parvathy, on His side and on His matted locks is 
ensconced another bride, Ganga. If He were to take out the 
garland from His chest, Devi Parvathy would definitely espy 
it. If the garland on the crown were to be offered, Ganges in 

* Kāsikkalambhakam, v. 682.
† Māṇikkavāchakar, Tiruvāchakam, 28-Vāzhāp Pattu, v. 3.



Commendation xxvii

the tresses would perceive it. From where and how would 
He give His garland of golden laburnum to the lady?

Maṅgai kāṇak koḍār maṇa-mālaiyai
Gaṅgai kāṇak koḍār muḍik-kaṇṇiyai
Naṅgai mīr-iḍai marudar-in naṅgaikkē
Eṅgu vāṅgik koḍut-tār idazhiyē.*
The Lord in wedlock giveth not the garland fragrant
From the chest seen by the Maiden of the Mount
He giveth not the chaplet from the crown
Seen by Ganga, Oh ye young maidens!
Wherefore did the Lord of Idaimarudur
Koṉḏṟai flowers to give this damsel procure?
So does Saint Appar exclaim in wonder in a Psalm of 

Thēvāram.
“If He, who is the Foremost wills it can anything remain 

undone?” What is there that is impossible for the Lord? is 
the answer to that question. Is not the Lord, the valiant hero 
who hastens to the place where His taintless devotees reside, 
seeks them out, enacts His Divine Leela (sports), takes His 
Abode there and stays on?

7. Why choose the short form of Tamil prosody?
Since easy commitment to memory is facilitated in a 

great measure by short and brief versification, Bhagavan 
chose this pithy couplet form. Just as Kapila, the celebrated 
Poet of Sangam era pays encomiums to Tirukkuṟaḷ (in the 
form of couplet) as “though brief like a minuscule seed, when 
read, reveals truths tall as palmyra tree,” it can legitimately 
be stated that these verses, due to the prowess of constant 
abidance in gracious jnana by Bhagavan, hold within 

* Appar Thēvāram, 5th Tirumuṟai, 015-Tiruviḍaimarudūr, v. 6.



xxviii Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

mighty contents and profound truths. This rare, lengthy and 
exhaustive commentary bears testimony to this truth. The 
author of this commentary, with his extensive scholarship, 
unbounded surge of devotion and by the divine gift and 
grace of his great Master, has delved deep and explained 
at length the subtle contents not easily discernable that 
each couplet encapsulates. Verse 62 is an instance in point: 
“Thou, the omniscient One, could have easily claimed me 
by giving Thyself unto me. But Thou did not. Thou wert 
blind to the very existence of me. Hence Thou art but a blind 
one as far as I am concerned” is the import that suggests 
itself at a cursory reading. But the wonderful way in which 
the rare and various layers of meaning that he has divined 
from the verse and laid bare in his commentary compels 
the readers to go over it again and again. Perceiving and 
penning an explanatory commentary for these verses based 
on what scholars in Sanskrit speak as ‘kākuḥ’ (H$mHw$:) namely, 
the differences of intonations implying varied versions of 
the content, is, indeed, insightful and precious. In the same 
way for verse 54 the excellent reading he has provided 
namely, “I, bereft of I and mine, bespeaking of attachment 
and concern, expressive of modesty and a sense of shame, 
have sought Thee and Thou, condescending to unite with 
me as one standeth motionless (in the words of Sundarar 
“Thou kept Thyself in the heart of mine” which is also 
reminiscent of what Arunagirinathar says as “bestow on me 
that compassion that rids me of the sense of shame”) brings to 
the fore his knowledge and depth of subtle truths. Numerous 
are such instances where rare insights are discernable to the 
delight of the readers. 



Commendation xxix

Alive to the nature of this Text being a “Text of recitation” 
by devotees of both the genders, the interpretation intuited 
by the author for the 20th verse beginning with “kūrvāḷ” is 
quite delectable indeed.

Uṇṇu-madu rak-kaṉiyōḍu aṟusuvai-pōl oṉḏṟi-yeṉaip
Paṇ-ṇoḍu pāṭṭup-pōl parindu-āṇḍa Vēṅkaḍaṉ-pāl
Uṇṇuṇuhi yucā-vuyirōḍu uṇarvum oḍuṅgoḍuṅgak
Kaṇṇoḍu-kaṇ ṇōkkiyē.
As sweetness in fruits, as music in melody did He 
with me unite, claiming me with concern making me 
go within, with breath absorbed, thought subsiding 
and subsiding, meeting eyes with eyes, let us play 
the eye-meeting game.
Can anyone, other than Muruganar, the author of 

Ramana Sannidhi Murai, who played the ‘eye-meeting 
game’ explain the subtle thoughts of Bhagavan?

Maṇa-mālai pāḍi maṇa-mālai nūlukku
Guṇa-māy amaitta kuṟi-sēr urai-pāḍik
Kaṇa-mām viṉai-tīr gati-kāṭṭum nūl-ihdē
Tiṇa-mām eṉap-pāḍit teḷḷēṇam koṭṭāmō
Let us acclaim with clear conviction 
That singing the Marital Garland, 
Singing the commentary to that Text 
Will wipe the crowd of deeds past 
And reveal the way Home. So saying 
Let us sing and beat Tellenam.
Hence let us with the exuberance of joy play the 

Tambuourine and dance in delight and sing



Hail Akshara Mana Malai
Hail the author Sri Bhagavan
Hail this Commentary and
Hail the author of it.
And hailing so
Let us praise the divine Grace of the Lord of Tiruttani.

31-10-1951 V.S. Chengalvaraya  Pillai



Verses Commending the Commentary

By ‘Thanigaimani’, Rao Bahadur  
V.S. Chengalvaraya Pillai, M.A.,

(Retired Personal Assistant to the  
Inspector General of Registration)

1.	 Tavar-palar tozhu-dēttum taṉmaiya Ramaṇēsar
	 Avar-ulahu uyya aruḷ Akshara Maṇa Mālaikku
	 Ivar-aladu urai-vallār evar-uḷar eṉu-māṟu
	 Vivaraṇa māṉa-vurai viḷak-kam aḷit-taṉarē.

2.	 Parugu vaṉ-ṉeṉum pat-tiyōḍu aṇṇalār
	 Aruhu jnāṉa amudu upadēsaṅgaḷ
	 Urugi niṉḏṟu-aṅgu uḷat-tiṉil paṯ-ṯṟiya
	 Murugaṉār eṉum mūdaṟi vāḷarē.

1. 	Lord Ramana, ever the Self, hailed by men of Penance
	 Bestowed this Marital Garland for People to gain 

Release.
	 And Muruganar, by this detailed gloss set the world astir
	 To wonder who else other than him can offer such an

exposition.



2. 	The learned Muruganar of ripe wisdom
	 And great devotion desiring to drink up
	 All the nectarine jnana upadesa of
	 Bhagavan stood rooted with a melting
	 Heart, absorbing them all within.



Preamble

Akshara Mana Malai, The Marital Garland of Letters 
is but the delectable outcome of the mystic experience of 
‘Parabhakti’. This Supreme Love welled up unbounded in 
all its sharp exceedingly acute and burning intensity towards 
the bridegroom Arunachala in the heart of Bhagavan wherein, 
sunk deep, he had subsided with a highly subtle mind 
with profound fortitude. This Text of fragrant verses – the 
foremost of Adhyatmic Texts bestowed by Bhagavan – with 
its expansive sweep, fathomless depth and rare grandeur is 
not easy of discernment even for scholars of great erudition. 
Only the supremely devout who have revelled in the utmost 
depths of the waters of devotion gaining access to it through 
its manifold inlets, blessed by the profuse Grace of the 
Master, can clearly comprehend the variegated layers and 
shades of the import. I, totally helpless to turn down the 
repeated and relentless persuasions of a few devout, dear to 
my heart, to pen a commentary for this Text have embarked 
on this endeavour though fully aware more than anyone else 
of my want of intellectual prowess that is grossly inadequate 
to meet the task on hand. Yet pegging all my hopes entirely 
on the guidance of the Grace Divine have I ventured. May 
the men of letters with their proverbial humility forbear this 
impudence of mine who like a fool have rushed in where the 
more wise would fear to tread. 



xxxiv Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

Kuṯ-ṯṟam ahaṯ-ṯṟik guṇam-koṇḍu pārāṭ-ṭal
Kaṯ-ṯṟu-aṟinda māndar kaḍaṉ.
It is indeed the duty of the learned to appreciate the 
merits and be blind to the faults.

Author of the Commentary



Dedication
Avaṉ ava-ḷāhi avaṉai aṇainda
Sivamaṇa mālai-yait tērum – kaviṉ-uraisaṟ
Bōdha sorūpap puraṇa Ramaṇaguru
Pāda samarp-paṇam pār.

This Marital Garland exuding the fragrance of 
Sivam is one with which He (Arunachala) becoming 
She (the Bride Ramana) got united with Him. This 
elegant commentary that explores and expounds this 
Garland is dedicated to the Feet of Bhagavan Guru 
Ramana who is the pulsating form of Sivajnana, 
Being-Awareness – the Self.

This is offered by Muhavai Kanna Murugan 
to the Feet twain of Guru Arunachala Ramana 

to commemorate the  
Ashtabandhana Maha Kumbhabhishekam 

of the Arunachaleswara Temple

4-6-1944, the 22nd day of the month Vaikasi in 
the year Dharana






Prologue

Bestowed by Sri Muruganar

Taruṇā ruṇa-maṇi kiraṇā vali-nihar
	 taruma kṣhara-maṇa magizh-mālai
Teruṇā ḍiya-tiru vaḍiyār teru-maral
	 teḷiyap para-vudal poru-ḷāha
Karuṇā kara-muṉi Ramaṇā riya-ṉuva
	 kaiyiṉāṟ coliyadu gati-yāha
Aruṇā chala-meṉa ahamē yaṟi-voḍum
	 āzhvār Siva-ṉula hāḷvārē.

Substance: 
Akshara Mana Malai, the Marital Garland of Letters, 

an outpour of ecstatic Bliss, was sung by the noble 
Sage, Preceptor Sri Ramana, out of his infinite love for 
Arunachala, to dispel the mental delusion (the one and 
only obstacle) of the devotees who seek True Awareness. 
Those devotees who take it as their sole refuge to praise 
and pray Lord Arunachala and thinking of Him, sink and 
subside in the stillness of their hearts with awareness, will 
get to rule Sivalokha by the grace of Siva.
Commentary:

taruṇā ruṇamaṇi kiraṇā valinihar
taruma ksharamaṇa magizhmālai



xxxviii Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

The fragrant garland strung with letters of magizha 
flowers that are luminous like the beam of the rays of the 
sun rising in the early morn.

tarunarunan: the rising sun
mani: (Stands for the expanse of light). The un-bored 

whole gem of the firmament (the Sun)
kiraṇā vali: rows of rays
nihar tarum: that sheds light.
The ordinary meaning of the term ‘nihar’ in Tamil, 

namely equal, may not be of any special significance here 
where it is used in the sense of ‘lustrous’, supported by 
this usage in Manimekalai (one of the five celebrated epic 
poems in Tamil literature) in the line “nihar malar nīyē 
koṇarvāy” (the luminous flower, you yourself fetch). Letters 
are compared to the rays of the sun and the garland to the 
rows of such rays.

Akṣharamaṇa magizh mālai: The meaning of this 
phrase is twofold. A reference to the flowers of letters that 
are strung into a garland is one. The exchange of marital 
garlands between the bride and the bridegroom that provides 
an exuberance of joy and thrill in the heart – as a symbol of 
the ritual of the wedding ceremony – is the other.*

The Bliss and joy referred to here denote the Bliss 
Supreme arising out of having become one with Siva, by 
commingling with Him with non-difference.

* This meaning is explicitly announced by Bhagavan in the last 
verse “mālai aḷittu Aruṇāchala Ramaṇa eṉ mālai aṇindu aruḷ 
Aruṇāchalā (Bestow Thy garland on me and wear this one strung 
by me).”



Prologue xxxix

teruḷ: True Awareness. Those who seek True Awareness 
(teruḷ) are indeed the ones enriched with the opulence of 
grace and are the fortunate ones. 

nāḍal: (seek) is to yearn or long for, intensely.
terumaraḷ: Connotes the mental delusion that conceals 

the Absolute Truth – The Substratum – from being known as 
It is in Reality from which arise the crowds of the transient 
names and forms of the phantasmagoria of the world and 
body like the sleigh of the hand of the conjuror and the 
consequent attachments – internal and external – that arise 
in them as “I and mine.”

Paravudal: is to hail and hymn; to worship 
poruḷ: fruit or the gain.
karuṇākaraṉ: One who is the abode of compassion 

abiding as the sea of Compassion.
muṉi: One who is given to the strict discipline of 

continuous or ceaseless reflection.
āriyaṉ: Worthy of being worshipped by sadhus and the 

pure hearted. 
uvahaiyiṉāl coliyadu: What was spoken as the 

outpouring of the overwhelming Bliss, that sprang, swelled 
and brimmed over in his heart by the Grace of Arunachala.

The sentence should be formed in such a way as to 
read “What Ramana Āriyan bespoke out of joy, the marital 
garland of fragrant letters of Bliss.”

That garland is the refuge for the intense seekers to sink 
in the heart with the awareness of Arunachala, as the form of 
meditation. Refuge or ‘gati’ means the pointer or ‘the guide 
on the path’. None can be rid of the illusion in the heart and 
attain the True Awareness unless one worships in the heart 



xl Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

Arunachala, the Flaming effulgence of Awareness, who, by 
His very nature, dispels in toto the darkness of nescience that 
envelops one’s heart. Hence the meditation on Arunachala 
is indispensable for seekers of True Awareness. That indeed, 
is Arunachalam by the vision of which the “ruṇam”, the 
bondage of karma caused by the darkness of nescience that 
obstructs the true vision, is rent asunder. So does Aruṇāchala 
Purāṇa bespeak of Arunachala.

Ellā ulahum tagai-yav viyalāl
Pollā viṉaigaḷ ruṇamām puhalīdu
Illā dadu-vām edukaṇ ṇuṟalāl
Ellām Aruṇā chala-mām iduvē*

All the perceivable worlds (however great) are 
but shackles, by their binding nature and by their 
inherent quality of causing attachments. All actions 
are the seeds of bondage resulting in the misery of 
endless cycles of births and deaths. A mere sight 
of Arunachala, the effulgent Hill, shining in all 
splendour, will render all actions fruitless.
Bhagavan further expounds the content of the term 

‘Arunachala’, as the dense mass of Being-Awareness-Bliss, 
union of jiva and para, and the form of golden Effulgence.

Sattiya chit-sukham aṉḏṟip para-vuyir chārayik-kam
Artta-vat tatva masi-aruṇap poruḷām achalattu
Arttaṅ gaṉa-madu āhum-chev vāḍaka āroḷi-yām
Mukti niṉaikka aruḷ-Aruṇā chala muṉṉi-ḍavē†

* Sri Bhagavan, Aruṇāchala Māhātmyam, v. 4.
† Sri Bhagavan, Aruṇāchala Navamaṇimālai, v.2.



Prologue xli

“When the content of the sound ‘Arunachala’, – the 
form of Light resplendent, like glowing red gold, 
bestowing mukti the moment thought of – is deeply 
enquired into, it stands revealed that ‘achala’ weighs 
with import, exalted and perfect, besides being Sat 
Chit Ananda, the union of jiva and para. It is also 
the import of the Vedic dictum (The Mahavakya) 
Tat Tvam Asi (That Thou Art).” Aham – heart; the 
Source of the mind. Aruṇāchalamē with emphasis 
at the end (chalamē) provides the meaning that 
Arunachala is, indeed, the heart.

Aṉiya bhāvat-tiṉ ava-ṉaham āhum 
Aṉa-ṉiya bhāvamē undī-paṟa
Aṉait-tiṉum utta-mam undī-paṟa*
Better than viewing Him
As other (than onself)
Is to hold Him as the ‘I’ within.

What is meant by this is total surrender, total self loss – 
the renouncing of ‘I’ and ‘mine’ that are ‘other than’ 
Arunachala – the Pure Form of Atman. In this state what 
inherently shines by itself with effulgence in the Heart is the 
experience of Pure Self or Brahmanubhava.

Yāṉē eṉṉai aṟiya kilādē,
Yāṉē eṉ-taṉadē eṉḏṟu irundēṉ;
Yāṉē nī-eṉ uḏaimai-yum nīyē;
Vāṉē ēttum-em vāṉa-var ēṟē!†	

* Sri Bhagavan, Upadēsa Undiyār, v. 8.
† Nammāzhwar, Tiruvāymozhi, Iraṇḍām Pattu, v. 9.



xlii Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

Ignorant was I of me as I
Remained all along as I and mine
Now I know I indeed am Thou
My possessions are but Thou
Hailed by all the celestials, Thou
Art indeed the Chief of Devas.

This psalm from Tiruvāymozhi revealing the experience 
of Nammāzhwar is to be known as the grammar and idiom 
of that state. “To sink within with awareness (aṟivōḍum 
āzhdal)” is the state of Turiya, the experience of being sunk 
deep in the Self as the form of Pure Awareness. This is the 
experience of perfect identity of the Self and Arunachala 
Brahman, who is the Heart, of not only the Self but the entire 
Universe – as the form of Awareness bereft of the obstacle of 
the assertion of the ego-self as ‘I’, the jiva, – a mere form of 
delusion. This experience of being the Self is gained, guided 
by Atma vichara which, by the Grace of the Lord, the Ruler, 
arises in the mind that is perfectly matured and thereby 
renders naught the primary defilement of veiling. Atma 
Vichara releases one from sleep and wakefulness,* from 

* Sleep and subsidence (laya) conceal the Truth of the Self, 
the form of Infinite Intelligence from shining as It is. Jagrat 
or wakefulness is the distortion vikshepa (of the Reality) for it 
projects and spreads before the jiva, the chidabhasa (the reflected 
awareness), like the moving pictures projected on the screen in 
a cinema show, fabricated unrealities involving the differences 
of the triad and make them appear real. Hence sleep and 
wakefulness are an obstacle to the experience of and abidance in 
the Self-Knowledge, changeless, beyond name and form. Though 
the mind subsides in the heart equally in sleep and laya, in the 
former, it subsides in the darkness of nescience and in the latter, 



Prologue xliii

subsidence (laya) and misperception rendering the primary 
nescience also extinct. It plunges one into a state which lies 
sharp, like the pointed edge of a sword, in between those 
two states.

They get to rule the world of Siva (Sivaṉulahu āḷvārē): 
The eternal state of Kaivalya – the state beyond name and 
form, will be attained by them.

in light. There is no difference between these two states since 
they conceal the Self, the Pure Awareness, the Transcendent 
Truth of oneself from being apprehended as It is. Though the Self 
shines forever, in the empirical level It never shines as It really 
is, as the unbroken, non-dual, Pure, Awareness-Being as ‘I’ in 
isolation but is always alloyed with the expansive ramification 
of illusory mental projections and appears ever intertwined 
as ‘I’ and ‘this’, in the dual plane. The awareness that appears 
then is reflected awareness and the misconceived knowledge is 
simply reflected jnana. The awareness of jagrat (wakefulness) is 
awareness of illusion fabricated by maya. That the learned seers 
are ever emphatic in their instructions to the seekers that by the 
sheer strength of ceaseless practice, the mind must be inhered in 
the gap between the two states of sleep and jagrat is due to this 
Truth. Verse 39 of Devi Kalottaram too reaffirms this truth:
uṟakkatti ṉiṉḏṟum uṇarttiṭuga cittam uṟiṉ calaṉam piṉṉaroḍukkāy
uṟakkam alaiveṉṉum ivviraṇḍum aṯṯṟa nilaiuṯṯṟāl nilai idaṉil 
niccalamā nil

If the mind becomes drowsy, awaken it
If it wanders retrieve it.
When you reach the state where there is
Neither drowsiness nor movement, remain there.






The Text – It’s Grandeur
Maṇavaṉ kāṇ-maṇa maṅgala mālai-sēr
Kaṇavaṉ kāṇ-padi ṉeṇkaṇaṅ kai-tozhum
Viṇavaṉ kāṇ-viṇ aḷaviḍa maṇ-misai
Yaṇavaṉ kāṇ-aruḷ Aṇṇā Malaiyaṉē.

Behold the Bridgegroom, bedecked with the 
wedding garland blessed

Behold the Spouse Divine, hailed by eighteen hosts 
of ghouls

Behold Him scaling the sky spanning Heaven and 
Earth

Behold Grace Personified, Annamalai on earth.
This Marital Garland is sung by Bhagavan Ramana 

Maharshi with jiva – an awakened soul – as the bride and 
Lord Siva as the bridegroom, in the path of Bhakti with 
Supreme Love ripened, and at its zenith. Filled with the 
fragrance of bridal bliss this garland is strung in mysticism 
and intoned in aesthetic terms, some just hinted and 
others with telling candour. Though comparable only to 
“Chiṯṯṟambalak Kōvaiyār” of Maṇivāchakar in general, 
this is far clearer and of easier comprehension, capable 
of bestowing bliss, both here and hereafter, on devotees 
according to their maturity. This Text has the power to 
deliver those who read it with melting love and brimming 
over with tears of piety to paths, virtuous and auspicious. 



xlvi Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

Āṉanda veḷ-ḷattu azhundum-ōr āruyir īruruk-koṇḍu
Āṉanda veḷ-ḷat tiḍai-tiḷaittāl okkum ambalam-sēr
Āṉanda veḷ-ḷattu aṟai-kazha lōṉ-aruḷ peṯṯṟavariṉ
Āṉanda veḷ-ḷam-vaṯṯ ṟādu-muṯṯ ṟādu-iv vaṇi-nalamē.*

The nurse speaks:
Felicity of conjugal bliss is such
That a single precious life
Immersed in floods of bliss
Courses through frames twain
To sink in the sea of bliss
Such is the bliss of those on
Whose crown rest the Feet
Of the Lord of Ambalam
That knows no less no surfeit.

* Māṇikkavāchakar, Chiṯṯṟambalak Kōvaiyār, 19-Maṇam 
Chiṟappuraittal, v. 9.



The Fruit of Reciting This Text

This Text, Akshara Mana Malai, is what Arunachala, 
the conflagration of the great effulgence of True wisdom, 
donning another form, sang of Himself. This dispels the 
dense darkness of nescience in the hearts, reveals the Truth 
of Turiya – Pure Being Awareness – and graciously blesses 
with the swelling up of the Bliss of the experience of the 
Self beyond name and form, in the hearts of devotees who 
recite it always with melting devotion, love and wonder.






May the Feet of Guru Arunachala Ramana guide us!

Arunachala 
Akshara Mana Malai

Explanation – The Title of the Text

Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai is a fragrant garland 
exuding the auspicious and divine aroma with letters as 
flowers – an adornment – befitting Lord Arunachala. Further 
it is one that conjoins the devotees who sing this Text, fully 
comprehending its impact, in wedlock with Arunachala, the 
Eternal Being. (The Akshara. ‘Content of Akshara’ means 
the imperishable content; The True Being).

A verse, midway through the Text pleads “Arunachala, 
rid my mind of all worldly attachments, make me attached to 
the fragrance of Brahman, the objectless Awareness, and by 
Thy Grace, unite me non-dually in wedlock with Brahman 
(bhūmaṇa māmaṉam pūraṇa maṇaṅkoḷap pūraṇa maṉam 
aruḷ Aruṇāchalā).” This verse lends credence to the latter 
meaning set forth, as the above verse quoted seems to hint 
at Bhagavan’s concurrence with the latter import.

Kāppu (Invocatory Verse)
Aruṇāchala varaṟ-kēṯṯṟa Aksharamaṇa mālai-sāṯṯṟak
Karuṇā-kara Gaṇa-patiyē kara-maruḷik kāp-pāyē



2 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

Substance:
Oh Ganesa! The Ocean of Compassion! Extend Thy 

helping hand and protect me so that, I can offer and adorn 
my chosen Lord Arunachala, my beloved, the best among 
all bridegrooms, with a bridal garland, befitting Him, 
made of letters.
Commentary:

The word “to adorn (chāttal)” on the strength of poetic 
liberty has been corrupted to form ‘chāṯṟal’ so as to render 
it a medium for the play of a pun, meaning both “to adorn” 
and “to compose a Text”.

“Aruṇāchala varaṟku” lends itself for two imports. 
Interpreted as “Arunachala Hara” it would mean 
“Arunachala Siva” while as “Arunachala Varan” it would 
stand for “the bridegroom, Arunachala, or the Foremost 
Lord.” Similarly “the ocean of Compassion (Karunakara)” 
also denotes “the hand of Grace”. Further “hand (karam)” 
would indicate “a ray” and also “Awareness”. “Hara” an 
epithet of Siva in Sanskrit, a causal name, is derived from 
His act of destroying the distress of His devotees* (Hara – 
the destroyer of distress). Saint Appar, hails this trait of Hara 
as follows: “The Lord who bestows grace on His followers 
who are followed close at heels by many an affliction 
(toḍarum toṇḍarai thukkam toḍarndu vandu aḍarumpōdhu 
araṉāy aruḷ seybavar)†”.

*	 The Tamil words “piḍāraṉ” and “piḍāri” etymologically owe 
their origin to the Sanskrit words “Pīḍāharan and Pīḍāhari” – 
names for Lord Siva and Devi Parvathy meaning “one who 
destroys the afflictions.”
†	 Appar Thēvāram, 5th Tirumuṟai, Tiruppālaittuṟai, v. 8.



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 3

It is an accepted time-honoured tradition to invoke Lord 
Ganesa before the commencement of any work to obviate 
the obstacles in the way of the accomplishment of any work.

Here, invocation to Him, apart from the traditional 
adherence to this custom has an added significance in 
particular as an address to one who extends a helping hand 
in composing this Text that unites in wedlock a devotee with 
Siva, His Father, just as He facilitated the wedding of Sri 
Valli with Lord Skanda, His brother.
Additional information from the Appendix (AIFA):

“That I may compose this Text – The Marital Garland 
of Letters – as a hymn to Siva, Arunachala, O Ganesa, the 
ocean of Grace, stretch forth Thy supporting hand and protect 
me.” Here Arunachala sreshta (vara) also means “the best 
and foremost”. The unique distinction and excellence that 
Arunachala enjoys among all other mountains is due to the 
fact that while the others house the Lord in them as different 
and apart from them in an Immanent Form, Arunachala Hill 
on the other hand, remains as the very Frame and Form of 
the Lord.

Lord Ganapathy is one who has compassion itself as His 
hand.

The Text
Arunachala Siva Arunachala Siva 

		  Arunachala Siva Arunachala!
Arunachala Siva Arunachala Siva 

		  Arunachala Siva Arunachala!!
This garland of verses must be chanted every single 

day enshrining Siva in the heart with a mind resolute, turned 



4 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

inward and single pointed in ceaseless contemplation of 
‘That-I am’; Siva, the content of ‘That’ am I, the Self.

1.	 Aruṇā chala-meṉa ahamē niṉaip-pavar
		  Ahat-taivē raṟup-pāi Aruṇāchalā

Substance:
Arunachala! Thou dost uproot the ego of those who 

in their hearts regard Thee ever with non-difference with 
their ‘Self’ as “Arunachala is verily I”.
Commentary: 

What is meant by Arunachala “uprooting the ego of 
the devotees” is as follows. When devotees through the 
dhyana delineated above, desire Him, the Divine will, in 
response, removes the ego that remains as the sole obstacle 
to the ultimate state of their experiencing Arunachala Siva 
or Annamalai, who is the very form of Self – the Heart. 
This experience is the essence of what is called Supreme 
devotion or parabhakti. It means abidance as Pure Being by 
the inner attitude that one is but the Self-Supreme that shines 
as Being-Awareness (sadbhava) by going beyond stance 
(bhavanatita) by the strength acquired by the one pointed 
and incessant practice of that attitude (bhava bhalam).

The ego is verily the seed of all the maladies of 
transmigration which are the outcome of the projection of 
multiplicity (misconception of Truth or vikshepa) through 
the veil of ignorance (called non-conception of Truth or 
āvaraṇā) caused by the ego.*
*	 In the first place, the ego, lapsing from the Self, its seat, 
springs up as a separate entity veiling the ground truth of itself. 



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 5

The meaning of ‘Aham’ or ‘I’ is twofold. While the first 
‘aham’ means the mind, the other, I or ‘nan’ or the ego self.

As Thāyumāṉavar aptly puts it 
Nāṉ-eṉṉum ōr-ahandai evark-kum vandu
Nalinda-vuḍaṉ jaga-māyai nāṉā vāhit
Tāṉ-vandu toḍarum-it-tāl vaḷarum tuṉba
Sāharat-tiṉ perumai-evar sāṯṯṟa vallār.*
As the hauteur that is “I’’
Affects and harasses you,
The maya that pertains to the world
Takes diverse forms and pursues you,
As flesh, body, organs external and internal,
As sky, wind, fire, water and earth,
As mountain and forest,
Thus is the ocean of sorrow
That grows out of this –
Who can describe indeed?
“The five sheaths, unreal and insentient, are not the ‘I’. 

And Arunachala, the Form of Self, Being-Awareness-Bliss, 
is indeed am I” is the import. Bhagavan’s pronouncement in 
verse 8 of Upadēsa Undiyār bears this out clearly. 

This is called ‘āvaraṇā’ or non-perception of reality. Secondly the 
ego projects outside, out of itself, the illusory phenomenon of the 
world teeming with multiplicities making them all appear as real. 
This is called ‘vikshepa’ – misperception of truth – of knowing 
one (the unreal) as the other (real). It is the false jiva’s nature to 
look, take them for real and suffer, while the Self’s nature is “to 
be and shine.” Hence the ego is the seed of all calamities.
*	 Thāyumāṉavar, 14-Ākārabhuvaṉam – Chidambara Rahasyam, 
v. 15.



6 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

Aṉiya bhāvat-tiṉ ava-ṉaham āhum 
Aṉa-ṉiya bhāvamē undī-paṟa
Aṉait-tiṉum utta-mam undī-paṟa*

Better than viewing Him
As other (than onself)
Is to hold Him as the ‘I’ within.

Arunachala simply stands for the identity between the 
Mount as the Form of Siva and the name of the Being whose 
substance is Pure Transcendent Awareness – the Self – and 
this is reitereated in Arunachala Pancharatnam as the Self-
Supreme (“Aruṇagiri Paramāṉmāvē..”) residing as the Hill 
Arunachala.

“Uprooting ego (ahattai vēr aṟuppāy)” may also mean 
“uprooting sins”.
A.I.F.A:

1. (a) What is Arunachala? It is “Aham” (the Self). 
“Arunachala, pray annihilate the ego or jivatva of those who 
enquire what that ‘Aham’ is.” This highlights the importance 
of ‘vichara’ or enquiry. As Arunachala has consummated as 
the Self further enquiry is centred on the ‘Self’.

As ‘ego’ is but the ‘jiva’, here jivatva is called ‘aham’.
(b) Aruṇāchalamē aham eṉa niṉaippavar ahattai 

vēraṟuppāy, Arunachala
By the emphasis on the suffix (ē in Aruṇāchalamē) to 

Arunachala implying “Arunachala alone indeed is aham” 
“abhēda bhāvaṉā” or the attitude of non-difference is 
indicated (Holding Him as ‘I’ within). Both Vichara and 

*	 Sri Bhagavan, Upadēsa Undiyār, v. 8.



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 7

contemplation with the stance of non-difference between 
Arunachala and Self will eliminate the jivatva.

(c) Arunachala, Thou dost wipe out the sins of those 
who think of Thee in their hearts. This speaks of meditation 
in general.

2.	 Azhagu-sundaram-pōl aha-mum nī-yumuṯṟu
		  Abhiṉ-ṉamā yirup-pōm Aruṇāchalā

Substance: 
Arunachala! Like “azhagu” and “sundaram” that 

differ as terms but are identical contentwise we – Thou 
and I – differ in the awareness of adjuncts, but remain one 
in our real nature.
Commentary:

Verse 24 of Upadēsa Undiyār reflects this very idea…
iruk-kum iyaṟkai-yāl īsa-jīvarkaḷ
oru-poruḷē yāvar undī-paṟa
upādhi uṇarvē-vēṟu undī-paṟa

The creature and the Creator are, in substance, one. 
They differ only in the awareness of adjuncts.
‘Azhagu’ and ‘Sundaram’, though differ as terms, one in 

Tamil and the other in Sanskrit, both mean the same namely, 
‘beauty’. This symbolizes the fact of these two ancient 
tongues having co-existed from the hoary past to the present 
day having walked the corridors of history hand in hand in 
perfect amity and harmony. Whatever may be the historical 
association and affinity between them, that they, with their 
common and special characteristics, unique and specific to 



8 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

them, have grown together with no rancour or animosity 
between them is crystal clear.

Bhagavan, having hailed the Mother and Sire of his life, 
Arunachala, in the first verse, in this second one, by way of 
drawing a parallel for his union with Arunachala, effected 
by His Grace, reminisces the father and mother of his body* 
indicating the perfect marital harmony and identity that 
prevailed between them. Verse 9 of Navamaṇimālai also 
reflects this fact wherein he says “Arunachala, as my father 
and mother having borne me (ammaiyum appaṉumāy eṉaip 
bhūmiyil ākkiyaḷittu)”

‘Azhagu’ and ‘Sundaram’ stand for ‘Jiva’ and ‘Siva’ 
respectively.
A.I.F.A:

Arunachala, though Azhagu and Sundaram are different, 
belonging to two lingua franca yet are non-different in 
content. Likewise let us remain non-different in True-Being, 
though Thou and I differ due to adjuncts.

“Remain (uṯṯṟu)” is to have the attitude of difference 
removed.

3.	 Aham-puhun dīrt-tuṉ aha-guhai siṟai-yāi
		  Amar-vitta deṉkol Aruṇāchalā

Substance: 
Arunachala! Entering the mind – my home – and 

forcefully pulling me to the Heart – the Home of Thy 

*	 Even Valluva Nayanar, the author of the famous literary classic 
Tirukkuṟaḷ – the magnum opus – in his very first couplet “ahara 
mudala” has hinted at his parents as “ādi bhagavan mudaṯṯṟē 
ulahu” is worth recalling here.



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 9

Being – Thou hath kept me imprisoned and inescapably 
secure forever. How wonderful is Thy Grace? 
Commentary: 

Cave (Guhai) is the Heart. The dwelling places of jiva and 
Siva are the mind and heart respectively. This is the content 
in spiritual (adhyathmic) sense. The cursory meaning is 
obvious. A lady love complains to her beloved of his daring 
act of forcing his way into her house and abducting her 
away to keep her imprisoned by force in his home. Even in 
the plane of earthly existence, from the time Bhagavan was 
brought to Arunachala – the Heart of the Universe – never 
did he, not even once, leave its precincts, is worthy of being 
remembered. The causeless compassion of Arunachala is 
wondered at by Bhagavan, his heart gladdened with bliss.
A.I.F.A:

“Arunachala! Thou entered my heart, pulled my 
awareness inward to that Heart Cave – Thy Form – and in 
that prison held me firmly. Wherefore dids’t Thou do this?” 

The Lord remains in the Heart inseparably for ever. 
Nevertheless, He does not reveal Himself and shine till one 
is claimed and ruled by Him. When He doth shine, after 
being claimed, He appears to have entered the Heart ‘just 
then’; hence “entering the Heart, (aham puhundu).”

The Lord, even while creating, has made the mind and 
organs oriented outwards with exteriorized perception. 
Only in the case of jivas chosen by Him He turns them 
inward and that unless and until they are made so by Him, 
for the externalized perception to turn inward is impossible, 
is a certainty. This explains the use of the term “drawing or 
pulling (īrttu)”. That the awareness that is interiorized once, 
never again turns outward, even a trifle is indicated by the 



10 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

phrase “made the heart-cave a prison (aha guhai siṟaiyāi 
amarvittu)”.

In the physical plane of the empirical life of Bhagavan, 
“entering the house,” would refer to the ‘house’ in Madurai 
whence he was drawn to Tiruvannamalai and “Cave of the 
Heart” would refer to the Cave in the Hill.

4.	 Āruk kā-veṉai yāṇ-ḍaṉai yahaṯ-ṯṟiḍil
		  Akilam pazhit-tiḍum Aruṇāchalā

Substance:
Arunachala! For whose sake dids’t Thou claim me? 

Thou, who ruled over me happily earlier were to abandon me 
thereafter with disdain the whole world will heap calumny 
on Thee. 
Commentary:

That the people of the world may hold on to Thy Feet 
and be redeemed, Thou, facilitating the engendering of such 
faith in them in Thy Grace by Thy example of claiming me, 
wert Thou to spurn me with indifference now, they would 
lose even the little faith they have in Thee. They, besides 
developing distrust in Thee, bereft of a way of Release, not 
only will come to rack and ruin but will start maligning 
Thee. Hence he queries the Lord “For whose sake didst 
Thou claim me?” and states that He Himself has invited the 
stigma on Him.



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 11

5.	 Ip-pazhi tap-puṉai ēṉ-niṉaip pit-tāi
		  Iṉi-yār viḍu-vār Aruṇāchalā

Substance: 
Arunachala, absolve Thyself from this infamy. Why 

didst Thou make me remember Thee? Who will let Thee 
slip and go away henceforth?
Commentary: 

“This calumny (ippazhi)” referred to here is the disgrace 
that would befall His lot (for withdrawing His Grace after 
having claimed him) that he mentioned in the earlier couplet. 

This ignominy in the first place, is inadmissible for it is 
incompatible with and is alien to His nature for He is verily 
the embodiment of limitless grace. Secondly, Bhagavan 
who had already been chosen and claimed into the sway 
of His grace cannot brook this indifference and finds it 
insufferable. He pleads to Him to absolve Himself from this 
slander either for Bhagavan’s sake or for His own sake, or 
for the sake of both and most of all for the sake of the people 
of the world at large who are worthy of being – and waiting 
to be – claimed by Him.

Further the compulsion of graciously letting him abide 
in Him forever and never letting him part from Him, is one 
that was brought on Arunachala by Himself and not by him. 
Hence Bhagavan boldly asks Him “Why did you make me 
remember Thee?” (uṉai ēṉ niṉaippittāy?)”. Maṇivāchakar 
also says:

Pērāmē cittam-eṉum tiṇ-kayiṯ-ṯṟāl tirup-pādam
Kaṭ-ṭuvitta vit-tagaṉār viḷaiyāḍal*

*	 Tiruvāchakam, 31-Kaṇḍapattu, v. 7.



12 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

Fast tied He to His sacred Feet by willing mind’s 
stout bonds that never part; the sporting of the 
wondrous One – all knowing.
The remembrance of Arunachala was not a volitional 

thought by a weak mind but one that was induced in him by 
the limitless prowess of the Lord. Hence it waxes forever in 
solid strength but never slackens or becomes weary. So he 
says, “Who will let Thee go, henceforth (iṉi yār viḍuvār)”. 
His mind having tasted the experience of the rapturous Bliss 
of Arunachala Siva, henceforth, on its own and effortlessly 
and unceasingly without ever forgetting, will joyously be 
contemplating Him. This is akin to the proverbial saying 
that a cat that has tasted the sweet milk once, will forever 
be after it. As he later remarks in this Text, “Exorcising me 
of the ghost of the ego, Thou, Brahman, the formless ghost 
beyond being exorcised, hast possessed me with a firmer 
grip, turning me into a ghost of Thy own Form “pēyttaṉam 
viḍaviḍāp pēyāp piḍittu eṉaip pēyaṉ ākkiṉai).”

Even if he were to give up the dhyana of Arunachala, 
the Arunachala dhyana will never relinquish its hold on him 
for it has become his Self is the import of “Who will let 
Thee go? (iṉi yār viḍuvār?)”

6.	 Īṉḏṟi-ḍum aṉṉaiyiṟ peri-daruḷ puri-vōi
		  Iduvō uṉa-daruḷ Aruṇāchalā

Substance:
Thou raineth on Thy devotees profound grace with 

compassion and solicitude that is far greater than that of a 
good mother who rears her begotten children. Oh! Such is 
the wonderful wealth of Thy Grace.



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 13

Commentary:
Though the pure and unselfish love a mother showers 

on her children is far inferior compared to the limitless 
grace of Arunachala, yet a mother’s love stands foremost 
and leads the scale as the highest form of love experienced 
by human beings. Taking it as the farthest boundary of 
unselfish and pure love, true to the testimony of the learned 
devout like Maṇivāchakar who proclaims, “With greater 
tenderness than a mother’s, Thou cut off illusive birth (tāyiṟ 
peridun dayāvuḍaiya tamperumāṉ*)”, Bhagavan affirms, 
“Who bestowest love far kinder and gracious than a mother 
(aṉṉaiyiṟ peridaruḷ purivōy)”. Further to preclude others like 
foster mothers and wet nurses, who to some extent fill in the 
role of a mother, he uses the phrase “more than a mother 
who begets (īṉḏṟiḍum aṉṉai)”. That he may not, like the 
ordinary worldlings wallow in the ways of the ephemeral 
world tossed about by trials and troubles only to become 
weary and fatigued, the Lord intercepted and redeemed him 
to His Feet by triggering the remembrance of Arunachala 
in him even in the innocent and non-discriminating age of 
boyhood. 

This deed of limitless Grace and mercy with 
overwhelming freshness ever remains uppermost in his heart 
and he refers to it pointedly as “this (idu)”. And this love 
being supremely greater than that of an endearing mother, 
is what, he says, befits Arunachala. Hence the exclamation, 
“Oh! Thy Grace, (O uṉadaruḷ).” Yet panicking out of the 
doubt that were He to impose on Himself an imaginary 
cause and cite it as pretext to withdraw His grace and turn 
him away (4th verse), wondering what he would do in such 

*	 Tiruvāchakam, 13-Tiruppūvalli, v. 3.



14 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

an eventuality, and thinking that forewarning would be the 
best way of forearming, declares, “Is this all the glory of 
Thy grace (iduvō uṉadaruḷ?)” Thus he upbraids Arunachala 
by virtue of the liberty which he has all along been enjoying 
with Him.

7.	 Uṉaiyē māṯ-ṯṟi ōḍā duḷat-tiṉmēl
		  Uṟu-diyāy irup-pāy Aruṇāchalā

Substance:
Let not my mind dupe and give Thee, the rightful 

Lord of my dear life, the slip and like an impounded cow 
let loose, rove and run after the objects of the five senses 
of this vile body and be whirled around Pray Graciously 
abide and be seated in my heart firmly.
Commentary: 

Due to the deep rooted vasanas of sense objects it is 
impregnated with, the mind, with its inherent nature of 
being turbulent with a speed that shames even the Lord 
of wind, exploits even the slightest slackness caused by 
inadvertence, plays pranks and cheats everybody landing 
one in distress spelling doom. Devotees wedded to sattvic 
qualities reposing faith in the munificent compassion of the 
Lord must endeavour not to give even the slightest room 
for such inadvertence but stand firm and safe on the shore – 
the Self – True Awareness – a Heart distilled in its pristine 
purity – is the content. When the words are regrouped 
“Transforming Thee – (uṉaiyē māṯṟi)” the meaning that 
emerges would be: “Let not my mind transform Thee – the 



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 15

Pure Awareness – into the gross, or the physical world made 
up of the five elements – an illusory appearance – due to 
delusion and maya and run after that led by vasanas but 
remain seated firmly and shine in all Thy real splendour in 
my Heart.” This is the spiritual or adhyatmic import.
A.I.F.A.:

Arunachala, The Rider of the horse of my mind pray, 
graciously be seated firmly on the back of my mind-horse so 
that it may not gallop on the wicked track.

“The wicked path (koḍuneṟi)” stands for worldly life. 
To the extent a mind is entrenched in the depths of worldly 
life, to that extent it is plagued by the travails and troubles 
of samsara. Hence the term “not to run after (ōḍādu)” is 
used. The mischief of even a tiny slip from “Being” may 
gradually sink one deeper and deeper into the quagmire of 
wicked wayward ways till unknown to the rider it plunges 
one irretrievably into its very depths. Hence he pleads 
“remain firmly seated (uṟudiyāy iruppāy)”.

8.	 Ūr-suṯ ṯṟuḷam-viḍā duṉaik-kaṇ ḍaḍaṅ-giḍa
		  Uṉ-azha haik-kāṭ Aruṇāchalā

Substance:
Pray Graciously reveal the splendour and beauty of 

Thy whole Beatific Form, so that my mind impelled by 
the strength of latent tendencies now running after the 
sense objects of the entire world without a respite, may 
behold Thee unceasingly and subside and sink in Thee 
never again to be resurrected.



16 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

Commentary: 
“World (ūr)” literally meaning a city or a village, here 

stands both for the world and the objects and affairs of the 
world. “The exquisite beauty of Thy Form (uṉ azhahu)” 
stands for the grandeur of the Beauty of the Real, All-
Full Form of Arunachala, that remains and shines as Pure 
Awareness, attributeless and immutable, shining with Self-
effulgence that negates all things that manifest out of the 
expansive play of maya, masquerade as real and befuddle 
the mind. Since the mind has not so far beheld within the 
One real Beauty that is homogenous and changeless in 
all its magnificence, lured by vasanas formed over time 
beginningless, darts out, roams and runs after the objects 
and affairs of the world that are quite ‘alien to’ and ‘other 
than’ itself. Hence Bhagavan pleads to Arunachala to reveal 
to him His ubiquitous Form so that his mind may cease 
such meandering. The prayer voiced here “Reveal Thy Real 
Beatific Perfect Form (uṉ azhahaik kāṭṭu)” gets echoed in 
Verse 75 as “Let me behold Thy splendour (uṉ bhavisu 
kaṇḍuṟa varuḷ)” and in verse 32 as “Reveal Thy Form of 
resplendent Light (uṉ jōti urukkāṭṭu)”. The import, simply 
put, is as follows: The mind is now beguiled and bound by 
the spell of the glitter of the attractions of mere external 
appearance of the mundane world and is ever on the ceaseless 
chase after them. If only it were to behold within perennially 
the splendour of the real Beauty of Arunachala, more 
beautiful and exalted than beauty itself, totally oblivious of 
all externals, it, homing in, in Arunachala will ultimately 
sink and subside in Him obtaining unbroken prevalence of 
Infinite Peace and Quiescence.



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 17

A.I.F.A.:
Reveal Thy Beauty so that my mind rummaging the 

whole world for sense pleasures may subside in Thee 
without ever leaving hold of Thee.

“To behold unceasingly (viḍādu kāṇḍal)” is also to behold 
without let or hindrance known as “seeing without seeing.”

“Subsiding or inhering here (aḍaṅgudal)”: The tendency 
of the mind is to linger on and settle upon the place wherever 
beauty is seen more than in other places. When it does 
happen to perceive ‘a beauty that is non-dual’, that very 
moment, completely rid of agitation, subsides and inheres 
in homogenous unity with That. Hence Bhagavan prays 
“Reveal Thy Beauty so that my mind ever beholding Thee 
may be stilled in Thee never to rise up again (uḷam viḍādu 
uṉaik kaṇḍu aḍaṅkiḍa uṉ azhahaik kāṭṭu)”. What is termed 
as ‘beauty’ here is ‘Swarupa’, His Nature and Form. “That 
which is” is, indeed, Beauty. “To reveal it (kāṭṭal)” is to rid the 
delusion of names and forms that appear in the mind like the 
spectacle of snake on a rope. The assumption of specialities 
arises only when there is artificial multiplicity. Likewise 
the use of metaphors arises when there is fragmentation 
by way of unreal attributes ignoring the underlying reality. 
This beauty, natural and non-dual, is really beautiful, neither 
fragmented nor unreal.



18 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

9.	 Eṉai-azhit tippō deṉaik-kala vā-viḍil
		  Iduvō āṇmai Aruṇāchalā

Substance:
If Thou dost not conjoin me in unison when I am 

matured and fit, deflowering the virginity of my ego, 
does this befit Thy manliness, Oh Purusha, the Supreme 
among men?
Commentary: 

The lady-love unable to contain the irrepressible and 
overwhelming pangs of love and passion for her beloved 
that has transcended the level of restraint even foregoes 
the modesty that is dearer than life to women of her virtue. 
And, contrary to the cultured ways of the world, with frank 
outspokenness, thus exposes her love for him. Since chastity 
for a woman is dearer than even modesty, for a lady-love 
of social status to resort to such plain and unconventional 
address is admissible. But how is that? It is so since the 
learned dictate “modesty is indeed superior to one’s very 
life; but chastity, is truly far superior.”

Those who accept and abet their own self-destruction 
are the rarest of the rare in the world. There are great heroes 
who to gain such a sweet and excellent loss (self-loss) 
not only endeavour with utmost intensity but extend such 
a warm invitation to Yama the Lord of Death who bides 
awaiting the right time to seize and carry away one’s dear 
life. But Bhagavan, dauntless and more heroic than such 
men of exemplary heroism, dares to throw his gauntlet to 
Arunachala enraging Him by giving a loud and clarion 
call challenging His manliness. Is it not the fruit of such 
daring deed that he has achieved the exalted state beyond 



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 19

which nothing greater can exist where he as Arunachala 
and Arunachala as he remains ensconced in perfect non-
duality in the form of Beatific Bliss beyond name and form 
as verily the Beacon light on the Hill beckoning devotees 
of Arunachala and shedding the effulgence of spiritual 
light illumining the path of the men of the world at large to 
blissful Beatitude?

10.	 Ēṉinda uṟak-kam eṉaip-piṟar izhukka
		  Idu-vuṉak kazhagō Aruṇāchalā

Substance: 
Wherefore is the unnatural façade of sleep on Thy part? 

While the desperadoes of lawless gang of desire, anger 
and others, aliens, who have nothing in common with me 
who rightfully belong to Thee, drag me forcefully to ways 
of depravity, does this deed (of nonchalance and seeming 
indifference despite Thy being aware of it) behove or lend 
beauty to Thee, who as the Lord and protector of the jivas 
is the Lord of my Self?
Commentary: 

Nakkirar pleads to Lord Subrahmanya, “Thou whose 
bounden duty it is to protect and guard me, wert Thou not 
to perform it, who does it serve, Oh the Six-faced Lord? 
(kākkak kaḍaviyanī kāvādu irundakkāl ārkkup paramām 
Aṟumukhavā?)”.

“This sleep – (inda uṟakkam)” at once indicates that it 
is not a natural slumber but a feigned and unreal one. Since 
the triple-eyed Lord, Arunachala Siva, the Pure Being-
Awareness, the very form of the Self, is ever unwinking and 



20 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

ever awake and never given to sleep by His inherent nature, 
this sleep is nothing but slumber feigned. Hence, the poser, 
“Why this sleep? (ēṉ inda uṟakkam?)”. Those wicked men 
who forcefully take the lady away out of lust to despoil her 
virginity have absolutely no relationship whatsoever with 
her. This he declares in the sentence “dragged away by 
wicked men totally unknown and alien to my nature (eṉaip 
piṟar izhukka)”. That she may not further be humiliated by 
being subjected to the transgressions of these wicked men 
of wayward ways and be branded as one fallen from virtue 
she pleads to Him for succour. Further she is helpless and 
weak and has no other refuge than her powerful Lord whose 
inalienable duty is to safeguard the twin virtues of chastity 
and honour of the beloved. Refraining from performing 
one’s bounden duty is not the ‘dharma’ (laid down propriety 
or righteousness) of the Lord. Hence she says “Does this 
behove Thee or lend beauty to Thy stature (idu uṉakku 
azhagō?)”. The mind that treads fast and strong on the 
chaste and virtuous way of the path of Bhakti – is often 
dragged down to evil ways in the region of sense objects and 
matters, even as the lustful and wicked Kauravas dragged 
the virtuous Panchali. Speaking of this most reproachful and 
sinful acts inanimates like greed, anger, etc. are personified 
as ‘beings’ – depraved reprobates – and are bracketed as 
“piṟar”. This is borne out by Valluvar’s statement too in 
“iṉmaiyeṉa vorupāvi” – “Caitiff indigence is an enemy to 
the joys of this life as well as to those of the next.”
A.I.F.A:

Arunachala, while I, Thy legitimate charge, is being 
dragged by wicked men unacceptable and alien to my 



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 21

nature, Thou, though perceiving it, turnest a blind eye and 
art keeping still, does it behove Thee? Wherefore is this 
feigned sleep on Thy part, Tell me?

It is the common experience that even ordinary men 
will not brook their better-halves being abducted away by 
men unknown. If that be so, it does not behove Thee, the 
Supreme among men, to remain silent, still and unmoved, 
while witnessing this event. Hence he says, “While I, Thy 
possession am being dragged away, does this seeming 
indifference lend Thee honour and credit? (eṉaip piṟar 
izhukka idu uṉakku azhagō?)”

What is referred to as “sleep” here is “turiateetham”, 
the state of Surpeme Quiescence. This is the adhyatmic 
meaning.

11.	 Aim-pulak kaḷvar ahat-tiṉil puhum-pōdu
		  Ahat-til-ṉī ilaiyō Aruṇāchalā

Substance: 
(To steal away unjustly from the treasure chest of my 

Heart, the priceless gem beyond compare of Atman, my 
birthright) When the marauders of the five senses enter 
my heart could Thou have been not present?
Commentary: 

When the rightful owner of the house is very much 
present in the house, the thieves dare not storm into the 
house, steal and scoot away with the booty. Secondly, it is 
impossible for the indweller of the heart to remain away 
from it even for a trice. Hence the plaintive sob, “When they 
entered the heart, wert Thou not present (kaḷvar ahattiṉil 



22 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

puhumpōdu ahattil nīyilaiyō?)”. The implied answer is very 
much in the affirmative asserting “Yes! Thou wert not absent. 
Certainly Thou wert there.” Even while Thou remainest, in 
truth, Thou did not slumber away either. This complaint was 
lodged in the earlier verse, “Why this pretended slumber 
(ēṉ inda uṟakkam)?” Bhagavan conveys his conviction to 
Arunachala, with injured innocence, that the robbers gaining 
entry did not happen but by His permission and accuses 
Him in the succeeding verse as “This is but Thy deceit (uṉ 
sūdē idu)”. In hearts where knowledge and realization of 
Arunachala’s firm abidance – the chaste Union with Siva – 
dawn, the mischievous play of the luring senses five ceases 
once for all.

The following verse from Tiruvāchakam is worth paying 
attention to here.

Urai-māṇḍa uḷḷoḷi uttamaṉ-vandu uḷam-puhalum
Karai-māṇḍa kāmap peruṅ-kaḍalaik kaḍattalumē
Irai-māṇḍa indiriyap paṟavai irindu-ōḍat
Turai-māṇḍa vā-pāḍit tōṇōkkam āḍāmō.*
The inner Light, past speech, the Worthiest entered 

within
My soul, and brought me through lust’s mighty sea 

that knows no shore
And then the craving senses’ sateless vultures routed 

fled!
Sing how a royal path in glory was made plain; and 

thus
Play we Tonokkam!

*	 Māṇikkavāchakar, Tiruvāchakam, 15-Tiruttōṇōkkam, v. 14.



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 23

A.I.F.A:
“When the marauders of the five senses entered the heart to 

seize and carry away the wealth, were you not there?”Is not the 
usefulness of one abiding in the House to prevent the thieves 
from gaining an entry into the house? Since that does not 
seem to be happening he says, “it seems Thou wert not there”. 
This prayer, based on the ways of the world, is to prevent the 
awareness from darting outward along with the senses.

12.	 Oru-vaṉām uṉṉai oḷit-tevar varu-vār
		  Uṉ-sūdē idu Aruṇāchalā

Substance: 
Thou, as the ever luminous Sun of Wisdom, bereft of 

a rising and setting, shinest as the One by virtue of Thy 
nature of being the Universal Witness. Who, then, have the 
power to blindfold Thee to make their entry unnoticeable 
and enter? (As there is none who has the might to do so) 
Their trespassing is but a planned deceit staged by Thee.
Commentary: 

The significance of the attribute (of being the sole one) 
“Thou who art the One (oruvaṉām uṉṉai)” is derived from 
the fact of Arunachala being the Self-effulgent Self that can 
never be hidden (oḷittu). Anticipating the defensive rejoinder 
in the form of a lame excuse that Arunachala may resort to, 
such as “Though I was in the heart I slept away,” Bhagavan 
pre-empts Him by calling Him as the “Sole one from whose 
notice none can escape” and thus refutes it. Citing this as 
sufficient reason, he arrives at the emphatic conclusion that 
“they entered, after all, with Thy concurrence.” 



24 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

The spiritual or adhyatmic content of this verse is as 
follows:

That Arunachala abides as the mere witness holds good 
in the empirical plane or in the transactional world with the 
outward perception in the sense of “otherness”. All mental
