appearances of differences and dualities like good and 
bad, nay, the entire empirical world of plurality, all being 
mere mental conceptions of the reflected Awareness – the 
jiva – are totally absent in Arunachala, the Transcendent, 
Effulgent, Pure Being – Awareness. This is the adhyatmic 
import in the ultimate sense. That the succeeding couplet 
reinforces this idea can easily be discerned by men of deep 
reflection and enquiry.
A.I.F.A:

Who can enter the Heart other than Thee, who is the 
One without a second? If anyone does so, it is Thy deceit 
Arunachala! 

That if someone else were to enter since Thou art the One 
without a second, Thou Thyself donning the form of others, 
dost enter, goes without saying. Hence as there is no ‘other’ 
this is “just Thy drama of deceit (uṉ sūdē idu)” says Bhagavan. 
“To hide from Thee, (oḷittal)” here means, “to avoid” or “to 
go out of existence”. What is seen as the senses is also the 
form of Atman is the import. Yet another reading is “Who can 
blind fold Thee, the unrivalled in safeguarding and enter?”

“Hiding Thee (uṉai maṟaittal)” – hiding from Thy 
Knowledge. Confirmed by the truth contained in the 
common adage that there can be no conception unknown 
to the mother, there can be no deceit unknown to the Heart. 
Never does anything come to pass, hidden from Thee who 



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 25

art the Witness of all beings in their entirety. Hence if there 
is any who enters the heart even that happens with Thy 
secret connivance by way of spying and not by blindfolding 
or hoodwinking Thee. This is expressed by the words “This 
is verily Thy stratagem (uṉ sūdē idu)”. These two contents 
are based on advaita and dvaita respectively.

13.	 Ōṅkārap poruḷ op-puyar vil-lōy
		  Uṉai-yār aṟi-vār Aruṇāchalā

Substance: 
Thou shinest as the glory of the mystic syllable ‘OM’, 

unrivalled and unsurpassed. Who can comprehend Thee 
the way Thou really art?
Commentary: 

The content of ‘OM’ remains, in the ego-free and integral 
vision as the all – imagined and projected by maya by its 
adroit and wondrous power of making apparently possible 
what is inherently impossible. Yet by the nature of its being 
the Peace Serene of Sivam, the Absolute Truth that is beyond 
the range of imagination, It is the Form of Knowledge whole 
and Perfect, Awareness with the nature “To be and shine”, 
and shines as the effulgence of Being alone, bereft of the 
manifestation and tricks of maya. The extensive details of 
this can be studied on their own by seekers from Texts like 
the Kārika (on Mandukya Upanishad) by Gaudapadha and 
by listening to those who have learnt and assimilated the 
truth conveyed in them. The import of ‘OM’ is the non-
dual Atman, the rich content beyond the ken of speech 
and thought. Appar in Thēvāram speaks of it as “ōṅkārat 



26 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

toruva ṉāhum” – (He will appear before you in Omkāram).” 
Hence Bhagavan hails Him as “one with none as equal or 
superior (oppuyarvillōy)”. Arunachala, lending light to the 
mind shines within the mind as its source, the form of Pure 
Awareness. He, being non-dual, He can be known either by 
Atma Vichara or by self-surrender. The body-bound ego, by 
the unceasing practice of Atma Vichara fades away, subsides 
as the true Self and abides merely as “Pure Awareness”. 
In the surrender of the self, the ego burns and dies in the 
steady flame of bhakti and shines in Siva as Siva Himself 
(Sivoham). And the mighty masters who, shedding the ‘I’ 
in its totality have become the very form of “That (TAT)” 
by being established in the tranquil peace of the primordial 
Silence (mona) can know Him directly in the plenitude of 
the experience of the Self (Pure Awareness) by being the 
Self (Awareness in the Heart).

On the other hand, the others having parted from 
Arunachala, the Atman, out of inadvertence, function as ‘I’ 
due to ignorance. The ego here further asserts itself as if 
in truth it were the sole and prime content. However wise 
and learned with razor sharp intellect people may be in the 
illusory fields of empirical knowledge, they will not be able 
to apprehend the Self – as It really is – by the sheer strength 
and prowess of their mind alone. Hence Bhagavan exclaims, 
“Who can know Thee (uṉai yār aṟivār)?”

Appaḍiyum an-niṟamum av-vaṇ ṇamum
	 Avaṉ-aruḷē kaṇṇāgak kāṇiṉ allāl
Ippaḍiyaṉ in-niṟattaṉ iv-vaṇ ṇattaṉ
	 Ivaṉ-iṟaivaṉ eṉḏṟu-ezhudik kāṭṭo ṇādē.*

*	 Appar Thēvāram, 6th Tirumuṟai, 97-Podhu, v. 10.



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 27

Except to behold Him with the Eye of His Grace, a 
Gift from Him, none can draw a portrait of the Lord 
and say “He is Civan, such and such a person, of 
such a colour and of such a nature”.

Iṉḏṟu-eṉakku aruḷi iruḷ-kaḍindu uḷḷattu
	 Ezhu-giṉdṟa jñāyiṟē pōṉḏṟu
Niṉḏṟa-niṉ taṉmai niṉaippu-aṟa niṉaindēṉ
	 Nī-yalāl piṟidu-maṯ ṯṟiṉmai
Ceṉḏṟu-ceṉḏṟu aṇuvāy tēyndu-tēyndu oṉḏṟu-ām
	 Tirup-perun tuṟai-uṟai Sivaṉē
Oṉḏṟum-nī yallai aṉḏṟi-oṉḏṟu illai
	 Yār-uṉṉai aṟiyakiṟ pārē.*
This day,
gracing me,
Thou chased away my murk,
Poised in my soul like the rising sun.
On this – Thy inhering nature and there being nought 

else but Thyself 
I thought and thought till thought Itself ceased.
O Siva abiding at the sacred Perunturai !
Coming closer and closer to Thee and in the process,
Wearing away again and again,
I was reduced to my atomic self shorn of all else and got 

Oned with Thee.
Thou are not aught in the universe;
Yet there is nought save Thee.
Who can know Thee at all?

*	 Māṇikkavāchakar, Tiruvāchakam, 22-Kōyil Tiruppadikam, v. 7



28 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

14.	 Auvai-pōl eṉak-kuṉ aruḷait tan-deṉai
		  Āḷuva duṉ-kaḍaṉ Aruṇāchalā

Substance: 
Thou art countless times greater in benevolent 

compassion than a mother who, by her inherent nature 
showers gracious love on her child. It is Thy bounden 
duty to enfold me who has sought and reached Thee in 
Thy Reign of holy Grace and claim me to Thy Feet.
Commentary: 

“Like a mother (auvai pōl)”: May Thou bestow on me 
like a solicitous mother showering gracious love on her 
child. Applying the rules of Tamil grammar, this can be 
interpreted to mean anyone of the following or of all the 
gracious deeds of Siva:

Just as Thou, in all compassion bestowed the grand vision 
of Kailasa on the lady-saint Auvai, just as Thou granted Thy 
left side to Devi Uma, the Universal Mother and just as 
Thou granted the ‘Divine State of Deliverance to my dear 
mother, bestow Thy Grace on me too.’* If a poser regarding 
the moot point, “Is it not Lord Ganesa who bestowed Grace 
on the grand old lady, Auvai?” is raised, It can be countered 
by citing the verity of the Upanishadic statement “The son 
is verily the father, (ātma vai putra namasi)”, that the act 
of compassion by the son can be deemed as the one by the 
Father, the Lord – a saying by way of courtesy. This can be 

*	 Obviously Muruganar out of his intense reverence to Bhagavan 
has considered this as a prayer of Bhagavan. While the Text of 
Akshara Mana Malai was composed in 1914, Bhagavan’s mother 
was absorbed in Arunachala in 1922.



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 29

substantiated by the worldly tradition of crediting the King 
with the victory while the actual war is won by the army and 
the army commanders. The literary tradition dealing with 
mythological subjects also lends support to this view. The 
proud status of being the helper of Siva obtained by Mount 
Meru by being the Lord’s bow is credited to Himavan as He 
is the King of Mountains, as revealed by “imayavil vāṅgiya 
vīrñcaḍai yandaṇaṉ (The Lord of long and widely spread 
matted locks who strung the bow Himavan).”

The blessed mother of Bhagavan through the prayer of 
Sri Bhagavan obtained the Grace of Lord Arunachaleswara 
and His Divine state, is evidenced by the Temple of 
Matrubhuteswara that stands over her physical remains. As 
a verse in Navamanimalai asserts “In His unmoving Form of 
Arunachala, the Supreme Atman soars high in all It’s might 
and splendour, with Sakti withdrawn to merge in Him non-
dually (achala uruvil accatti oḍuṅkiḍa ōṅgum, Aruṇāchalam 
eṉḏṟu aṟi).” That Devi Uma, in order to commingle as one 
with Siva obtaining the left part of Him subsided in the form 
of Arunachala is famed in legendary lore.
A.I.F.A.:

“Arunachala, like a loving mother who, showering her 
love governs her child, raining Grace on me and ruling over 
me is Thy bounden duty.”

Though a mother’s solicitous grace and loving care pales 
into insignificance when compared to the all-embracing 
Grace of the Lord yet greater than a mother’s love, there is 
none in the world; hence the comparison. Just as in days of 
yore Thou, by bestowing Grace on the grand old lady-saint 
Auvai and governed her, lording over me with Grace is also 



30 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

Thine own duty. It is known that it is Lord Vinayaka who 
bestowed such grace on Auvai but is superimposed on His 
Father, Lord Arunachala here.

15.	 Kaṇ-ṇukkuk kaṇ-ṇāy kaṇ-iṉḏṟik kāṇ-uṉaik 
		  Kāṇuva-devar-pār Aruṇāchalā

Substance:
Thou shinest as the Eye of the eye of the perceiver. 

And yet, without that ‘Eye’ Thou perceivest everything 
without seeing. Who then can behold Thee (how and by 
which eye)? Bestow Thy grace and look at me so that 
I can perceive Thee with Thy Eye of Grace.
Commentary: 

The five instruments of perception like the eye are mere 
sockets. The mind which illumines them with light and 
referred to as the sixth sense is the ‘eye’ of them. That which 
illumines the mind – the chidabhasa, the mere reflected 
awareness – with light, is the Pure Awareness – the Self. 
The Self in this verse is referred to as the ‘Eye of the eye.’

The verse from Ātma Vidyā Kīrttaṉam throws further 
light on this truth.

Viṇṇādiya viḷak-kum kaṇṇādiya poṟikkum 
Kaṇṇā maṉak-kaṇukkum kaṇṇāy*

Annamalai is the Self, the Eye behind the eye of 
mind, which sees the eye and all other senses of 
perception which illumine the sky and other gross 
elements.

*	 Sri Bhagavan, Ātma Vidyā Kīrttaṉam, v. 5.



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 31

From the angle of empirical perception to which we 
are accustomed if it is asked ‘is not seeing without an eye 
an improbability?’ the answer is “yes”. But unlike our 
perception, imagined and differentiated, involving the triad 
of the perceiver, the perceived and the perception, that True 
Perception is a singularly wondrous spectacle but of a totally 
different nature. Hence it was commented upon as “seeing 
without seeing”. 

In the Absolute plane Arunachala, the Pure Awareness 
shines sole and whole, bereft of everything but Himself. 
Arunachala’s ‘seeing without an eye’ is the wonderful 
spectacle of Himself remaining and shining as the all that 
appears real in the perception of jiva – a mere reflected 
awareness – mere illusory creations conceptualised, 
fabricated and projected by the mind and appearing as if 
real. This is based on the doctrine of illusory appearance 
or vivartha. The serpent on a rope is a vivartha and since 
the rope appears as the serpent, the rope is the serpent. 
So is the case of movement in Being. Based on the same 
logic Arunachala Himself has become and shines as the 
world of multitudinous names and forms. He is all – true 
Being-Awareness, and the world perceived as the ‘other’ 
is a perverse misunderstanding. The rope itself being the 
Seer Awareness, can it see some being other than itself, 
some snake? (Without the body the world is not; without 
the mind the body is not. Without awareness there is no 
mind. And without being there is no awareness. Hence 
‘Being’ is the all).

This spectacle of perception without an eye is what 
is acknowledged and accepted by knowers of Truth like 
Bhagavan as the Supreme Truth or goal, the Omniscience 



32 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

or the quality of all-knowing. Firstly there is no instrument 
of perception other than the eye. Secondly Arunachala, 
who is the Non-dual Truth of Unbroken Pure Awareness, 
is the True Eye and is ever totally bereft of inadvertence 
and the consequent delusion of the ego, that identifies itself 
with its upadhis and adjuncts which in turn is the cause of 
the instrument of perception. Hence the ordinary spectacle 
involving the triad – other than the one mentioned above – 
is not applicable to Him. Uḷḷadu Nāṟpadu (Forty Verses on 
Reality) Verse 4 speaks of this: “Without the eye can there be 
sight or spectacle? The Self, the real eye is Infinite.” What is 
to be known is that the Witness of the Jiva who is called 
“the indweller or the inner ruler” who remains as the life 
of life and the Awareness of awareness is Arunachala. 

What is seen is known by the eye but the eye cannot be 
known by what is seen. Likewise the All-seeing Witness, 
the Overseer, can know the jiva but the jiva cannot know the 
Witness. Hence Bhagavan remarks “Who can perceive Thee 
(uṉaik kāṇuvadu evaṉ?)”.

Since the self-loss or the annihilation of the ego which 
remains as the sole obstacle to the wonderful spectacle of 
Arunachala is one that is mainly accomplished by the Grace 
of Arunachala alone Bhagavan pleads with Arunachala, 
“Bestow Thy Grace and look at me (par)” so that the self-
loss can become an accomplished fact. A verse in Ozhivil 
Oḍukkam affirms this idea picturesquely:

Taṉṉizhappai yeṉṉakaṯṯṟuc cādippāṉ Saṟguruvā
Maṉṉuperuñ cīya madayāṉai - taṉṉaḍaimēṟ
Coppaṉattē tōṉḏṟit tuḍippaṯṯṟuk kēvalamā
Niṟpadupōṟ pārā ṉeṉiṉ.



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 33

With what learning can one achieve the loss of the 
self? It is only when the lion of the sadguru appears 
in the world-dream of a mad elephant of a ripe 
disciple, destroys his ego and makes him stand still 
by his look of compassionate grace, that the jiva is 
made to abide in stillness, with the jivabodha erased 
totally.
As asserted by Bhagavan in a verse later in this Text 

as “Arunachala who shinest as my Guru (guru uruvāy oḷir 
Arunachala)” Arunachala Himself is indeed the Sadguru.
A.I.F.A:

Arunachala, Behold! Who can perceive Thee, Who, being 
the Eye of the perceiving eye, perceives without an eye?

“Eye of the eye (kaṇṇukkuk kaṇṇāy)” by implication 
means remaining as the instrument of the other instruments 
of perception too and mind, the sixth sense, also gets 
included in that. The gathered content of the pithy statement 
“to perceive without an eye (kaṇṇiṉḏṟik kāṇal)” is to know 
without the instruments and the inner organs. As there 
is nothing as the ‘other’ the function of knowing is non-
existent for the Lord. Yet the Witness-nature of the power 
of Awareness, as per the identity of power and the one who 
possesses the power is superimposed on Him in the address 
“Thou the perceiver, without an eye.” If, the perceiver 
seeking who the perceiver is, and tries to perceive himself, 
he disappears. Hence he remarks, “Who can perceive Thee 
(kāṇuvadevar pār)?” However intelligent one may be, unless 
the mind is turned within and inhered in the Lord who, 
lending the light of awareness to the mind shines within the 
mind, one can never know Him by that mind.



34 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

16.	 Kāntam irumbu-pōl kavarn-deṉai viḍāmal
		  Kalan-deṉō ḍirup-pāy Aruṇāchalā

Substance: 
As the magnet draws the iron filings to itself, by the 

prowess of Thy Grace, pray draw me unto Thee never 
letting me part from Thee anymore, graciously commingle 
with me and be united as one with me in all homogeneity 
for ever more.
Commentary:

Pārt-taṉaṉ pudu-mai uyir-vali kānta
	 paru-vadam oru-daram idaṉai
Ōrt-tiḍum uyiriṉ seṭṭai-yai oḍukki
	 oru-daṉa dabhi-muka māha
Īrtta-dait taṉ-pōl acalamāc ceidav
	 iṉṉuyir bali-koḷum ihdeṉ
Ōrttuy-miṉ uyirkāḷ uḷamadil oḷiriv
	 uyirk-koli Aruṇa-mā giriyē.*
Hearken, Ye denizens of the world, the miraculous 
wonder I’ve beheld. This wonderful Hill known as 
Arunachala is one possessed of the power of the 
magnetic pull and entices away beings who perceive 
it, to Itself. It stills the movements of the jiva who 
thinks of it but once, draws him irresistably to It’s 
presence full of divine power beyond compare 
and like a (soul) transforming alchemy ripens it 
to become motionless and finally feasts on it. Is 
not this act of the Hill superbly wonderful? Dear 
brethren! Like the disease co-born with the body 

*	 Sri Bhagavan, Aruṇāchala Padikam, v. 10



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 35

kills the body, this Hill, the destroyer of lives, has 
the nature of lying hidden within as life of life 
and terminating the jivabodha. Dear fellowmen! 
Recognize the grandeur of It’s glory, take refuge in 
It and be released.
Thus it will be seen that Bhagavan voices the same idea 

in Padikam too referring to Arunachala as “the loadstone 
of lives”. Bhagavan highlighted the fact that none can by 
one’s mental prowess alone come to know and realize 
Arunachala in an earlier verse. In this and the succeeding 
verses he beseeches Arunachala the Omniscient, Sole and 
Whole, with dependence on none, to graciously draw him 
who is powerless with no individual freedom and totally 
dependent on Him, by His causeless compassion to Him and 
commingle with Him with non-difference.
A.I.F.A:

Arunachala, even as a hill of lodestone attracts iron 
particles, Thou, drawing me forcefully to Thee and holding 
me firm, never letting me go, be united with me non-dually.

The terms ‘Hill’ and ‘particles’ were employed to 
make the import self-revealing. This comparison brings 
out besides His power of captivating jivas forcibly, the 
exceeding prowess of the Lord in uniting with Him people 
however hard of heart they be. “Since I do not possess the 
sufficient strength to seek Thee by the ‘markata nyaya’ (the 
way a young one of a monkey clings on to its mother, never 
loosening its grip), by the ‘marjala nyaya’ (the way a mother 
cat holds on and takes the kitten along with), Thou must 
claim me”, is the import of the sentence.



36 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

“As a loadstone attracts iron magnetizing it and holds 
it fast (kāntam irumbu pōl).” What prompts Bhagavan to 
say so is the ignorance on the part of the jivas of the fact 
of the Lord ever remaining with them unceasingly. What is 
to be known and realized is that the awareness of this truth 
by way of experience culminating in the serene peace and 
quiescence of the mind is, precisely the Grace of the Lord.

17.	 Giri-yuru vāhiya kirupaik kaḍalē 
			   kirupai-kūrndu aruḷu-vāy Aruṇāchalā

Substance: 
Though a hard and rocky mountain for a physical 

perception, for a gnoetic eye Thou art truly an ocean of 
compassion of a highly soft nature of being moved easily. 
Pray bestow on me Grace with Compassion and solicitude.
Commentary: 

“Unmoving Hill, melting into a Sea of Grace (giri uruvāhiya 
kirupaik kaḍalē)” is an embellishment by way of a figure of 
speech – an oxymoron. The same usage occurs in verse 7 of 
Ashtakam wherein he addresses the Lord as “Arunachala (the 
Mountain Aruna), the ocean of effulgence of boundless Grace 
(aruṇa malaiyeṉu melaiyaṟu maruḷoḷik kaḍalē)”.

The supplication the earlier verse couches, continues here 
as well. Bhagavan here pleads for the descent of Grace in 
general, without spelling out his particular needs. Further, 
Arunachala, the Lord of munificent grace, in His benign 
dispensation bestows on devotees with great solicitude 
whatever they ask for and in the way they desire. He, in the 
wellness of His mind which is but the wealth of all-embracing 
benign grace bereft of bewilderment knows best what is truly 



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 37

most beneficial for them and to be aspired for by them. The 
knowledge of men who seek fulfilment of specific desires 
springs from illusory and perverse knowledge characterized 
by a lack of discrimination mistaking the good for the bad and 
the bad for the good. Those who are well conversant with this 
truth steer clear of this faulty way. Whatever He, an ocean 
of nectarine compassion and of frontierless Knowledge, 
bestows upon him is Pure Grace and His sweet will is closest 
to his heart. Further even if he were under any compulsion to 
ask for the fulfilment of any specific desire, even that would 
be nothing other than devotion to Him. Indicating this clearly 
he rests content with praying simply “Extend Thy merciful 
compassion on me (kirupai kūrndaruḷvāy)”.

A Verse in Tiruvāchakam is just a replica of this sentiment 
Vēṇḍat takkadu aṟivōy nī! vēṇḍa, muzhudum 

taruvōy nī!
Vēṇḍum ayaṉ, māṟku, ariyōy nī! vēṇḍi, eṉṉaip paṇi 

koṇḍāy;
Vēṇḍi, nī yādu aruḷ seydāy, yāṉum, aduvē vēṇḍiṉ 

allāl,
Vēṇḍum parisu oṉḏṟu uṇḍu eṉṉil, aduvum, uṉ taṉ 

viruppu aṉḏṟē?*
Thou know’st what to Desire is meet – when we Desire
	 Thou’rt He who wholly grants! To Ayan and Mal
Desiring Thee, how hard to reach! Yet me Thou didst
	 Desire, my service claim! Desiring what didst Thou
Bestow Thy grace? That and naught else do I desire!
	 And If aught else there be that stirs in me desire,
That too, in sooth, is Thy Desire, – Is it not so?

*	 Māṇikkavāchakar, Tiruvāchakam, 33-Kuzhaitta Pattu, v. 6.



38 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

A.I.F.A:
“Oh, The Ocean of Grace in the form of rocky Hill! 

Bestow Thy Grace and compassion on me.” In keeping with 
the analogy “the Ocean of Compassion (kirupaik kaḍal)” 
the idea “What an inexplicable wonder is it that Thou who 
art verily the Ocean of Compassion appeareth in the form 
of a boulder that doth not melt at the misery of others?” 
emerges. Bhagavan couches this truth in the words “A hard 
and rocky Mountain, verily an Ocean of Compassion (giri 
uruvahiya kirupaik kaḍale)”. Describing a hard-hearted one 
as a stone hearted one is an accepted way of the world. An 
embellishment by way of a figure of speech (oxymoron) is 
used in describing “Ocean in the form of a Hill”. As the 
ocean receives and accepts waters of all kinds and hues 
with no concern for their respective merits and demerits, 
raining Thy Grace uniformly on devotees of many a kind 
unmindful of their differences in ripeness and competence 
is Thy very nature indeed! This he encapsulates in the 
phrase “O, the Ocean of Compassion (karuṇaik kaḍalē)”. 
“Bestowing grace with Compassion” is a plea to the Lord 
to make him abide as one with Him with non-difference in 
the same way the ocean absorbs all the rivers small or big 
or pure or impure into it. The sullage water on joining the 
sea, losing its nature and name becomes a ‘tīrthā’ or ‘holy 
water’. People however lowly or ill-bred be, on uniting with 
the Lord, their jivatva getting annihilated attain to the state 
of Siva. Hence the prayer “to bestow Grace.”



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 39

18.	 Kīzh-mēl eṅgum kiḷar-oḷi maṇi-yeṉ
			   Kīzh-maiyaip pāzh-chei Aruṇāchalā

Substance: 
Arunachala, the precious gem of Sivam, abides and 

shines as sat-chit in all Self-effulgence in the hearts of 
all, high or low, ripe or unripe, as the indweller with 
non-difference. Pray, shine on in my heart and destroy 
the darkness of ignorance that envelops my heart (by the 
Light of Thy serene Grace) and bless me.
Commentary: 

The “low and high (kīzh and mēl)” refer to people of low 
or high status (may be on the basis of ripeness, competence, 
learning and social and economic status). Though the jiva in 
reality is the Atman, the form of most undefiled Knowledge, 
forgetting the truth of himself due to inadvertence he identifies 
the Self with the despicable body of flesh and filth. Hence 
the term lowliness (kīzhmai) naturally refers to the low and 
vile conception of the fragmented individuality or selfhood 
or the ego. It also stands for the cause of this attitude namely 
the dark void of nescience. Hence he beseeches Arunachala 
to render void, the void of ignorance. [Nescience, darkness, 
void or abyss, bondage, nothingness (sunya) Prakriti (nature) 
and Māya are all words that connote lowliness or kīzhmai]. 
Here we may conclude that the plea is for the dissipation of 
the darkness of nescience and the removal of all maladies of 
samsara as nescience is the fundamental cause of the ego 
which in turn is the sole cause of the three fold* miseries of 
grievous embodiment.

*	 The three fold afflictions are: Aadhyatmikam: afflictions arising 
out of one’s own body and mind. Aadhi daivikam – afflictions 



40 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

A.I.F.A:
Arunachala, Thou, the form of light who irradiates 

above, below and all over with Thy Light, rend my 
lowliness – nescience – void.

“All over or everywhere (eṅgum)”: Within, without and 
in all the eight quarters. “Shining (oḷirdal)”: With nothing 
as the “other” of it, with “all” as Itself, Arunachala shines in 
boundless entirety. 

He, being the rarest of the rare to be obtained, is called 
a “jewel or gem (maṇi)”.

He being the form of Effulgence, irradiating everything 
with His own light of Sivam, piercing and brimming over all 
the eight quarters and the intermediate sixteen angles (sub-
divisions of quarters) soaring tall and high, is called “the 
shining precious gem (kiḷar oḷimaṇi)”.

“Lowliness (kīzhmai)” refers to the darkness of 
ignorance. Having slipped and fallen from its exalted state 
of the non-dual, condensed form of Being-Awareness-Bliss, 
due to inadvertence and identifying itself with the transient, 
insentient and grievous body as ‘I’ is styled as ‘lowliness’ 
and “to destroy it (pāzh chei)” is to annihilate that ego so 
as to enable one to regain the state of being the Atman, the 
natural state of one. The description, “Jewel (maṇi)” can be 
attributed to the nature, sui generis of Arunachala and can 
be interpreted as either Arunachala, the gem or Arunachala, 
like the gem. As the immaculate undefiled form of Atman 
is his natural form, it may also mean “the gem that I am 
(maṇiyeṉ)”.

born out of the displeasure of Gods and Aadhi bouthikam – 
afflictions caused by the five elements and other beings. 



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 41

Naḷ-ḷum kīzhu-ḷum mēlu-ḷum yāvu-ḷum
Eḷḷum eṇṇaiyum pōl-ṉiṉḏṟa endaiyē*
In midst, beneath, above, in all contained,
Thou art, my Sire, like oil within the seed!
This verse couched in prayerful tone berating his 

lowliness when read along with verses 18 and 49 helps the 
readers comprehend the content with clarity as they all echo 
the same attitude.

19.	 Kuṯṯṟa-muṯ ṯṟaṟut-teṉai guṇamāyp paṇit-tāḷ
		  Guru-vuru vāy-oḷir Aruṇāchalā

Substance: 
Arunachala! Root out all my faults in their entirety 

along with their source the ego. Make me a virtuous one 
and graciously claim me as a worthy servant to the Feet 
of Thine Who shineth verily in and as the form of Guru.
Commentary: 

The ego, the conceptual body-based I, which is the 
subtle miniscule seed that, containing within itself all the 
faults of man, is the original sin† of man. Until it is dealt a 
death blow, its numerous offsprings – the other faults – lying 
dormant in it but manifesting themselves in gross forms at 
times propitious to each of them will not be decimated at a 

*	 Māṇikkavāchakar, Tiruvāchakam, Tirucchatakam, v. 46.
†	 It is to be known that what is referred to as the “original sin” 
in the Bible is nothing but the awareness of the ego that is body-
based.



42 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

single stroke. The singular ‘fault (kuṯṟam)’ encompasses all 
faults due to their oneness of genre. 

Faults though be very many all fall under three broad 
categories namely kama (desire), vehuḷi (anger) and 
mayakkam (delusion). What Valluva Nayanar proclaims is 
worth recalling here:

Kāmam vehuḷi mayak-kam ivai-muṉḏṟaṉ
Nāmam keḍak-keḍu nōy.*
All suffering ceaseth for a man when he hath 
conquered utterly desire, anger and delusion.
Virtue (guṇam) stands for the virtuous. It is the ego, 

indeed, that houses all faults and conversely egolessness is 
the citadel of all virtues. An egoless man is verily a virtuous 
one. Such a one possessed of the opulence of all auspicious 
virtues is truly a ripe and competent aspirant who delights the 
Heart of the Lord, becomes the worthy recipient of His Grace 
and a true devotee of the Lord. Hence the submission “deign 
to make me virtuous (guṇamāyp paṇittāḷ).” In addition to 
the hoary tradition of looking upon the Guru, in the ultimate 
sense, as that very form of formless Transcendental Sivam, 
even in the gross plane of wordly transactions Bhagavan had 
no guru other than Arunachala in a physical human frame 
not even in dreams, unlike others. This truth he recounts and 
hails the magnificence of His Grace, “Thou who shineth in 
the form of the Guru, (guru uruvāy oḷir Aruṇāchalā)”, living 
heart and soul in the content of that line, with love flooding 
his heart and speech.

*	 Tirukkuṟaḷ, v. 360.



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 43

A true ‘Guru’ is one who has sundered totally the delusion 
of the mutated individuality of the ego – the one obstacle to 
abiding as the Self. A true ‘guru’ is one who has attained the 
natural experience and ever shines within as mere non-dual, 
Being-Awareness – the Self in the Heart as Pure Knowledge, 
unbroken and Impartite, the Transcendental Sivam, the 
whole and Prime Content. The other gurus, bereft of this 
experience, whatever may be the grandeur of excellence to 
their credit acquired on account of other virtues, are gurus 
only in name and addressed so merely as a form of courtesy 
and are not gurus in the true sense of the word.

The very trait of the fragmented individuality – the 
selfhood – remaining intact and not decimated brands them 
as “hollow or light (laghus)” without essence.

Here Muruganar, the poet par excellence with a fine 
sense of subtle humour plays a pun on the word “Guru” 
which means both a “preceptor” and “heavy” and “laghu” 
the opposite of guru (meaning trivial, poor or light) is used 
here with reference to the so called gurus bereft of this 
experience.
A.I.F.A:

Arunachala who shineth as the Preceptor! Pray command 
me to annihilate all my faults and fill myself with virtues 
and graciously govern me.

The faults are namely desire, anger and delusion. 
“Destroying them totally (muṯṯṟum aṟuttu)”: Eliminate them 
wholesale so that they go out of reckoning along with their 
names with which the misery of embodiment disappears too.

“Make me virtuous (guṇamāy)” refers to all the sterling 
qualities acquired through earnest endeavours that stand 



44 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

in good stead for an aspirant or a sadhak. The Lord, aware 
of the ripeness or maturity of the beings, to make them an 
heir to His all-embracing Grace, puts on a human vestment, 
true to the old adage of “catching a deer by setting a deer”, 
assumes the role of a preceptor. Bhagavan begs Arunachala 
to “Shine on as the preceptor (guru uruvāy oḷir Aruṇāchalā).” 
The frame of a guru – the living shrine – is the condensed 
mass of Sat Chit Ananda, Being-Awareness-Bliss.

20.	 Kūrvāṭ kaṇṇi-yar koḍu-maiyiṟ paḍā-daruḷ
		  Kūrnd-eṉaic chērnd-aruḷ Aruṇāchalā

Substance: 
Bestow Thy benign and abundant compassion and 

graciously commingle with me in non-dual union so that I 
may not be ensnared in the scheming stratagem of wicked 
people with sword sharp, closely knit eyes armed with a 
net spread vast.
Commentary: 

“Sword Sharp (kūr vāḷ)” stands for gruesome disposition 
and cruel deeds and “those with eyes (kaṇṇi)” signifies “one 
who possesses a honeyed tongue”. ‘Kaṇṇi’ also implies ‘a 
net’ as it is made of numerous eye-lets (kaṇ). “Those with 
sword sharp glances (kūr vāḷ kaṇṇiyar)” points to such people 
of the world who, with the cruel disposition of the deft-
handed fowlers spread their nets wide from which escape is 
impossible, attract people towards them by various deceitful 
stratagems and hold them captive. Then, unafraid of slander 
or sin and caring two hoots for social norms, torment them 
for selfish ends as if to spirit their lives away. Bhagavan by 



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 45

this alludes to the cruel-hearted worldlings who follow the 
ways of the world for selfish ends – mere animals in the garb 
of men – who are a law unto themselves and swear by what is 
merely perceived and which alone constitutes the whole truth 
for them. Hence he beseeches Lord Arunachala to release 
him from co-horting with such wicked people and claim him 
espousing the sentiment of Rukmini who poignantly prayed 
to Lord Krishna pleading succour and release from the 
clutches of the conspiring wicked men like Sisupala and to 
claim her. There are some who interpret the import to mean, 
“Guard me from being enslaved by the scheming and wicked 
women possessed of intentions that are sword sharp and vile.” 
Though this cannot be deemed as incorrect as an occasional 
occurrence pertaining to one gender in a Text full of sayings 
common to both genders, it runs counter to the entire tenor of 
the Text that is couched in bridal imagery. 

In this scheme all along whatever has been said is 
addressed by a lady-love to her beloved. Abruptly to 
interpret it as an address from a youth is untenable. But it 
may be accepted, if it is not deemed as a flaw.
A.I.F.A:

This special commentary dwells at length on the ideas 
expatiated to Sri Muruganar by Dr. Srinivasa Rao, a devotee 
of Bhagavan. 

In the ultimate sense the personal soul in relation to God 
is always feminine and passive and this is the everlasting 
truth of mysticism in religion. Hence in the whole universe 
there is but one purusha – the Sole Man – the Lord – and 
the rest are all His maids aspiring to ‘wed’ Him and be in 
Supreme Bliss. 



46 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

“I, a woman by my natural quality (prākrita swabhava) 
and Thou, by Thy spiritual nature, being the Sole Purusha 
transcending nature (aprākrita swabhava), instead of 
attaining Bliss Supreme in Thy embrace, due to inadvertence, 
forgetting my passive and feminine nature (merely holding 
on to the exterior of the body) perversely regarded myself 
as a man. In the human species all jivas are but women. 
Women like me, labouring under the self-imposed deception 
behave like ones who enact a play for amusement assuming 
various roles of men and women of the ‘dramatis personae’. 
But even after the play is over they fail to revert back to 
their earlier and natural state but carried away by the roles 
assumed earlier continue to embrace each other and thus 
become the laughing stock of good and knowledgeable 
people.” “Since I, unlike the others of this story, do not 
want to be the butt of ridicule, pray, save me” is taken as the 
content of “snares of those cruel and sword sharp intentions 
(kaṇṇiyar koḍumaiyil paḍādaruḷ).” 

What was offered as another reading from a cursory 
view and appeared untenable, becomes tenable from this 
adhyatmic view.

1) The cruel nature of the wicked ‘maya’ that lends 
a helping hand to “prakriti” (nature) as a way of formal 
courtesy was superimposed on women. The true, foremost 
‘purusha’ (who is filled with all the traits of ‘sat purusha’ 
in full measure) is the I, the true Subject who shines as the 
One, in the heart where the ego-self, the body bound, is 
sundered with the second and third persons (you, he, she 
and it) ended. The heart of those having accomplished the 
loss of the ego, has become the form of That (TAT) the jiva, 
self-sundered shines in all the splendour as Siva, bereft of 



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 47

bondage. There, by virtue of the dance of ‘I-I’ as the Pure 
Self, the Supreme Atman is but Arunachala, the sole One 
who announces loud and clear “the content of Four Vedas 
am I.”

2) Stretching the esoteric sense a little further the lady 
love complains that since the pangs of love and separation 
that she suffers have not been the lot of others they without 
the milk of human kindness indulge in heartless derision 
that are harsh and grating on the ears. Hence “pray, protect 
me that I may not be subject to such a cruel treatment”. Like 
rubbing salt on a wound, a dejected heart that is already on 
fire with pangs of separation, pierced by these arrows of 
harsh words torment her endlessly. She calls those women 
“Women of sword sharp eyes (kūrvāḷ kaṇṇiyar)”.

The reason for the reference to their eyes here is to 
highlight their ghostly nature of deriving sadistic pleasure 
from the grief of others, totally bereft of the least natural 
kindness the only jewel for the eyes and their despicable 
habit of causing causeless grief to others.

Valluvar speaks of this
Tuṉ-ṉiyār kuṯ-ṯṟamum tūṯ-ṯṟum mara-biṉār 
Eṉṉai-kol ēdilār māṭṭu.*
Those who love to speak abroad the transgressions of 
their friends, how will they spare the transgressions 
of their enemies?
Here the similarity of the eyes to the sword is not to the 

shape but to the function of the sword to hurt and harm. 

*	 Tirukkuṟaḷ, v. 188.



48 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

But hereby lies an exhortation to the womankind in 
general. Women regarding other women amidst them as 
themselves and instead of deprecating them must treat them 
all with kindness and understanding.

Piṟaṉ-pazhi kūṟu-vāṉ taṉ-pazhi yuḷ-ḷum 
Tiṟaṉ-terindu kūṟap paḍum.*
One must bear in heart that if you slander another he 
will look into your own transgressions and expose 
the worst of them.

21.	 Keñji-yum vañji-yāik koñja-mum iraṅ-gilai
		  Añjal-eṉḏṟē aruḷ Aruṇāchalā

Substance: 
Despite my poignant and piteous pleas that would soften 

and move even a stone, Thou like a hard-hearted, deceitful 
individual, remaineth unmoved without extending to me 
even the least mercy. (At least now, without any further 
delay please bestir). Let Thy stone-heart soften and melt 
and graciously assure me Thy refuge saying, “Fear not”.
Commentary: 

The term ‘vañji’ denotes a cheat, a dupe or a fraud. 
Though “like a deceitful person (vañji pōl)” is the meaning 
of that, instead of accusing Him so, to be in tune with “Thou 
remaineth unmoved (koñjamum iraṅgilai)” of the second 
line he employs the term “do not deceive me (vañjiyāy)” 
with great literary finesse. Though, in truth, Arunachala is 

*	 Tirukkuṟaḷ, v. 186.



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 49

the Ocean of Grace, outwardly He appears to be a deceitful 
person, not condescending to exhibit even a trace of mercy 
on those devotees who pine and melt away with love and 
longing only making them weary and worn out instead 
with a bruised and contrite heart. This is only to make them 
thaw and melt more and more quivering in every fibre so 
that they “go through the divine process of ‘wasting’” (in 
the words of Maṇivāchakar), till the jiva is purged of all 
its dross and becomes Siva and there is nothing in between 
the twain.

This is what the learned ones eulogise and address as the 
“Compassion of a Hero” or “Heroic compassion” the raison 
de etre of which is to uplift the jiva and not to deceive him.”
A.I.F.A.:

While he upbraids Arunachala with a bruised heart here, 
in verse 78 he berates himself as one “Who is of the lowly 
nature of abject cowering in the face of overpowering might 
(miñjiḍiṟ keñjiḍum koñja aṟivaṉ).” This brings to the fore 
the fairness of a sensitive, balanced mind.

22.	 Kēḷā daḷikkum-uṉ kēḍil puga-zhaik 
		  Kēḍu-cei yādaruḷ Aruṇāchalā

Substance: 
Thou hast earned an everlasting fame by Thy 

munificent nature of conferring grace on devotees, though 
unasked, just by divining their intention or need. Let that 
not be tarnished on my account alone as the one who 
grants not even when asked for, thus belying Thy nature. 
Pray, shower Thy Grace on me with solicitude.



50 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

Commentary: 
The Lord, ever abiding at the source of the mind, as the 

Witness, divines in advance what will transpire eventually as 
desires in the hearts of the devotees. And with more than the 
gracious solicitude of one’s own mother, He bestows it all on 
them, thus earning for Himself a fame that is an everlasting 
and a never fading one. Hence it is hailed as “the eternal 
fame as the Giver of gifts unasked (kēḷādu aḷikkum uṉ kēḍil 
pugazh).” Amongst such devotees, if for some reason were 
He to withhold His Grace only towards a few like him, that 
deed, indeed, becoming well known like the blemish of the 
black spot on the moon may well undo His fame. Therefore 
he requests Him “tarnish not Thy Fame, (kēḍu seyyādu aruḷ)”. 
The same anxiety and anguish gets echoed in Padikam too 
when he pleads, “Were I to give up my life at Thy Lotus Feet, 
the ignominy that Thou invitest upon Thyself would become 
a planted column of disgrace (uṉḏṟa ṉaṟpadappōdil nāṉ uyir 
viṭṭāl naṭṭa tūṇāhum uṉ pazhiyē).”

Juxtaposed to Thy inherently munificent nature of 
prompt bestowal of benign grace uniformly on all, wert Thou 
to withhold Thy Grace to anyone amongst us, the undefiled 
and eternal Fame that has been Thine so far not only runs 
the risk of being tarnished but also will invite ignominy on 
Thee by the people of the world at large. He has forewarned 
Arunachala of this in an earlier verse as well as “If Thou 
wert to desert me with indifference the entire world will 
reproach Thee (ahaṯṯṟiḍil akilam pazhittiḍum)”.

Hence, ‘at least to safeguard Thy fair name and to 
absolve Thyself from such an ignominy, Thou must make 
me an heir to Thy Grace’, is the drift of thought. It may be 
worth recalling here what Bhagavan says in verse 5 “Escape 
from this blame (ippazhi tappu)”.



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 51

A.I.F.A. :
Arunachala! Let not the undying Fame Thou hast earned 

by Thy nature of granting gifts unsolicited be sullied and 
lost. Pray bestow Grace on me. 

It is well known that the devout desire desirelessness 
and never seek any other. More than the mother who, by 
intention and observation anticipates the needs of her own 
begotten children who are sheltered under her loving care 
and provides them whatever they require at proper times, 
the Lord too, aware on His own of the wants of the devotees 
who, having abandoned themselves at the Feet of the Lord 
and abide, provides timely fulfilment of their needs. This is 
brought to the fore by the phrase “giving without the desires 
being voiced (kēḷādu aḷikkum)”. 

That such a reputation will be spoilt forever and anon if 
He fails to respond even when sought for, is voiced in “kēḍu 
ceyyādu aruḷ”. This has been reflected earlier in verse 6: 
“Far kinder and more gracious than a mother who begets and 
rears her children (īṉḏṟiḍum aṉṉaiyiṟ peridaruḷ purivōy)”.

It is worth focusing our attention on verse 6 of ‘Kuzhaitta 
Pattu’ of Tiruvāchakam

Vēṇḍat takkadu aṟivōy nī! vēṇḍa, muzhudum 
taruvōy nī!

Vēṇḍum ayaṉ, māṟku, ariyōy nī! vēṇḍi, eṉṉaip paṇi 
koṇḍāy;

Vēṇḍi, nī yādu aruḷ seydāy, yāṉum, aduvē vēṇḍiṉ 
allāl,

Vēṇḍum parisu oṉḏṟu uṇḍu eṉṉil, aduvum, uṉ taṉ 
viruppu aṉḏṟē?*

*	 Māṇikkavāchakar, Tiruvāchakam, 33-Kuzhaitta Pattu, v. 6.



52 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

Thou knowest what I should pray for;
Thou wilt wholly Grant me what I pray for.
Unto Vishnu and Brahma That seek Thee,
Thou art hard of access.
Thou willingly made me serve Thee.
Whatever Thou willingly blesseth me with,
I desire nought but that.
If yet,
I seek aught,
Is not that indeed Thy own desire?
In the commentary “that which was not given even when 

asked for” was interpreted as Grace. If it be that devotees 
desired something, it is best to construe that they desired 
only desirelessness. If perchance a few did desire some 
worldly thing, it is, as if, even that urge was prompted by 
His desire and will, for they are but Him.

23.	 Kaiyi-ṉiṟ kaṉi-yuṉ meyrasaṅ koṇḍuva
		  Hai-veṟi koḷa-varuḷ Aruṇāchalā

Substance: 
Shower Thy Grace on me so that I may drink the 

nectar of Thy quintessential Form – the sweet grapes that 
have befallen on my palm – and remain inebriated in Thy 
rapturous Bliss.
Commentary: 

The expression “gooseberry held on one’s palm” 
signifies the certainty of possession and clarity of perception. 
The expression “grapes on the palm” implies, in addition, 



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 53

the nature of answering the palate faster, easier and with 
greater sweetness, but without the astringency specifically 
associated with gooseberry. Further, it is an elixir ideally 
suited to drown one in the intoxication of joy that is prayed 
for here and hence the choice of grapes over the traditional 
gooseberry, it being ideal. 

It is true gooseberry is a rare plant and it gains 
additional renown by the fact of its (the black variety of it) 
being offered to the lady Saint Auvaiyar by the celebrated 
Chieftain Adhiyaman. 

Bhagavan having been already claimed by Arunachala, 
and enfolded within His Grace, has experienced the 
resultant Bliss of aparoksha anubhuti – the non-mediated 
experience of the Self and bespeaks of it as “fruit on hand”. 
Maṇivāchakar, the sovereign of speech, recounting the joy 
of Grace, hails the Lord who claimed him as the “One, who 
to me became the gooseberry fruit in the broad hand of 
mine” and goes on to say, 

Maiyi laṅgu-nal kaṇṇi paṅgaṉē
Vandu eṉaip-paṇi koṇḍa piṉmazhak
Kaiyil aṅgu-poṉ kiṇṇam eṉḏṟu-alāl
Ariyai yeṉḏṟu-uṉaik karudu kiṉḏṟilēṉ.*
One half of Her with eyes of glistening jet,
Thou cam’st and mad’st me Thine with tender hand
As feeding me from the golden cup since when
Hard of access I deem Thee never more.
Thus he bespeaks of the Lord both ‘as the fruit’ and ‘the 

receptacle of the hand’.

*	 Māṇikkavāchakar, Tiruvāchakam, Tirucchatakam, v. 92.



54 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

Even the king of speech, Navukkarasar personifies the 
Lord as the juicy fruit, due to His Quintessential Bliss.

Maṉidarkāḷ iṅgē-vam oṉḏṟu sollukēṉ
Kaṉi tandāl-kaṉi uṇṇavum vallirē
Puṉidaṉ poṟ-kazhal īsaṉ eṉum-kaṉi
Iṉidu sālavum ēsaṯṟa var-kaṭkē.*
Oh ye mortals! Here will I say
Ye good at tasting fruit if given!
The fruit of the holy Lord with Golden Feet
Is the Supreme to the faultless.
This madness is verily what is prayed for by our own 

Bhagavan too as “Bestow Grace that I, drinking the nectar 
of Thy Real Self, may remain inebriated with joy (uṉ 
meyrasam koṇḍu uvahai veṟi koḷa varuḷ).”

This madness of Supreme Bliss is compared by 
Thāyumāṉavar to the inebriation caused by intoxicants:

Adu-eṉḏṟāl edu-eṉavoṉ ḏṟaḍukkuñ caṅgai
Ādaliṉāl adu-eṉalum aṟavē viṭṭu
Madu-uṇḍa vaṇḍeṉa-vuñ caṉakaṉ ādi
Maṉṉavarkaḷ Sukar-mudalōr vāzhndār.†
‘If we call it “That”, then the question will arise, 
“What is That?” 
Therefore did Janaka and the other kings 
And the rishis, foremost among whom is Suka, 
Live happily, like bees intoxicated with honey, 
Entirely avoiding any mention of “That”

*	 Appar Thēvāram, 5th Tirumuṟai, Tirukkuṟuntohai, Podhu.
†	 Thāyumāṉavar, 14-Ākārabhuvaṉam – Chidambara Rahasyam, 
v. 23



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 55

Remained in this state.’
This indeed, is the state of Self, ultimate jnana 
Silence unknown to the ego-mind.
This experience is extolled in sheer wonder and ecstasy 

by Maṇivāchakar as follows:
Eṉai-nāṉ-eṉbadu aṟiyēṉ-pahal iravu-āvadum aṟiyēṉ 
Maṉa-vāchakam kaḍandāṉ eṉai matta-uṉmattaṉ-ākki
Ciṉa-māl-viḍai uḍaiyāṉ maṉṉu Tirup-peruntuṟai 

uṟaiyum
Paṉavaṉ eṉaic ceyda-paḍiṟu aṟiyēṉ param cuḍarē.* 
I know myself no more; Nor day’s nor night’s 
recurrence; He
Who, mind and speech transcends, with mystic bliss 
maddened me;
He has mighty Vishnu as His steed, O Lord of Perunthurai
The Brahman played on me tricks, I know not 
Oh! Beam Transcendent!
Self-relish is the highest Bliss. Arunachala’s bestowal 

of Grace in order that he may consume and taste with relish 
the fruit that has befallen into his hand and revel in the 
rapturous Bliss is dwelt upon here. He, Arunachala, is the 
‘fruit’ of the joy of Awareness and the Bliss of Being as the 
form of his own Self. Yet lest he should, out of inadvertence 
and mental bewilderment, part from Him, He, by shining 
forever at the source of his mind, in his Heart, without the 
night of forgetting and the dawn of rememberance, in the 
firmament of Awareness must make him blissfully think of 
the ‘Self as the Self’ unceasingly. 

*	 Tiruvāchakam, 34-Uyiruṇṇip Pattu, v. 3.



56 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

A.I.F.A.:
Arunachala! Bestow Thy Grace so that, I, feeding 

on the Bliss of Thy Form – the gooseberry fruit that has 
befallen onto my palm – and due to the overflowing bliss of 
inebriation, may possess the madness of joy for Thee.

“The nectar of Thy Real Self (mei rasam)”: The 
spring of Bliss occurring at the touch of Truth with 
Truth (or Brahma Samsparsam or the Union of the Self, 
the Atman with Brahman – Arunachala). This verse 
has an esoteric content indicating the physical pleasure 
indulged in by the lady-love and her beloved in mutual 
embrace.

– āsaikoṇḍu, 
navamākap para-puruḍaṉ taṉṉaik kūḍi 
nayanda sukham aṉubhavitta nāri neñjam, 
tavamāṉa maṉait-tozhilgaḷ ceyyum pōdum
tazhuvi aṉubhavitta sukham taṉai viḍādē*
If the statement “the whole period of activity is 
also the state of peace”, is objected to by saying 
“Does not action demand a changing mind 
resulting in the slipping away of Peace, hear this 
illustration: The state of the sage is like that of a 
lady who never ceases to thrill with love for her 
paramour, even while she attends to her duties at 
home.”

*	 Kaivalya Navaneetham, Doubts cleared Away, v.123.



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 57

24.	 Koḍi-yiṭ ṭaḍi-yaraik kollu-ṉaik kaṭṭik
		  Koṇ-ḍeṅgaṉ vāzhvēṉ Aruṇāchalā

Substance: 
Thou hast hoisted and unfurled Thy flag, fluttering 

high, firmly intent on Thy vow of dealing death unto Thy 
votaries without killing them. Relating myself to Thee 
(as my beloved), who ever remaineth wedded to Thy 
true resolve, how will I survive henceforth (as a jiva like 
others without dying)?
Commentary: 

“Determined to deal death unto the devotees (aḍiyaraik 
kolludal)” is to deal death to their illusory or fabricated 
jivahood (ego). That indeed, in itself, is an act of Grace of 
the Lord for thereby He bestows Sivatvam, Eternity. (There 
is no ‘death’ meted out here. The sole and simple Truth of 
beings is the Transcendent Non-dual Truth of Arunachala – 
the Supreme Self that can neither kill nor be killed).

“Accepting Thee as the Lord (kaṭṭik koṇḍu)” – is an 
usage in common parlance meaning “wedded to Thee”. In 
one sense it means meditating intensely and dwelling in the 
thought of Him unceasingly and abiding in Him. In another 
sense it stands for “embracing” or “marrying” Him. Hence 
there is a play of words in this usage. The fate of devotees 
claimed by Arunachala is very much akin to that of the 
sugar cane in the elephant’s mouth or the deer caught in the 
mouth of the tiger. Arunachala Siva intermingles with His 
devout servitors, enters into them and makes them Himself. 
They are transformed totally into the Self by Him, the non-
dual Self, and are consumed and digested by Him. They can 



58 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

never revert back to the old ways of living with ‘jivatva’, the 
awareness of the individuality of ‘I’, as the ‘other’ of Him. 
This can be compared with verses 1 and 48.

Aruṇāchalam eṉa ahamē niṉaip-pavar
	 Ahattai-vēr aṟuppāy Aruṇāchalā
Thou dost root out and annihilate the ego of those 
who meditate on Thee in their hearts.

Deyvam-eṉḏṟu uṉṉaic cāravē eṉṉaic 
	 Cēra ozhittāy Aruṇāchalā
When I surrendered unto Thee as my God and 
saviour Thou didst destroy me altogether.
Belying the fond hope – and contrary to the intent of the 

devotees who come to Thee with the desire for advancement 
and upliftment – Thou, flying Thy flag high, really killest 
them. Uniting myself in wedlock with such a cruel and 
heartless man as a husband how will I live like other women 
in the empirical world? This is a complaint of the lady-love 
lodged to her beloved with a tinge of eroticism.
A.I.F.A.:

Arunachala! Please tell me, how will I survive accepting 
as my ruler with stern dispassion Thee who with Thy self-
imposed task of ‘killing’ Thy devotees as Thy chosen 
profession raisest Thy flag high to achieve that purpose.

“To unfurl and raise Thy flag high (koḍiyiṭṭu)” is again 
a common usage betokening a firm and unshaken resolve to 
accomplish something.

“Determined to deal death unto the devotees (aḍiyaraik 
kollum)” signifies the extinction of the adjunct or upadhi of 
the selfhood, ego or the jivatva.



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 59

“Accepting Thee as my Lord (kaṭṭikkoṇḍu)”. If the Lord 
is held on to unceasingly without letting Him go, one shines 
as Siva but with the loss of the ego. “If so how will I survive” 
is the poser. This is in a lighter vein evoking in the listeners, 
who hear this truth, a sense of novelty and wonderment.

25.	 Kōpa-mil guṇat-tōy kuṟiyā yeṉaik-koḷak
			   Kuṟai-yeṉ seidēṉ Aruṇāchalā

Substance: 
Oh, the one of unruffled Peace devoid of anger! Thou 

hast singled me (among the many devotees specially) as 
the target of Thy Grace. What austerities (tapas) have I 
performed in the bygone births of mine?
Commentary: 

The term “kuṟai” though means literally ‘inadequacy’, 
‘want’ or ‘shortcoming’, here connoting ‘wanting in tapas’ 
or ‘tapas left incomplete’, stands for ‘tapas’. To “make me 
Thy target what merit have I done (kuṟiyāy eṉaik koḷak 
kuṟaiyeṉ ceydēṉ)?” may also be interpreted to provide a 
different import too. “Thou who art devoid of anger towards 
any devotee, contrary to Thy nature, why hast Thou made 
me the target of Thy anger and for what crime committed by 
me to deserve this?”

In this sense, it is in tune with the utterance of Saint 
Sundarar “pizhaippa ṉāhilum tiruvaḍi pizhaiyēṉ”: though 
I may commit mistakes in other things, I have never 
committed any to Thy holy feet.

Bhagavan queries, “What wrong have I indulged in 
(kuṟai eṉ ceydēṉ)?” He further elaborates on this in verse 3 
of Padikam too:



60 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

Iṟai-uṉai niṉai-yum eṇṇamē naṇṇā
	 eṉai-yuṉadu aruḷ-kayiṯṯṟāl īrttu
Iṟai-uyir iṉḏṟik koṉḏṟiḍa niṉḏṟāi
	 eṉkuṟai iyaṯ-ṯṟiṉaṉ ēzhai?
Arunachala! Never had I the faintest idea of 
contemplating on Thee, yet Thou on Thy own, by 
the cord of grace, binding and pulling me by force 
to Thee, standeth resolved to kill me. What offence, 
the poor wretch that I am, have committed?
In “to mark me as the target (kuṟiyāy eṉaik koḷa)” – the 

target of anger is not the Atman, the Transcendent Truth – 
Sat Chit Ananda – but the trivial, troublesome and deceitful 
phantom ego appearing in the darkness of nescience as the 
body-based concept of I and bustling as the transactional 
jivahood – the root cause of all maladies – which being 
inimical to Reality conceals one’s true form of Being 
Awareness Bliss. This is in fact extolling Arunachala by way 
of upbraiding Him – another figure of speech – that enriches 
the content. Thus Bhagavan asserts with certainty, that taken 
either way, the prowess of Arunachala’s Feet of Grace in 
accomplishing His mission of imparting Grace has become 
a fait accompli in his case, for he now stands adorned with 
Grace and illumined with enlightenment.
A.I.F.A.: 

“O! The virtuous One! Thou careth not to mark me in 
Thy Heart to claim and govern me! What wrong have I 
done to Thee that Thou shouldst turn Thy Face away?” is 
yet another reading.



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 61

26.	 Gautamar pōṯ-ṯṟum karu-ṇaimā malaiyē
		  Kaḍaik-kaṇit tāḷvāy Aruṇāchalā

Substance: 
Oh, The mighty Hill! The embodiment of compassion, 

hailed and extolled by Sage Gautama! (Just as Thou happily 
accepted the hymns of adoration of Sage Gautama and 
responded by bestowing auspicious Grace on him, Pray, 
lend Thy blessed ears to mine as well so that, claimed 
by Thee, I may cling to Thy Holy Feet). Adorn me with 
Thy Gracious glance of Grace and gather me under Thy 
governance.
Commentary: 

“The Hill, hailed by Gautama (Gautamar pōṯṯṟum)”: 
(The great sage of the most austere penance who guided the 
universal Mother, Uma, to gain the left half of Siva, by the 
stratagem of penance) highlights the splendour and greatness 
of Arunachala and the epithet, “the mighty Hill, the Mountain 
of compassion (karuṇai mā malaiyē)” expresses explicitly the 
innate munificence of Arunachala in the bestowal of soul-
subduing grace on the devout who take Him as the haven of 
refuge. The glory of this liberality of Arunachala is recounted 
by Saint Maṇivāchakar in “Kīrttit Tiruvahaval” where he 
hails Arunachala as the Great Hill of Grace

Iruḷ kaḍindu-aruḷiya iṉba ūrdi
Aruḷiya perumai aruṇmalai āhavum
His vehicle, a gift of joy, dispels the darkness;
His is the Mount of grace that greatness gives
Fitted to each one’s lofty nature, each one’s power.



62 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

“Graciously hold sway over me (āḷvāy)” The request is 
to envelop him as a subject, self-sundered, and in unfading 
bliss under the Reign of His divine Feet, the sway of 
Sivabodham and to remain irradiated with Sivan’s light. 
Since this cannot happen but for the merciful beam of the 
glance of Grace of the Lord, the “Ruler”, he pursues his 
request for Grace as “Bestow Thy side-long glance of Grace 
(kaḍaikkaṇittu āḷvāy)”. 
A.I.F.A:

Arunachala, The Mountain Great, The embodiment of 
Compassion! Graciously bestow Thy sidelong glance of 
Grace and govern me!

Sage Gautama*, one of the foremost amongst the 
eminent rishis of yore, resided at Thiruvannamalai 
performing penance. When the Universal Mother approached 
him, he guided Her on the mode of penance to be observed 
to become non-dually one with the Lord. Bhagavan, by 
using the phrase “praised and prayed to by sage Gautama 
(Gautamar pōṯṯṟum)”, underscores specifically the idea that 
the Sage adored in reverence the “Unmoving” state of the 
Lord here where with Sakthi withdrawn into His Unmoving 
Form, He soars high in the form of a Hill in all might and 
splendour unlike in the sacred Hall of Tillai, where, coming 

*	 Sage Gautama, of great renown is the presiding sage of 
Thiruchuzhi, the birth place of Bhagavan as well. He is also the 
patron Saint of Arunachala, the permanent abode of Bhagavan. 
Thus, right from the hoary puranic days there have been many 
divine features common to both, thus proving the truth of the 
strange but wonderful divine affinity between them.



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 63

under the sway of the Devi, He enacted the Dance of ecstatic 
Bliss.

While in an earlier verse (17) of this Text Bhagavan 
addresses Arunachala as “the Ocean of Compassion (kirupaik 
kaḍalē)”, here he uses the sobriquet “the mighty Hill of 
Compassion (karuṇaimā malaiyē).” The term “Gautama” is 
a generic one, embracing men of great erudition and men 
resplendent with the lustre of enlightenment. The truth 
that what others perceive as a mere frame of rocks and 
stones, was seen as the very embodiment of compassion by 
Bhagavan, is amply made clear by his address as the “Hill 
of compassion.” ‘Compassion’ as a term referring to Devi 
or Parasakthi is often come across in spiritual lore. The 
import of the plea “turn Thy Glance of Grace on me and 
govern me (kaḍaikkaṇittu āḷvāy)” is to enfold him into 
His Form and dissolve him as His very Form even as He 
did unto Devi.

Arunachala, lauded by Sage Gautama, has the destinction 
of having sage Bringi too as the presiding Sage.

27.	 Sakala-mum vizhuṅ-gum kadiroḷi yiṉa-maṉa 
		  Jalaja-malart-tiyi ḍaruṇāchalā

Substance: 
Arunachala, The Sun of resplendent Awareness art 

Thou! Thou by Thy hands of effulgent beams of Grace 
and wisdom, gather, gobble up and assimilate as Thyself 
the entire phantom Universe teeming with throngs of 
shadowy dualities, all of which pose as real and true 
to the jiva, the chidabhasa, just a reflected awareness. 



64 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

My heart lotus stands folded up like a bud inhabited in 
full by the dense darkness of ignorance with the latent 
impressions of ego, desire-bonds and the like. Graciously 
make it unfold and bloom forth in full (with the embedded 
darkness dispelled without the slightest trace) in all lustre 
(as the infinite expanse of the Supreme).
Commentary: 

As Arunachala is spoken of as “the Sun”, “all 
(sakalamum)” that is swallowed up in entirety by Him is 
the mere appearance of the shadowy spectre follows, though 
left unsaid.

The little fault of inadvertence of forgetting one’s 
own Supreme Truth that ever shines as the substratum 
of everything lends life and breath to the dualities. They 
masquerade as ‘real’, only in the empirical world which is 
the offspring of the illusory maya. The jiva regards them 
as ‘real’ due to the awareness of adjuncts and he deludes 
himself due to the blunder of inadvertence by misconstruing 
him as a finite part of the whole Infinity as one among the 
teeming multiplicities that are transient. They will not appear 
real to him, if he, with his body-based conception of I, does 
not share the truth of ‘equal existence’ (or non-existence) 
with them (for the one enjoys only as much reality as the 
other). (Permance is a criterion of Reality).

In the presence of Arunachala, the form of Pure 
Awareness, the illusory creation of the world of samsara, 
in its entirety, projected by maya has no being and is totally 
non-existent in the absolute sense. But with due deference 
to the ways of the worldly men, who, out of ignorance, 
take them to ‘exist’, it has been described as “gathered and 



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 65

swallowed”, falling in line with the accepted poetic tradition 
and liberty just as an embellishment. Here Arunachala, who 
dispels the darkness of nescience that conceals one’s Absolute 
Truth and the dream-like trivial samsara that appears in that 
sleep of nescience, is compared to the Sun. Extending the 
comparison and in tune with it, the spontaneous unfoldment 
of lotus in the mere power of the presence of the sun, is 
compared to the Heart lotus and the sundering of the ego-
knot or Hridaya granti, that he prays for, for the blossoming 
in full of the Heart lotus. The lotus bud is the ego-knot or 
Hridaya granti or the bound heart and the full-blown lotus 
is the heart, freed of its bondage. 

“Release from bondage” is the attrition of grievous 
vasanas of many a fated birth that the jiva is enmeshed in, 
which is the extinction of the mind and the sundering of the 
knot. A verse in Pancharatnam composed by Bhagavan also 
states the very same truth of the Reality being worldless and 
non-dual.

Aruḷ-niṟai vāṉa amudak kaḍalē
Viri-kadi-rāl yāvum vizhuṅ-gum – Aruṇa
Giri-paramāṉ māvē kiḷar-uḷappū naṉḏṟāy
Viri-paridi yāha viḷaṅgu
Ocean of nectar, full of Grace, engulfing the universe 
in Thy splendour! O Arunachala, the Supreme Self! 
Be Thou the Sun and open the lotus of my heart in 
bliss.

A.I.F.A:
In verses 27 and 29 Bhagavan personifies Arunachala 

as the Sun and Moon respectively. While in the former he 



66 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

implores Arunachala – the Sun – to make the lotus of his 
heart blossomforth, in the latter he beseeches the Lord – the 
Moon – to unseal the mouth of the lily of his heart filled 
with nectar. 

28.	 Sāppā ḍuṉ-ṉai chārn-duṇa vāyāṉ 
		  Sānta-māyp pōvaṉ Aruṇāchalā

Substance: 
Considering Thee as the food for me (and with the 

intent to eat Thee up) I resorted to Thee. But, in lieu of it, 
I becoming Thy food (consumed and digested by Thee as 
Thy very form) am naught.
Commentary: 

The term “food (sāppāḍu)” also means “experience”. 
‘Sāntam’ reveals something with an end (sa+antam). 
The ego, becoming food for Arunachala, the very Self, is 
rendered naught by being assimilated as the form of the 
Self – mere Being. That end of the journey of the aggressive 
and alienating ego is the final quietude of mukti. The 
phrase, “sāntamāyp pōvaṉ,” may be taken to mean, “I will 
be enveloped in total peace.” Thus it is made clear that the 
experience of Siva or Sivanubhava is the destruction of 
jivatva. The attainment of Sivanubhava can at best, be only 
a pipedream, if the ego remains even in its miniscule form. 
All spiritual Texts proclaim uniformly and conclusively – 
and in one voice – that giving up the ego as food for the 
Lord (pouring the mind as food for Siva into the sacred fire 
of Pure Awareness) also known as self-loss, surrender and 
prapathi, is the sadhana. This is vouchsafed by Bhagavan 



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 67

at the very outset in verse 1 of this Text as “Thou dost root 
out and annihilate the ego of those who meditate on Thee in 
their hearts.”

Aruṇā chalam-eṉa ahamē niṉaip-pavar
Ahattai-vēr aṟuppāy Aruṇāchalā
The real act of Grace of the Lord is the uprooting of 

the ego of His devotees.
Vārik-koṇḍu uṉṉai vizhuṅguvaṉ kāṇil-eṉḏṟu
Ārvu-uṯṯṟa eṉṉai ozhiya-eṉṉil muṉ-ṉam
Pārittu, tāṉ-eṉṉai muṯ-ṯṟap paruhiṉāṉ,
Kār-okkum kāṭkarai appaṉ kaḍiyaṉē.*
O Lord! The moment I espied Thee an intense 
longing seized me to take Thee in both my arms 
and swallow Thee up. But Thou, preempting 
me, forestalled me and drank me to the lees. The 
Lord of Tirukkatkarai is verily the dark clouds of 
compassion raining down grace. (The devotees to 
the Lord are but food to appease His hunger and 
water to quench His thirst).
This mystic poem, bestowed by saint Nammāzhwar, 

compels men of knowledge to brood over it again and again, 
thrilling the frame to the core with ecstasy. Further this – 
almost like a carbon copy of the verse by Bhagavan – is in 
perfect identity with it.

The death that grants immortality is most intimate and 
so rare and dear. The nature of that state is delineated by 
Māṇikkavāchakar in Tiruvāchakam:

*	 Tiruvāymozhi, 9.6.10



68 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

Kayal-māṇḍa kaṇṇi-taṉ paṅgaṉ-eṉaik kalandu-
āṇḍalumē

Ayal-māṇḍu aru-viṉaic cuṯṯṟamum-māṇḍu 
avaṉiyiṉ-mēl

Mayal-māṇḍu maṯṯṟu-uḷḷa vācakam-māṇḍu 
eṉṉuḍaiya

Seyal-māṇḍa vā-pāḍit teḷḷēṇam koṭṭāmō.*
When He, Her spouse whose eyes shine bright, mixt 

with my soul,
And made me His, deeds and environments died 

out;
Upon this earth confusion died; all other mem’ries 

ceased;
How all my ‘doings’ died, sing we and beat Tellenam!
In that state everything in entirety, including the adjunct 

of his individuality, along with its seed, is totally rendered 
naught. In the mind that, with the extinction of the ego, has 
become distilled into the waves of bliss of Siva, the Form 
of Pure Awareness, not a whit of vasana can stand the 
splendour of Pure Awareness.

In the mystic verse bestowed by Nammāzhwar cited 
here as an example, the content of the line “He drank me to 
the lees (muṯṯṟap paruhiṉāṉ)” deserves deep reflection.

If, “Is there not individuality in jivanmukti?” were to be 
asked, like all the external forms perceived, it is confined to 
the perception of the on-lookers alone who are still bound. 
How is it? 

*	 Tiruvāchakam, 11-Tirutteḷḷēṇam, v. 11.



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 69

Tiruvaruḷ ceybavaṉ pōla eṉ uḷ puhundu
Uruvamum ār-uyirum uḍaṉē uṇḍāṉ*
As if to bestow grace divine
He entering into me swallowed at 
Once my life dear and frame.

Vāṉ-keṭṭu mārudam māyndu-azhal-nīr maṇ-keḍiṉum
Tāṉ-keṭṭal iṉḏṟic calippu-aṟiyāt taṉmaiyaṉukku
Ūṉ-keṭṭu uyir-keṭṭu uṇarvu-keṭṭu-eṉ uḷḷamum-pōy
Nāṉ-keṭṭa vā-pāḍit teḷḷēṇam koṭṭāmō.†
Though Ether, Wind, Fire, Water, Earth should fail,
His constant Being fails not, knows no weariness!
In Him my body, soul, and thought and mind were 

merged.
How all myself was lost, sing we, and beat Tellenam!
These two verses by Āzhvar and Maṇivāchakar speak 

explicitly of the highest Truth, that in the state of Liberation 
(jivanmukti) everything – including individuality – melts 
away and merges as Pure Awareness. That this was very 
much the experience of Bhagavan is very well known to 
those servitors in close attendance on – or proximity to – 
him who stood so instructed by observing him. It will be 
rewarding to read this verse along with verse 103 and the 
commentary on the content for them is worthy of deep 
analysis in view of the identity of thought in certain respects.

*	 Nammāzhvār, Tiruvāymozhi, 9-6-5.
†	 Tiruvāchakam, 11-Tirutteḷḷēṇam, v. 18.



70 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

29.	 Chittam kuḷirak-kadirat-tam-vait tamu-davā 
		  Yait-tiṟa arul-mati Aruṇāchalā

Substance: 
Arunachala, The Moon of Grace! That my heart, 

stricken and harassed by the triad of afflictions may, rid 
of them, become cool, graciously open with Thy hands 
of nectarine grace my heart which, brimming with nectar 
within, but remains closed like a lily bud* without. 
Commentary: 

This verse couches yet again a prayer for the sundering 
of the ego-knot as in verse 27. The only difference between 
the two is that while the ego-knot and Arunachala, who 
undoes the knot are personified as a lotus bud and the Sun 
that makes it bloom in one, in this, as a lily bud and the Moon 
that frees the flower from the bonds that keep the petals of 
its bud folded up. The Lord of nectarine form alone is fit to 
open the mouth of the pitcher of nectar tightly closed as if 
sealed with lac and stamped and release the nectar. Hence 
the invitation to Arunachala, the Moon of Grace with the 
radiance of nectarine rays.

The Pure Awareness, the awareness of Being, the 
form of “That which is”, is the import of Grace which 
is the very form and frame of Arunachala Siva. “The 

*	 oru-poruḷ āmbal arumbu-eṉa uḷḷē
	 iru-viral valattē irup-padum idayam 

– Sri Bhagavan, Supplementary to Forty Verses, v. 18
Between the two paps, below the chest, above the stomach, there 
are six organs of various colours. Of these, one, looking like a lily 
bud, is the Heart, at two digits’ distance to the right of the centre.



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 71

nectarine rays of Thy hand (kadirattam)”, “The hand of 
compassion (karuṇākaram)” (in invocatory verse) and 
“hand of Grace (aruṭ kai)” (verse 51) all mean “Grace”.
A.I.F.A:

Arunachala, the Moon of Grace! That my heart, scorched 
and anguished by the triple afflictions may become cool, 
open the mouth of my heart filled with nectar by Thy hands 
of Grace: “Keep Thy radiant hands (kadirattm vaittu)” may 
also mean: “initiating me by Thy luminous hands” and 
“open the mouth filled with nectar (amuda vāyaittiṟa)” may 
mean “open Thy divine mouth and by the rain of nectar – 
upadesa – initiate me.”

The triple afflictions are:
Adyatmikam – ills born of one’s nature
Adibautikam – miseries born of natural elements (like 

earthquakes, volcano and eruptions etc.) 
Adi Deivikam – born out of the displeasure of Gods.
Samadhi does not mean merely remaining still and 

unmoving unless it is accompanied by the ‘serene coolness’ 
that springs up from the heart within. Hence the usage “so 
that my heart and mind may become cool (chittam kuḷira)” 
and since unless the ego-knot is sundered, the coolness will 
not spring up, the phrase “open the mouth of nectar (amudha 
vāyait tiṟa)” has been employed.



72 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

30.	 Sīrai azhittu-nir vāṇamā chei-daruḷ 
		  Sīrai aḷit-taruḷ Aruṇāchalā

Substance: 
Stripping me of my robe of nescience and exposing 

my real form of total void (of Supreme Awareness), 
graciously enrobe me in Thy attire of True Awareness – 
the redeeming Grace of Enlightenment.
Commentary: 

“Robe (sīrai)” is common for both the cotton saree 
that covers the body and the five kosas or sheaths (matter 
envelopments) that hide the “sariri” (the ‘indweller’ of the 
body).

In tune with what follows, namely “the robe of Thy 
Grace (aruṭ sīr)”, this in contrast to it, is taken to mean the 
“attire of nescience (maruṭ sīr)”.

“Disrobing or destroying (azhittal)” signifies the act 
of making one upadhiless or freed of the appendages. 
“Nirvanam” denotes nakedness or the Transcendent Expanse 
of Awareness, the Form of Atman. “The robe of Grace (aruṭ 
sīr)” denotes the Divine robe of ecstatic grace that thrills 
every pore or the cover of nectareous flood of grace. The 
import arrived at by taking these two phrases namely 
‘disrobing me of my nescience” and “bestowing on me the 
robe of Grace” is: Destroying the pseudo greatness of my 
ego, the form of nescience and making it a void, graciously 
grant me the grandeur of my Real Form which is the Form 
of Thy Grace in exchange. In the adhyatmic sense, it is to 
be known that disrobing itself is establishing one in the 
void or in one’s Real Form – Atman –and, establishing 
one in void or Sunya itself, is “the granting of the robe 



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 73

of Grace.” “Stripping me of my robe making me naked* 
and to remove that nakedness bestow on me Thy Robe 
of Grace.” This is in tune with the context of the bridal 
imagery expressing the erotic sentiment. “Arul – Grace” 
also means ‘Prasad’ – a gift.

31.	 Suhak-kaḍal poṅga sollu-ṇar vaḍaṅga
		  Chummā porun-diḍaṅ garuṇāchalā

Substance: 
That my heart may surge forth in sweet joy as an 

ocean of beatific Bliss, with thought and speech subsiding 
together within, abide in my heart still and in tranquil 
quiescence. 
Commentary: 

In the phrase “abide there (porundu)”, by “there” it is 
deduced to mean the “Heart”. That Arunachala need neither 
fight shy of taking up residence in the temple of his Heart nor 
does He need to pay a rent for making his heart His Abode, 
Bhagavan says, “just abide (chumma porundu)”. The place He 
– the Form of Peace – abides, gets transformed as the ocean 
of Bliss where the clamour of the senses ceases, fruitless 
occupations of the mind fade into oblivion, wasteful taxing of 
the vocal chord and purposeless fulminations disappear. That 
His mere abidance in his Heart bestows on him blessings 
beyond measure is conveyed by the line “the ocean of Bliss 
swelling up, with speech stilled and mind subsided (suhak 
*	 Amana – The Jains, in the past, were walking about with 
no clothes on. Etymologically this word “Amanam”, meaning 
nakedness, seems to have originated from this word.



74 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

kaḍal poṅga solluṇarvu aḍaṅga)”. In experiencing Siva it is 
but natural for the mind and speech to cease. The Heart that 
has become the Abode of the Lord gets inundated with the 
Bliss of Serene Peace, with speech and mind silenced and 
stilled is the gist driven home.

32.	 Sūdu-sei deṉṉai sōdiyā diṉi-yuṉ 
		  Jyōti yuruk-kāṭ ṭaruṇāchalā

Substance: 
Pray, do not, (as Thou art wont to, all along) subject 

me to trials by Thy wily deceptions henceforth; but 
graciously reveal to me, here and now, Thy True Form of 
Glorious Light that I may directly perceive Thee.
Commentary: 

“Were Arunachala, the Master of prowess beyond 
measure, empowered with the unfathomable might of 
maya were to test, when potent celestials like Brahma and 
Vishnu cannot escape being caught in those trials, what 
to speak of ‘me’ with poor endowments of intelligence?” 
Hence Bhagavan requests Arunachala not to subject him to 
any more ordeals: “Do not test me anymore by Thy wily 
deceptions (sūdu seydu eṉṉai sōdiyādu)”. 

Ōdariya duvidamē adduvida jñāṉattai
Uṇḍupaṇu jñāṉam-āhum*
The inexplicable Dvaita knowledge
Is the knowledge that
Leads to Advaita knowledge.

*	 Thāyumāṉavar, Eṅgum Niṟaigiṉḏṟa Poruḷ, v. 3.



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 75

Though as Thāyumāṉavar says the perception of 
the form with the attitude of duality is just the right way 
to the perception of the formless, non-dual Truth, that in 
itself cannot be the true perception in the ultimate sense. 
Bhagavan, who was well aware of this Real Truth, prays to 
Arunachala not to beguile him anymore with the spectacle 
of forms, for the names and forms conceal Awareness but 
to bestow on him the revelation of the Absolute Truth – His 
Real Form of Effulgence, of Pure Awareness as his own 
Atman or Self bereft of the difference of triads so that the 
darkness of ignorance in the guise of knowledge made up of 
triads flees before His mercy’s beam. This, in essence, is the 
content of the phrase, “Reveal Thy Form of light (uṉ jyōti 
urukkāṭṭu).” While he had prayed earlier to Arunachala to 
“Reveal Thy Beauty (uṉ azhaigaik kāṭṭu)”, he pleads here 
“Reveal the resplendent Light of Thy Grace,” which is His 
Swarupa.

The Swarupa of Arunachala, the True Beauty, ever 
shines as the solid mass of Self-effulgence. This Pure 
Awareness, Arunachala, prevents the awareness that has 
touched it once from running after external things, binds it 
to Itself by making it subside and get anchored in It.

The Self, perceiving that effulgence of Awareness as 
Itself is what is proclaimed as the Non-Dual Experience by 
Bhagavan in verse 8 of Uḷḷadu Nāṟpadu.

Ep-peyar-iṭṭu ev-vuru-vil ēttiṉumār pēr-uruvil
Ap-poruḷaik kāṇ-vazhiya dāyiṉum-am – 

meyp-poruḷin
Uṇmaiyil-tan uṇmai-yiṉai ōrndo-ḍuṅgi oṉḏṟu-dalē
Uṇmaiyil kāṇal uṇar.



76 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

Under whatever name or form we worship It, It leads 
us to the knowledge of the nameless, formless Absolute. 
Yet to see one’s true Self in the Truth of the Absolute and 
abide in It, as one non-dual Self, is true darshan (vision). 
Know this.

“To test by deceptive guiles (sūdu seydu sōdittal)” here 
means to befuddle one, displaying the tricks of maya. This 
also refers to the method of leading one the garden path by 
the bestowal of the eight fold siddhis like anima etc. The 
word “henceforth (iṉi)” can be interpreted as in common 
usage “Pray do not henceforth deceive me;” or “at least 
from now on”. This word can also aligning with the word 
“Reveal (kāṭṭu)” will yield the meaning “henceforth reveal 
Thy Swarupa”.

This verse can be viewed in the milieu of bridal imagery 
too.

33.	 Sep-paḍi viddhai-kaṯ ṯṟip-paḍi mayak-kuviṭ 
		  Ṭurup-paḍu viddhai-kāṭ ṭaruṇāchalā

Substance: 
Graciously free me from the worldly delusion of 

assuming as real the art of jugglery and magic. Free 
me further from learning and getting deceived by it but 
reveal to me the sadvidya, the Supreme Art, the pursuit 
of which will make me behold the surging emergence of 
Thy Effulgence.
Commentary: 

“The deceptive art (seppaḍi viddhai)” connotes the arts 
of worldly sciences, created and projected as ‘real’ by the 



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 77

nescience that is inexplicable, possessing the wondrous 
adroitness of making apparently possible what is inherently 
impossible. Since that art is not ‘real’ or true, it is called 
“seppaḍi viddhai” or the art of “māyajāl” where, by sleight 
of hand, things are conjured up. 

Sat-tamum cōdi-ḍamum eṉḏṟāṅgu ivai-pidaṯ-ṯṟum
Pit-tariṉ pēdaiyār il.*
There are no fools greater than those who rave about 
the treatises of phonetics, grammar and astrology

Kala-kala kūun tuṇai-yallāl koṇḍu taḍumāṯ-ṯṟam
Pōom tuṇaiyaṟivār il.†
Of what avail is garnering of empirical knowledge, 
however vast, from worldly books – mere noisy 
verbiage. It is but the books of wisdom that light the 
way to freedom from sorrow.
It is evident from these that other learned and 

knowledgeable elders too regard the various arts relating to 
the sciences of the universe as nothing but ignorance. In this 
verse the term ‘paḍi’ stands for the world and the people of 
the world. In one sense, it means, “the art of make – believe, 
the learning of which deludes the world” and in the other, 
“learning the art of jugglery and by the use of that art, taking 
the gullible people of the world for a ride.”

Kal-lāda pērkaḷē nallavargaḷ nallavargaḷ 
	 kaṯ-ṯṟum-aṟi villāda-eṉ
Karmat-tai yeṉ-solgēṉ-madiyai-yeṉ sollugēṉ
	 kaivalya jñāṉa-nīdi

*	 Nālaḍiyār, Chapter 6, Asceticism, v. 52.
†	 Nālaḍiyār, Chapter 14, Learning, v. 140.



78 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

Nallōr uraik-kilō karma muk-kiyam-eṉḏṟu
	 nāṭ-ṭuvēṉ karmam-oruvaṉ 
Nāṭ-ṭiṉālō pazhaiya jñāṉa-muk-kiyam-eṉḏṟu
	 navi-luvēṉ vaḍa-mozhiyilē
Vallāṉ orut-taṉ varavum drā-viḍat-tilē
	 vandadā vivaharippēṉ
Valla-tamizh aṟijñar-variṉ aṅṅgaṉē vaḍa-mozhiyin
	 vacaṉaṅgaḷ ciṟidu-puhalvēṉ
Vel-lāmal evaraiyum maruṭṭi-viḍa vagai-vanda
	 vid-daiyeṉ mukti-tarumō
Vēdānta siddānta samarasa naṉ-ṉilai-peṯṯṟa
	 vittaga siddhar-gaṇamē.*
Certain it is, certain it is
Are they the goodly ones,
That have learning none.
What shall I speak of my fate,
My intelligence, who, though learned,
Is possessed of wisdom none?
If the good people say:
