of the jivabodha. Bhagavan prays for this false assumption 



240 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

to be rendered naught, one that sets the jiva apart, as 
if it is a separate entity different from the non-dual Self, 
Transcendent Expanse of Grace through the experience of 
jnana by gathering and shoring him up in Him in perfect 
union. This is what was spoken of in verse 30 as “disrobe 
me (destroying the pseudo greatness of the ego) and expose 
me naked (sīrai azhittu nirvāṇamāc ceydal).”

Let that be known as the bestowal of the ecstatic robe of 
His grace and true protection. “Reveal Thy Holy Grace (aruḷ 
veḷiviṭṭu)” may also mean “proclaiming Thy Holy Grace 
within my heart.”

99.	 Vēdān tattē vēṟaṟa viḷaṅgum 
		  Vēdap poruḷ-aruḷ Aruṇāchalā

Substance: 
Graciously deign to make mine that experience of 

True Knowledge of Being (sadbodha) that shines bereft of 
anything other than itself which eternal verity is enshrined 
in the Upanishads.
Commentary: 

The Vedas speak of the Eternal in stray passages here 
and there on diverse occasions couched in various ways and 
words. These can be divined and known only through hints 
thrown and indirect indications solely by men of pre-eminent 
calibre with competence and dispassion of a high order. But 
for others, in tune with their ripeness and requirements, it 
hails highly (in Karma kanda) the performance of actions and 
rituals of worship, all in the plane of duality. It is the jnana 
kanda of the Vedas that expounds explicitly, the Absolute 



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 241

Knowledge or Pure Knowledge. The Eternal, Non-dual 
Verity revealed is that the Self (the Atman) dissociated from 
the jivabodha with purely imagined attributes is Brahman. 
It is this shadow or phantom jivabodha that separates the 
Self from Brahman. As it is the jnana kanda, which is the 
Vedanta (Vedanta means the end of Vedas or summit or 
crown of the Vedas) that openly reveals the ultimate Truth, 
Bhagavan says “the conclusive and eternal verities of the 
Vedas that the Upanishads proclaim (vēdāntattē vēraṟa 
viḷaṅgum vēdapporuḷ).” Since this cannot be comprehended 
or grasped by any means except through the elucidation by 
perceptors and by an in depth study of sastras and scriptures, 
Bhagavan entreats Arunachala “Do bestow on me that” and 
earlier in Verse 19 he declares and addresses Him as “Thou 
who shineth as my guru (guru uruvāy oḷir Arunachala).”

“In the Upanishads (vēdāntattē)” means “the state 
of Absolute Silence – the farthest boundary of jnana” 
and “Shining as the Sole One without the other (vēraṟa 
viḷaṅgum)” can be interpreted to mean non-dually (with 
the difference between the jiva and para eliminated) and 
the “content of Vedas (vēdapporuḷ)” refers to “the Truth 
conclusively established by the knowledgeable elders as the 
intent and heart of the Holy Vedas”.



242 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

100.	 Vai-dalai vāzhttā vait-taruḷ kuḍiyā
		  Vait-teṉai viḍā-daruḷ Aruṇāchalā

Substance: 
Deign graciously to consider in Thy Divine Heart 

my verses of disparagement as verses of praise. And do 
further grant me the citizenship of Thy Empire of Holy 
Grace (under Thy Sovereign umbrella of Sivananda (the 
rapturous Bliss of Siva) enfolding me with Thy foremost 
servitors; forsake me not ever but forever shield me in 
sweet solicitude.
Commentary: 

“Unbecoming reproach (vaidal)”: This derogation is not 
only the outcome of the augmented upsurge of exceedingly 
intense longing to attain his beloved spouse Arunachala that 
possessed him but has also been occasioned by the privilege, 
liberty and exuberance of love engendered in him by His 
Grace prompting him to compose such verses of familiarity, 
seemingly an upbraid. 

Instances of verses containing revilement in varied forms 
occur in various places of this Text. As was entreated by him 
earlier to regard his faults as merits, here he pleads with 
Arunachala to consider his abuse as praise. Further in verse 
107 he begs Him to bear with his undignified and inelegant 
words and regard them as hymns of joy. “Accepting my 
derisive words as words of praise (vaidalai vāzhttā)”: That 
Bhagavan considered Arunachala his Father is evident from 
this line where he beseeches Him to be like a loving and 
indulgent father who enjoys the abusive prattle of a toddler 
as words of sweet melody. Further by requesting Him to 



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 243

accept him as a “rightful denizen of His reign of Grace (aruḷ 
kuḍiyā vaittu)” he reveals his conception of Arunachala as 
his Sole Lord, Emperor, who, regarding all beings as part of 
His own life, reigns over the entire universe as the Sovereign 
Ruler under a single umbrella – the royal insignia of that 
suzerainty.

The two-kinds of relationships between the ruler 
and the ruled and between the Preceptor and the disciple 
are addressed by the elders as “Dasa marga” (the path of 
servitor towards the Sire) and “Satputra marga” (the path of 
the ideal son towards the Sire) respectively.

Yet another reading for “vaidalai vāzhttā vaittu” will be 
as follows: “Keep” (vai) Thy hand of Grace on my ‘head’ 
(talai) whereby the initiation by way of Hastha masthaka 
Saiyogam, hastha (hand) masthaka (head) Saiyogam (union) 
is referred to.

“Citizen in the domain of Grace (aruṭkuḍi)” means 
a citizen worthy of being the recipient of the Fortune of 
Grace. “To keep (vaittu)” would also mean to be shored at 
the haven of the Holy Feet of Siva.
A.I.F.A.:

In the three verses (100, 106 & 107) Bhagavan addresses 
Arunachala as the Mountain of Forbearance and pleads 
with Him to be indulgent and to condone his faults and 
shortcomings in this Garland and that His ears accustomed 
to accepting the dulcet words of the ardent devotees may 
deign to accept his beratings and treat his upbraid as praise 
and on par with those sweet words. He further beseeches 
Him to guard him and keep him in His reign of Grace 
irrevocably as a blessed citizen of Grace.



244 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

101.	 Ambuvil āli-pōl aṉburu vuṉil-eṉai 
		  Aṉbāk karait-taruḷ Aruṇāchalā

Substance: 
Pray do dissolve me graciously in Thy Form of 

Supreme Love, as Self, as that very form of love to 
become one with Thee even as a hailstone dissolves back 
in water as the very form of water.
Commentary: 

“To dissolve – (karaittal)” is to release without the least 
trace of attachment from the awareness of the ‘upadhis’ – 
mere imagined entities – that are the sole reason for the 
appearances of dualities (in short, effecting abstraction from 
objective thoughts) by the experience of deep quiescence 
which is but Supreme devotion or para bhakti. Hail stone and 
water though in physical perception may differ apparently – 
the one being solid and the other liquid – in truth they are 
one in essence. Siva and jiva, though they appear distinct 
due to the imagined awareness of the adjuncts, by their 
quintessential nature of “Being” both are one and the same.

Iruk-kum iyaṟkai-yāl Īsa jīvar-kaḷ
Oru-poruḷē yāvar undī-paṟa
Upādhi uṇarvē-vēṟ undī-paṟa*
The Creator and the created are in substance one. 
They differ only in their awareness of attributes.
If, in truth, hailstone is heterogeneous to water, never 

– and by no means – can it dissolve to become one with 
water. If jiva in truth is other than Siva, then the ultimate end 
of Non-Dual Release of commingling with non-difference 

*	 Sri Bhagavan, Upadēsa Undiyār, verse 24.



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 245

with the Sacred Form of Siva can never – and by no means 
– be possible. In that case, the ‘mahavakyas” of Upanishads 
and Agamas that proclaim so emphatically and conclusively 
this commingling of jiva and para as ‘Supreme Release’ 
will be rendered void of content.

That is why Thāyumāṉavar affirms this truth in a verse 
thus:

Apporuḷum āṉmāvum āraṇa-nūl coṉṉapaḍi
Tappu-illāc cittu-oṉḏṟām cādiyiṉāl - eppaḍiyum
Tēril tuvitam Sivākamamē sollu-niṭṭai
Āru-miḍattu attuvida mām.*
That Substance (God) and the soul
Art both of the same category, Chit (Spirit),
So the scriptures say.
No doubt about it.
The silent trance
That the Agamas speak of
Is but dvaita, however considered.
But advaita is when
The union of the two takes place.
Viewing the indispensable need of spiritual endeavours 

in the path of jnana for an ajnani or an ignorant one to ensure 
release from the bondage of samsara, Thāyumāṉavar strikes 
a conciliatory note between dvaita (duality) and advaita 
by admitting duality only during the spell of sadhana.† 
But Bhagavan asserting his dissent with this attempt at 
conciliation rejects it. He declares conclusively with a 
striking emphasis as the Seer of the Upanishad “Uḷḷadu 

*	 Thāyumāṉavar - Uḍal Poyyuṟavu, v. 16.	
†	 This, reportedly, is the religion of Sage Odulomi.



246 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

Nāṟpadu” (Forty Verses on Reality) that he authored as 
follows:

Sāda-kat-tilē duvitam sāddi-yattil addu-vidam
Ōdu-kiṉḏṟa vāda-madum uṇmai-yala ‒ ādara-vāyt
Tāṉ-tēḍum kālum taṉai-aḍainta kālat-tum
Tāṉ-dasamaṉ aṉḏṟi-yār tāṉ.
Even the reasoning advanced that the duality as jiva 
and Iswara is real while practicing the sadhana to 
gain Atman and that there will be no duality at the 
end of the sadhana upon the attainment of jnana (as 
there is union of the two) is not true. When eagerly 
he sought himself and later when he ‘found’ himself, 
the tenth man in the story was the tenth man all 
along and none else.
Thus does Bhagavan declare to men of competence the 

truth of his experience as it really is with no inhibition or 
restraint. Thāyumāṉavar’s fear that if advaita is accepted 
during the intervening spell of sadhana, the necessity 
of sadhana of the pursuit of enquiry required as an 
indispensable tool to remove the veil of nescience – the real 
obstacle to true jnana – is rendered redundant. Admission of 
non-duality even during sadhana does not impair the truth 
of the Reality as exemplified by the tenth man in the parable 
that Bhagavan has cited.

Aṉbum Sivamum iraṇḍu-eṉbar aṟivilār
Aṉbē Sivam-āvadu ārum aṟihilār
Aṉbē Sivam-āvadu ārum aṟinda-piṉ
Aṉbē Sivamāy amarndu irundārē*

*	 Tirumular’s Tirumandiram.



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 247

The ignorant prate that Love and Siva are two. 
But none do know that Love alone is Siva 
When men but know that Love and Siva are the same 
Love as Siva, they ever remain.
The excellent congruence of the idea in the exactitude 

of the declaration of the Truth this verse bears to the above 
one is to be dwelt in depth and appreciated. 

In Siva, the Form of Love, the jiva, by the essence of 
the rapturous bliss of Awareness dissolving as that Form 
of Love is Para Bhakti* or Supreme Love. This is, to see 
one’s true Self in the Absolute, to merge into It and be one 
with It. That jiva and para are one, both by this parajnana† 
(Transcendent Knowledge) and by the real nature and form 
and experience, is to be assimilated.
A.I.F.A.:

“Arunachala, just as a hailstone dissolves in water as 
that very water, graciously dissolve me in Thee, the form of 
Love, as that very form of love.”

*	 Bhāva bhalat-tiṉāl bhāvaṉā-tīta sat
	 Bhāvat tirut-talē undī-paṟa
	 Para-bhakti tat-tuvam undī-paṟa (Upadēsa Undiyār, v. 9)
Abidance in Pure Being simply as ‘I am’ – the Sphurana of Sat – 
the state beyond the reach of any bhavana gained by the strength 
of dhyana (bhavanabala) is Supreme devotion.
† 	 Taṉṉai upādhi viṭ-ṭōrvadu tāṉ-Īsaṉ
	 Taṉṉai uṇar-vadām undī-paṟa
	 Tāṉāy oḷir-vadāl undī-paṟa (Upadēsa Undiyār, v. 25)
To see oneself free of all attributes
Is to see the Lord,
For He shines ever as the Pure Self.”



248 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

Though the jiva appears to be a chidabhasa (just the 
reflected Awareness) he, in truth is Awareness. If he but 
merges in the Lord rid of his reflected status, he dissolves 
in his real form of ‘chit’ (Awareness), the very form of 
the Lord. Hence the comparison “like hailstone in water 
(ambuvil ālipōl).” How is it? Though the hailstone seems a 
solid object, it is but water in essence. If it mixes with water, 
its solid form disappearing, it dissolves into its real form of 
liquid as water. “Dissolve me, the form of love, in Thy form 
of Love as the very form of Thy Love.” In tune with “Thy 
Form of Love (aṉburu uṉil)” the import for “me (eṉai)” is 
expounded as “hard hearted (vaṉburu)” referring to one 
entrenched deeply in the concept of “I am the body,” and 
possessed of a heart hard as stone that softens not even at the 
sight of miseries of others. Dissolving the jiva as the form of 
the Self is indeed, the bestowal of Grace by the Lord.

102.	 Aruṇaiyeṉ ḏṟeṇṇa-yāṉ aruḷ-kaṇṇi paṭṭēṉ-uṉ
		  Aruḷ-valai tap-pumō Aruṇāchalā

Substance: 
The very instant I thought of Thee as ‘Arunachala’, I 

got entrapped in Thy net of Grace. Designed to entangle 
devotees (designed to make devotees plunge inward), will 
that net of grace err even a little in its intended mission?
Commentary: 

‘Net’ is meant by the word “kaṇṇi” and “to get caught” is 
‘paḍudal’. At the very commencement of this litany Bhgavan 
declares “Aruṇāchalam eṉa ahamē niṉaippavar ahattai vēr 
aṟuppāy Aruṇāchalā (Thou dost uproot and annihilate the 



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 249

ego of those who meditate on Thee in their hearts).” “To 
think – (eṇṇa)” is taken in the sense of both ‘cause’ and 
‘effect’. True to the pronouncement of the celebrated saint 
Maṇivāchakar “He who willingly gets caught in the net of 
devotion – (bhakti valaiyil paḍuvōṉ kāṇga)”, Arunachala, 
who is sought to be ensnared by the devotees in their net 
of devotion, encircling them all around, is ever eager to 
entrap them in His net of Grace and thus preempts them. It 
is precisely because of this reason that they become worthy 
and capable of thinking of Him.

Eṉṉai yēdum aṟin-dilaṉ em-pirāṉ
Taṉṉai nāṉum-muṉ ēdum aṟin-dilēṉ
Eṉṉait taṉ-aḍiyāṉ eṉḏṟu aṟi-dalum
Taṉṉai nāṉum pirāṉ-eṉḏṟu aṟindeṉē.*
I did not know the truth about myself nor of the Master
Even so in days of yore.
As soon as He recognised me as His protege
Then did I recognise Siva as my Master!
The Tamil Veda of Maṇivāchakar, by hailing Him as “By 

His Grace, His Feet bowed down to (avaṉ aruḷālē avaṉtāḷ 
vaṇaṅgi)” renders the comprehension of this idea clear. As 
if answering in the affirmation, the question “will that net 
of Grace ever fail (aruḷ valai tappumō)” as “No, It will not” 
the succeeding verse pursues the idea further with elegance 
and beauty.
A.I.F.A.:

“Arunachala, when I thought of Thee by Thy holy 
name, Arunachala, the foremost and exalted of all Thy Holy 

*	 Appar Thēvāram, 5th Tirumuṟai, 91-Podhu, v. 8.



250 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

Names, with that very thought as the refuge (or the bait) 
I was caught in Thy web of Grace. Will that net of Grace 
allow me to escape? Do please tell me.”

The Nature and Form of the Lord namely Truth, Pure 
Awareness, Bliss is the content of the three letters A-RU-NA. 
Hence it is venerated as the most exalted of all His names. 
The line “The instant I thought of Arunai, I was ensnared by 
the net of Grace (aruṇai eṉḏṟu eṇṇa yāṉ aruṭkaṇṇi paṭṭēṉ)” 
reveals the other import that the term “Arunai” carries that 
of the Union of Para and jiva which is the content of the 
Mahavakya, That Thou Art (TAT TVAM ASI). ‘Kanni’ is a 
causal name for the net as it has many eyes (eyelets). The 
form and frame of the Lord is simply the Divine Grace and It 
is Whole and Infinite. Grace and God are one and the same. 
There is no other place to go to escaping It and that never 
fails. Hence the query, “Will it ever fail (avvalai tappumō)?” 
That it will not, was proclaimed in verse 92, “Thy missile of 
Grace consumed me alive.”

103.	 Cindit taruḷ-paḍac cilandipōl kaṭṭi 
		  Ciṟai-yiṭ ṭuṇ-ḍaṉai Aruṇāchalā

Substance: 
Thou weaving wide Thy web of Grace like a spider 

(marking me), thinking, “let him be trapped in it” and 
when I just as per Thy stratagem got caught in it, that 
very moment, imprisoning me tight without allowing 
me to escape (to the outside world) Thou devoured and 
destroyed me and made me the form of Thyself.



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 251

Commentary: 
The word “thinking or planning or designing (cindittu)” 

hints by implication, specially “the initiation by thought”, 
one of the three kinds of initiation that the Supreme Guru 
adopts. “Weaving the web, well planned and designed like 
a spider” is one meaning arrived at by the phrase “cindittu 
cilandi pōl kaṭṭi.” Instead of taking the phrase as “cindittu 
kaṭṭi” but as “cindittu aruḷ paḍa” as it is in the verse, so 
that “I, thinking of Arunachala, got entrapped in Thy web of 
Grace” is the other meaning that emerges.

The spider spins its web out of the strands of its saliva 
with a well thought out plan of letting the insects and 
worms get caught in it. And when they do get trapped in it, 
without letting its prey escape it tightens its hold on them by 
further entangling them and finally consumes them. All this 
is implicitly conveyed by the word “like a spider (cilandi 
pōl)”. This verse is reminiscent of what he states earlier in 
Verse 3: “Thou made me a prisoner in Thy Heart’s cavern 
(uṉ ahaguhai ciṟaiyāy amarvittadu).” “Thy Heart Cavern” 
there and “Thy net of Grace” here both mean the same.

“Thou devoured me (uṇḍaṉai)”: The attainment of this 
rare fortune namely, the state of self-loss or jivanmukti or the 
rapturous life in Siva is highly extolled by Māṇikkavāchakar 
in a decad and also by Nammāzhwar in many a Pasuram 
(holy hymn). While Nammāzhwar exclaims, “He entering 
within me, as if to bestow grace, my form and life devoured 
instantly (tiruvaruḷ ceypavaṉ pōla eṉ uḷ puhundu uruvamum 
ōr ār uyirum uḍaṉē uṇḍāṉ);” while in “uyiruṇṇip pattu” 
Maṇivāchakar records, “My soul is consumed”. “Entering 
me too, He made me His, melting my very bones!” And 



252 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

again “He entered my flesh; mixed with my life; and leaves 
not my soul.”

Bhagavan in this and verse 92 “lakshiyam vaittaruḷ 
astiram viṭṭeṉaip bhakshittāy prāṇaṉōḍu: Thou marking me 
as the target of Thy unfailing sharp arrow of grace released 
Thy weapon of grace and devoured me alive” reveals his 
attainment of that blessed fortune of Life of Bliss in Siva, a 
life lived in the clear bright light of Pure Self-Awareness as 
Siva which he obtained by the Grace of Arunachala.

104.	 Aṉboḍuṉ ṉāmaṅ-kēḷ aṉbar-tam aṉbaruk
		  Kaṉ-baṉā yiḍa-varuḷ Aruṇāchalā

Substance: 
Graciously grant me that I, Thy bounden slave, may 

become a votary of votaries of Thy devotees whose hearts 
well up in bliss on hearing, to the delight of their ears, the 
Panchakshara of Thy Holy name – Arunachala (A- Ru-
Na-Cha-La).
Commentary: 

The lord and the holy name of the Lord are non-different. 
The glory and greatness of the named that the name* 
celebrates is called upalakshana or secondary characteristic. 
(The characteristic of the goal that makes the goal known is 
called upalakshana.)

The name of Arunachala is indicative of His Fame 
which His Name is inclusive of. The name is identical with 

*	 Nāmam or name, Sambandhar records “the ears on hearing 
His famed name (pugazh nāmam cevikēṭpa).ˮ



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 253

the Mountain as the form and Siva as the name of the Being 
whose substance is Pure Transcendental Awareness.

“Those who listen to Thy name with a heart yearning 
with utmost love” is the import of “kēḷ aṉbar”. The concept 
of devotion inherently runs through the word “devotee-
(aṉbar)”. That all spiritual endeavours like Sravana, 
devotion and the like will be of no avail in the absence 
of Shraddha is the significance of the use of the phrase 
“those who listen to Thy name with love and devotion 
(aṉbōḍu kēḷaṉbar)”. Bhagavan, purely to emphasize this 
fact employs this phrase even though, strictly speaking, it 
was not an absolute necessity. The fruit of having listened 
to the divine names of the Lord being chanted in chorus 
in the midst of groups of devotees (nama sankirtanam or 
Bhajanam) with longing and devotion in the beginning and 
subsequently learnt by repeatedly hearing it manifests as the 
seed nurtured to blossom as Supreme Jnana that is identical 
with Para bhakti. This process by slow degress transforms 
the delusion of pride and desires, extinguishes the bonds of 
attachments, familial and worldly, like the observance of 
codes of conduct in vogue in the world. It gradually evolves 
and matures into ceaseless inherence in the thoughts of the 
Lord making one as if seized with madness for the Lord. 
Finally it fruitions into the state of self-loss and bestows 
with certainty the state of deliverance on the devout. Saint 
Appar speaks of this transformation, gradual but firm in 
great graphic details

Muṉ-ṉam ava-ṉuḍaiya nāmam kēṭ-ṭāḷ
Mūrtti avaṉ-irukkum vaṇ-ṇam kēṭ-ṭāḷ
Piṉ-ṉai ava-ṉuḍaiya ārūr kēṭ-ṭāḷ
Peyart-tum ava-ṉukkē picci yāṉāḷ



254 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

Aṉṉai-yaiyum atta-ṉaiyum aṉḏṟē nīt-tāḷ
Ahaṉ-ḏṟāḷ ahaliḍat-tār ācā rattait
Taṉṉai maṟandāḷ taṉ-ṉāmam keṭ-ṭāḷ
Talaip-paṭ-ṭāḷ naṅgai talaivaṉ tāḷē.*
To begin with, she heard of His name;
She heard of Moorti`s way of life;
Then she heard of His Aaroor;
Yet she became mad after Him;
She quit her mother and father that very day;
She forsook the mores of the worldly;
She became oblivious of herself;
She became nameless;
The woman was oned with the feet of her Lover. 
Auvaiyar, the grand old lady-saint of Tamil Sangam era 

proclaims “The Lord is indeed enshrined and abides in the 
heart of His servitors. Oh! The grandeur of the servitor is 
far too great to be uttered (iṟaivaṉō toṇḍar uḷḷattu oḍukkam, 
toṇḍartam perumaiyaic sollavum peridē)”.

While that which describes the legends of the worshipful 
deities is called merely “Puranam”, that which celebrates 
the lives of holy and blessed servitors is eulogized as Peria 
Puranam (The Great Puranam). The prefix great (peria) 
indeed thus celebrates the exalted glory of the devotees. 
Appar also upholds this revered tradition.

Evarēṉum tāmāha ilāḍat tiṭṭa
Tiru-nīṟum sādhaṉa-mum kaṇḍāl uḷgi
Uvarādē avar-avaraik kaṇḍa pōdē
Uhandu aḍimait tiṟam niṉaindu aṅgu-uvandu nōkki

*	 Appar Thēvāram, 6th Tirumuṟai, 25-Tiruvarur. v. 7.



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 255

Ivar-dēvar avar-dēvar eṉḏṟu solli
Iraṇḍu-āṭṭādu ozhindu-īsaṉ tiṟamē pēṇik
Kavarādē tozhum-aḍiyār neñjiṉ uḷḷē
Kaṉḏṟāp-pūr naḍu-taṟiyaik kāṇa lāmē.*
Whoever they be (it matters not);
If you behold in their forehead the holy ash and if 
You behold in their Person 
The marks of Saivism,
Give up dislike;
Think of the greatness of the habit;
The moment you sight them,
Think on the greatness of their servitorship and 
Behold them in joy immense;
Never say: “These are devas and He,
The Lord,
Is the Deva!
Do not distinguish;
Think these to be The Lord Himself;
In the hearts of such adiyar 
Who so adore in single-minded devotion you can 
Behold the Nadutari of Kandraappoor!
While Arunachala is the sthavara linga (the immobile 

stationary linga) the devotee is the jangama linga embodied 
linga in motion; hence to view or observe any difference 
between the two is deemed sheer ignorance. Saint Sundarar 
to enlighten us on this truth, declares himself as “the servitor 
of the servitors of Lord Siva” and hails them enthroning them 
on a high pedestal and crowning them with the glory of his 

* 	 Appar Thēvāram, 6th Tirumuṟai, 61-Tirukkaṉḏṟāppūr, v. 3.



256 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

limitless love and wonder for them by naming them all, one 
by one in his “Thiruthondat Thohai”. Bhagavan too, following 
the same tradition refers to them in this verse collectively, 
though not individually as “let me become a votary of the 
votaries of Thy devotees (aṉbartam aṉbarukku aṉbaṉ āyiḍa 
aruḷ).” Celebrating the absence of difference among Bhagavan 
(the Lord), Bhagavata Purana (the epic) that celebrates the 
glory of the Lord and His servitors) and the Bhagavata (one 
who sings His glory) is the lauded, well established and 
hoary tradition of the Bhakti cult. Thāyumāṉavar was such 
a saint who, enjoying highly the greatness of the glories of 
the devotees of the Lord, hails them as follows: “If only I am 
made to be the servitor of the devotees, Bliss perennial will 
seek and come to me on its own:” 

Aṉbar-paṇi ceyya eṉṉai āḷākki viṭṭu-viṭṭāl 
Iṉba nilai tāṉē-vandu eydum Parā-paramē.
However much in grandiloquent terms the greatness 

of association with Sats, the devotees of Siva – the Siva 
jnanis – is extolled it can never be a hyperbole. They are the 
true devotees who enable those who seek and reach them 
attain the summum bonum of spiritual gain without their 
undergoing the rigours of painful physical and spiritual 
observances. They are the true devotees who bless their 
followers with the knowledge of the Upanishads which is 
unattainable without much toil and trouble, with ease and 
without arduous labour.
A.I.F.A.:

“Arunachala! Bless me that I may become a devotee 
to the devotees of Thy devotees who listen to Thy Divine 
Name with love.”



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 257

While the phrase “the devout who listen (kēḷ aṉbar)” 
would have sufficed, the addition of the word, “Who listen 
with love (aṉboḍu kēḷaṉbar)” reveals not only the inherent 
blessed sweetness that the holy name brings to the ears but 
also the wondrous and abiding delight and the abundant 
longing Bhagavan had in listening to His name.

The specific mention of his desire to “become a humble 
devotee to the devotees of Thy devotees,” instead of the 
sentence “becoming a devotee to those who listen with love” 
brings to the fore the onrush of intense love and affection he 
bore for such holy servitors of the Lord. “Those who listen 
(kēḷaṉbar)” refers to those given to ceaseless listening. 
No fortune can be of greater worth than consorting with 
devotees whose greatness is beyond reckoning.

105.	 Eṉ-pōlum dhīṉarai iṉbuṟak kāttu-nī 
		  En-nāḷum vāzhn-daruḷ Aruṇāchalā

Substance: 
May Thou live for all eternity protecting forever the 

poor and hapless devotees like me all over, bestowing on 
all the infinite and rapturous bliss in Siva.
Commentary: 

Bhagavan exemplifying the dictum that “great men 
never live for themselves but belong forever to others” 
supplicates Arunachala not only on his but on behalf of all 
the other devotees, imploring “like my humble self, all the 
helpless and simple devotees (eṉ pōlum dīṉarai)”.

“To guard them so that they live in happiness (iṉbuṟak 
kāttu)”: He pleads for the Grace of Arunachala so that they 



258 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

are protected from the wretched woes of transmigration 
and bestowed with the infinite Bliss of Brahman. As bliss 
coupled with joy bereft of wearisome mental worries is rare 
to obtain but for the grace of the incomparable Absolute, 
the content of ‘That’ (Tat), he appeals for the grace of the 
Lord in the words “protect them with happiness (iṉbuṟak 
kāttu)”. This act of saving the simple devotees plagued by 
miseries with succour, happiness and upliftment is not one 
that happens out of a resolution but the natural ‘dharma’ an 
inherent characteristic of His Swaroopa (essential nature) as 
it is for the Sun, the form of brilliance, to light up and save 
the world enveloped in darkness.

Grace apart from Arunachala and Arunachala apart 
from Grace, both dissociated from each other, operating at 
any point of time or place is an utter impossibility: for Grace 
is Arunachala and Arunachala is Grace and the operation of 
His Grace is infinite and eternal. Hence “guarding (kāttu)” 
and “living forever and anon (ennāḷum vāzhndu)” are 
occurrences, simultaneous and eternal.

Since when Arunachala is, since then is His act of Grace; 
both are ancient and beginningless. The word “forever and 
anon (ennāḷum)” by its secondary characteristic also stands 
for “all places (evviḍattum)”. Even as the kural which is 
so praised as to contain all truths like a mustard seed 
into the hole of which is compacted the waters of the 
seven seas, this beautiful and divine verse encapsulates 
within itself with sweet elegance all prayers in their 
entirety with brevity and clarity to the delight of all. 
Since this beautiful, divine and inspiring couplet pleases 
Arunachala, the ocean of grace, inordinately, like the 



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 259

holy mantra of ‘Panchakshara’ it is worthy of being 
chanted mentally, unceasingly and steadily by devotees 
who seek His grace with ardent love.

A prayer for the eternal life for all devotees and a 
benediction for Him who bestows it are hailed together 
in this verse. As He protects the loving devotees with 
happiness, Arunachala, the embodiment of grace, is 
verily the pith and marrow of the term “life”. Guarding 
the loving devotees, enveloping them in the Supreme Bliss 
of Siva – Sivananda – is the ‘eternal life’ of Arunachala, 
the embodiment of Grace.
A.I.F.A.:

“Arunachala! May Thou live forever uniformly the 
same as Thou art today, guarding all guileless devotees like 
me from the miseries that assail them and granting them the 
attainment of joy and happiness.”

Revelling, merged and saturated in the depths of the 
boundless ocean of Grace Divine, Bhagavan intercedes on 
behalf of other devotees like him and pleads with the Lord 
saying “Simple folks like me (eṉ pōlum dhīṉarai)”. Since 
the bestowal of Grace on them by Him is in response to 
his request and is therefore onesided, he hails Him with a 
benediction for life till eternity in the line “Guard them with 
joy and live forever (iṉbuṟak kāttu nī ennāḷum vāzhndaruḷ)”. 
Though such benedictions as per tradition so occur at the 
conclusion of the litany, Bhagavan, acknowledging that the 
Grace that the Lord will rain on them will be prompt and 
profuse – the Heaven of Compassion that He is – in response 
to his request hails Him well in advance.



260 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

106.	 Eṉburu kaṉbar-tam iṉsoṟ-koḷ sevi-yumeṉ
		  Puṉ-mozhi koḷa-varuḷ Aruṇāchalā

Substance: 
May Thy divine ears accustomed to feast on the 

grandeur of mellifluous utterances of hymns with love, 
melting the very bones, by the learned devout, graciously 
condescend to hear the insipid and prosaic litany of mine 
devoid of such fervour of devotion.
Commentary: 

The term ‘bone’ (eṉbu) implies the entire body. It 
means not only the heart but even the body of such devotees 
simply melts away with love and longing for Him. “The 
devotees with bone-melting love (eṉburukaṉbar)” stands 
for saints of the calibre of “the Saint of Thiruvadavur” 
namely Māṇikkavāchakar and the “sublime poesy (iṉsol) of 
them” points to Texts of Litany like Tiruvāchakam (sacred 
utterances) and Tirukkōvaiyār (sacred Kōvaiyār, kōvai being 
a form of metrical prosody). “No utterance can move and 
melt those who are not stirred and moved by Tiruvāchakam 
(Tiruvāchakattāl uruhādār oru vāchakattālum uruhār)” is 
how the learned-devout celebrate that holy litany investing 
it with solemn greatness beyond compare, emphasizing 
the fact of its being the foremost among Texts in spiritual 
literature that inspire in the readers deep devotion that thaws 
and melts the very bones.

The ears of the Lord graciously lend themselves to the 
poignant hymns of the devotees and accept them. Since 
they possess the greatness of being the instruments of grace, 
showering sweet and profound grace on them, they are 



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 261

celebrated as of special significance. Even for men of the 
ordinary run, the instruments of hearing are of particular 
importance as evidenced from the adage “listening is any 
day better than learning (kaṯṟaliṉ kēṭṭalē naṉḏṟu)” and the 
couplet of Thiruvalluvar, “The most sublime of all riches 
is the treasure of the ear (selvattuḷ selvam seviccelvam)”. 
Verily it is the acme of all wealth. Even Saint Jnana 
Sambanda, in his maiden hymn breaks forth in the very first 
line addressing the ear of Lord Siva as follows: “Behold 
Him with an ear having a plug of rolled palmyra-leaf stuck 
in its pierced lobes (tōḍuḍaiya ceviyaṉ).”

For the phrase “the devotees with love that melts the 
very bones (eṉburukaṉbar)” the contrary meaning for “my 
(eṉ)” is “the loveless me” and similarly for “sweet words 
(insol)” the contrary one is “my prosaic words (“puṉmozhi”). 
Espousing humility, the spontaneous characteristic of all 
great souls, Bhagavan speaks of his verses as “prosaic and 
insipid (puṉmozhi).”
A.I.F.A.:

Arunachala! May Thy ears wont to accept with delight 
the sweet words of Thy devotees with fathomless love that 
melts their very bones, do deign to accept, with grace and 
solicitude my unsavoury words wanting in such love as well.

The love that springs and wells up in the hearts of 
these other devotees imparts the sweetness to their words. 
Knowledgeable ones declare love as the seventh taste and 
flavour. As his words lack the lustre and sweetness of love, 
he terms them ‘prosaic and plain’.



262 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

107.	 Poṟu-maiyām bhūdara puṉ-solai naṉ-solāp
			   Poṟut-taruḷ iṣṭampiṉ Aruṇāchalā

Substance: 
Oh Hill! The embodiment of Forbearance! Deign to 

bear the pedestrian and inelegant words of mine and be 
pleased to accept them graciously as words, auspicious 
and worthy of Thee. Let Thy will prevail!
Commentary: 

Bhagavan who pleads for the forbearance of his inelegant 
address and to treat them as sweet and pleasant supplications, 
appeals to Arunachala as the “Hill of Forbearance 
(poṟumaiyām bhūdara)”, the name quite apposite for the 
place and occasion. The words ‘poṟuttal’, ‘darittal’ and 
‘taṅgal’ all mean “to bear”. The word “bhūdara” is not used 
here as a generic term or a common noun, as a synonym 
for “a hill”. Arunachala, the sole Hero, hailed in this and in 
other Text of hymns by Bhagavan, in truth, like Himachala, 
the king of all mountains, is not a mere hill, gross and 
physical, an object of perception but the effulgence of Pure 
Awareness, the form of ‘drik’ (the Subject or the Perceiver), 
Brahman, the Supreme Siva. Hence He is the one who bears 
the earth (bhūdara) – it also being a causal name with a 
different connotation. In the case of all other mountains the 
term ‘bhūdara’ remains a causal name in the sense of “being 
borne by Mother Earth”. But with respect to Arunachala 
who is famed universally as a Hill as such, He – not being 
ordinary, but truly and simply out of this world, of the most 
subtle and esoteric form – the Self – the term ‘hill’ may apply 
to It as an ordinary gross image as a ‘linga’ formed of granite 



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 263

stones in the plane of physical perception for temporal use. 
But in the case of the Transcendental form of Arunachala, 
the flare of the effulgence of knowledge, the rich content of 
Omniscience, beyond the purview of speech and mind, the 
application of the term ‘hill’ (bhūdara) in the sense of being 
borne by earth is highly inappropriate and improbable, He, 
being the substratum of all. Notwithstanding this, while 
Bhagavan beseeches Arunachala to bear patiently with him 
and his faulty words and addressing Him in eulogistic terms, 
as ‘bearing the entire world’ by virtue of His Transcendental 
nature – an address that is out of the ordinary, – is most apt. 
On the contrary, addressing Him as being borne by “an earth” 
merely from the gross or mundane perception is denigrating 
Him and, therefore, highly inapt and inappropriate. Since 
a prayer addressing Him in that fashion will become 
infructuous, in the sense of “being borne by earth” He is 
“bhūdara”, is to be dismissed summarily.

Bhagavan who prayed earlier in Verse 100 “to treat even 
his slander as praise and to bestow grace (vaidalai vāzhttā 
vaittaruḷ)” in tune with the paramount importance of this 
prayer, reiterates his submission in this verse too as he is on 
the verge of winding up this Text. He beseeches Arunachala 
“to treat his puerile words as hymns of joy (puṉsolai naṉsolāp 
poṟuttaruḷ)”, declaring “I have no other prayer than this” in 
consonance with what he announced in Nava Manimalai:

“I will never think of merits or demerits. Thou art my 
very life! Do as Thou pleaseth with me Lord! The Eye of 
my eyes!. Grant me the cascading flood of love at Thy twin 
Lotus Feet (eṇṇēṉ ivaṯṟai eṉṉuyirē eṇṇam eduvō aduceyvāy 
kaṇṇē uṉdaṉ kazhal iṇaiyil kādal perukkē taruvāyē)”. 



264 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

Bhagavan finally proclaims “Thy Sweet will, (iṣṭampiṉ). 
I, who remain as the Truth of Thee – the Non-dual true 
Being – have no desire of my own except Thy will”

“Whatever Thou doth, that being the sweet will of Thee, 
my Ruler, that shall be my will too” is the import.

Vēṇḍi, nī yādu aruḷ seydāy, yāṉum, aduvē vēṇḍiṉ 
allāl,

Vēṇḍum parisu oṉḏṟu uṇḍu eṉṉil, aduvum, uṉ taṉ 
viruppu aṉḏṟē?*

Desiring what didst Thou Bestow Thy Grace?
That and naught else do I desire.
And if aught else there be, that stirs in me as desire
That too in sooth, is Thy Desire –Is it not so?
If Thou wert to query me what right do I have to pray 

for the forbearance of my trivial words and to treat them as 
acceptable ones following the tradition of the people of the 
world when, I without the sense of doership sang this litany 
of the wedding garland abiding by Thy grace, Bhagavan 
says “I shall not say even that too” except to say “Thy sweet 
will and desire”. This indeed is “abiding in the service of the 
Lord”. The celebrated prayer offered by Jesus, the Son of 
God is made by Bhagavan too, here.

Even Maṇivāchakar, the one of Sovereign speech, hails 
in ecstasy, “Is mine the authority here? Do Thou to me 
what’s good alone or do Thou ill. Have I mastery over this?”

*	 Māṇikkavāchakar, Tiruvāchakam, 33-Kuzhaitta Pattu, v. 6.



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 265

108.	 Mālai yaḷit-taru ṇāchala Ramaṇa-veṉ 
			   Mālai yaṇin-daruḷ Aruṇāchalā

Substance: 
Arunachala Ramana! Do help me graciously by 

granting me the cascade of true love for Thee and deign 
to accept and adorn Thyself with my offering to Thee – 
the garland of letters.
Commentary: 

Intense and ever-increasing love is ‘māl’. Bhagavan 
prays for that very same exuberance of love in 
Navamanimalai too. The word “Ramanan” has many 
connotations like “the one who revels”, “the sweet heart”, 
“a darling” and “a beloved”.

Earlier, at the commencement of this Text, Bhagavan 
addressed Arunachala as the “bridegroom (Arunachala 
‘varaṉ’)”. Arunachala is the Atmaraman who, as the form 
of Grace, in the heart lotus of all beings beginning with 
Hari, revels as Awareness. Hence the address ‘Arunachala 
Ramanan’.

“My garland (eṉ mālai)”: The garland of this Text is 
what I, with the strand of ecstatic love that sprang up from 
my heart, have strung the blossoms of sweet words and have 
wreathed this garland of fragrant devotion specially for Thee. 
In the wedding ritual the exchange of garlands between the 
bride and bridegroom is an integral and important part. This 
couplet in the ritualistic wedding of ‘kaivalya’, the union 
of jiva and para, fulfils the singularly significant role of 
exchange of garlands. As Lord Ranganatha accepted with 
delight the garland that Andal wore and offered to Him with 



266 Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai

Love, graciously accept and adorn Thyself with this garland 
fashioned by me in joy is the spirit of this verse.

Those devotees who worship Arunachala with true 
devotion by repeatedly going over this Text in their hearts, 
by His Profound Grace besides gaining all good on earth, 
will obtain in the hereafter, the State of Siva, the Bliss 
of Union with Siva beyond name and form. Likewise, 
unmarried maidens who sing this Hymns of wedding garland 
set to sweet tunes daily with devotion, specially this one 
couplet unceasingly as a ‘mantra’, with total dedication to 
Arunachala in their hearts will find that whatever they long 
for will come to pass by the Grace of Arunachala Ramana. 
All those who chant This Text regularly and with devotion 
will be endowed with the wealth of Divine Grace, will live 
long on earth obtaining all the four dharmas of the temporal 
world, united with fame. This indeed is the fruit and blessing 
accruing to the devout by reciting this Text.

Arunachala Siva Arunachala Siva
Arunachala Siva Arunachala
Arunachala Siva Arunachala Siva
Arunachala Siva Arunachala

A.I.F.A.:
The glory and grandeur of Grace which is but Arunachala 

is expatiated in diverse ways in this Text of Grace by 
Bhagavan. They are:

The Treasure trove of Holy Grace (49)
The Jewel of Grace (53)
The Smile of Grace (53)
The Shower of Grace (55)
The Divine Play of Grace (74)



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 267

The Blessed Medicine of Grace (76)
The Collyrium of Grace (84)
The Missile of Grace (92)
The Expanse of Grace (98)
The Citizen of the Reign of Grace (100)
The Snare of Grace (102) and
The Net of Grace (102)

Benediction
Aruṇā chalam-vāzhi aṉbarkaḷum vāzhi
Akshara Maṇa Mālai vāzhi

Substance: 
Hail Arunachala, the Lord of the song!. Hail the 

devotees who worship Him with love and devotion! Hail 
the Marital Garland of Letters, the guide to worship Him.



A Benediction on the Completion of the Gloss

The mystic experience of Supreme Love or Prema 
Bhakti that Bhagavan cherished for Arunachala, the Sat 
Chit Ananda (Being Awareness Bliss), Sarvātmaṉ (the Self 
of All), even after culminating in the attainment of oneness 
of identity with Him did not subside in the heart. The 
eternal verity voiced by Saint Valluvar as one that cannot 
be disproved at all times finds perfect agreement here in an 
adhyatmic sense. 

aṟidō ṟaṟiyāmai kaṇḍaṯṯṟāṟ kāmañ
ceṟidōṟuñ cēyizhai māṭṭu.
“Even as a man feeleth his ignorance more the more 

wise he growth”
“Even so do I love her the more ardently the more I 

enjoy her company”
Though ‘love’ is spoken of in the temporal plane here, 

it finds total resonance in the plane of esoteric and mystic 
experience as well.

The yearning for the ecstatic experience of the union, 
irrepressible in his heart, flourished further and further, 
again and again ever afresh and anew and surged forth and 
brimmed over.

In those earlier days when this Text was bestowed by 
Bhagavan, he appeared like a madman* in the perception of 
the ignorant people very much like the mahatma Marula Deva 
*	 Akshara Mana Malai, v. 71.



Arunachala Akshara Mana Malai 269

eulogized by none other than Prabhulinga Deva, an avatar 
or incarnation of the Lord. Bhagavan, having been released 
of the ghost of ego but possessed by the unexorcisable ghost 
of Brahman, was seized with the madness of jnana. Losing 
sway over himself due to the intoxication of Bhakti, he 
moved around like a “madman” and a “ghost”.

Like the hymns wherein Maṇivāchakar and 
Nammāzhwar, disconsolate, wail aloud, mainly as a means 
to lead and direct the devotees possessed of intense and deep 
love to His Feet of Grace, this Text of “Marital Garland of 
Letters” was bestowed by Bhagavan to help the people of 
the world at large.

May the precious gem of the preceptor – Sri Ramana 
Moorthy – ever flocked by the throng of sadhus athirst for 
the attainment of the Self, live forever.

May “The Marital Garland of Letters,” that links and 
unites the devotees with intense love with the bridegroom 
Arunachala, the ocean of Bliss, shining as the flare of the 
effulgence of Awareness in the divine wedlock of Grace and 
Transcendent Bliss, live forever and anon.

May this gloss, rendered with total devotion 
and sraddha – a fit medium of worship as ceaseless 
meditation – and composed and concluded by the expansive 
Grace of the Lord and Ruler, who abides and shines as 
Being of being and Awareness of awareness in the heart of 
Mukavaikanna Muruganar, the author who is but a mere 
speck of the dust of His Gracious Feet, along with the 
multitude of devotees who learn this with great zeal, live 
forever.



The Benefit of Praying with the Text of  
Marital Garland of Letters 

By Muruganar

Bhaktik kaḍaliṟ pathindu tiḷait-tavaruṭ
Siddhik kaḍalāṉa sīramaṇaṉ - ḏṟuyt-tapara
Māṉubhavam poṅga vaḷit-tamaṇa mālai-siva
Jṅāṉa-sukha nalha namakku
May this Text of Marital Garland of Letters, 
bestowed by Sri Ramana Bhagavan, the Ocean of 
Gracious attainment of siddhi, sunk deep and soaked 
in the ocean of Parabhakti, the form of jnana, by 
the upsurge of the Supreme Experience of the Self 
that he forever revelled in, grant us all the Bliss of 
jnana and the Joy of Awareness.
